practice on black economic empower-\nment issued in terms of section 9(1) of the Broad-Based Black Economic Empowerment Act, 2003 (Act\nNo. 53 of 2003).\nEmployer\nThe contracting party named in the Contract who employs the Service Provider.\nEngineer\nThe natural or juristic person, partnership, Incorporated Company, Proprietary Limited Company or\nClose Corporation appointed in writing by the Employer for the construction monitoring and management\nof the engineering Works undertaken by the Contractor.\nC1-16\n\nForce Majeure\n“For the purpose of this Contract the expression ‘Force Majeure’ shall mean an event or circumstance\ndescribed in clause 8.3.1.\nKey Persons\nPersons who are named as such in the Contract Data who will be engaged in the performance of the\nServices.\nOthers\nPersons or organizations who are not the Employer, the Service Provider or any employee, sub-\ncontractor, or supplier of the Service Provider.\nParties\nThe Employer and the Service Provider.\nPeople with Disabilities\nPeople with Disabilities has the meaning assigned to it in section 1 of the Employment Equity Act, 1998\n(Act No. 55 of 1998).\nPeriod of Performance\nThe period within which the Services are to be performed and completed.\nPersonnel\nPersons hired by the Service Provider as employees and assigned to the performance of the Services\nor any part thereof.\nPersonnel Schedule\nA schedule naming all personnel and key persons.\nPricing Data\nData that establishes the criteria and assumptions that were taken into account when developing the\nContract Price and the record of the components that make up of the Contract Price.\nProject\nThe project named in the Contract Data for which the Services are to be provided.\nPublic Official\na) Any official or employee of any government department and/or state-owned entity;\nb) Any person performing a public function;\nc) Any official or employee of a public international organisation including without limitation donor or\nfunding agencies or the Employer;\nd) Any candidate for political office; or\ne) Any political party or an official of a political party.\nQSE\nQSE is a qualifying small business enterprise in terms of a code of good practice on black economic\nempowerment issued in terms of section 9(1) of the Broad-Based Black Economic Empowerment Act,\n2003 (Act No. 53 of 2003).\nScope of Work\nThe document which defines the Employer’s objectives and requirements and specifies the Services\nwhich must, or may, be provided under the Contract.\nService Provider\nThe contracting party named in the Contract Data who is employed by the Employer to perform the\nServices described in the Contract, and legal successors to the Service Provider and legally permitted\nassignees.\nServices\nThe work to be performed by the Service Provider pursuant to the Contract as described in the Scope\nof Work.\nSub-contractor\nA person or body corporate who enters into a sub-contract with the Service Provider to perform part of\nthe Services.\nC1-17\n\nTargeted Enterprise\nA Consulting Engineering Firm who is an EME (BEP)* or QSE (BEP)*, contracted by the Service Pro-\nvider to perform a tendered percentage of work stated in the Contract Data under his guidance and\nwhich complies with the following:\na) Is at least 51% owned by black people and\nb) Has a B-BBEE status* of ‘level one or level two contributor’; and\nc) does not share equity holding with the Service Provider; and\nd) is registered in terms of the Company’s Act, 2008 (Act No. 71 of 2008) or Close Corporation Act,\n1984 (Act No. 69 of 1984); and\ne) is registered on the National Treasury’s Central Supplier Database (CSD).\n* NOTE: All references to EME, QSE, B-BBEE status are in terms of the amended Construction\nSector Codes published in Notice 931 of 2017 in Government Gazette No 41287 on 1\nDecember 2017.\nTechnical Proposal\nThe Key Persons proposed by the Service Provider to undertake the respective functions/duties as\ndefined under the Contract.\nWorks or Works Contract\nThat project or part of a project that the employer wishes to have delivered and for which the Service\nProvider has been appointed for the performance of the services specified in this contract.\nYouth\nFor purposes of this contract, Youth means persons between the ages of 18 (eighteen) and 35 (thirty-\nfive).\n1. INTERPRETATION\n2.1 Unless inconsistent with the context, an expression which denotes:\na) any gender includes the other genders;\nb) a natural person includes a juristic person and vice versa;\nc) the singular includes the plural and vice versa.\n2.2 The documents forming the Contract are to be taken as mutually explanatory of one another. For the\npurposes of interpretation, the priority of documents shall be in accordance with the following sequence:\na) the Form of Acceptance\nb) the Form of Offer\nc) the Contract Data\nd) the General Conditions of Contract\ne) the Scope of Work\nf) the Pricing Schedule and any other documents forming part of the Contract.\n2.3 The clause headings shall not limit, alter or affect the meaning of the Contract.\n1. GENERAL\n3.1 Governing laws\n“Law” means all national (or other spheres of Government) legislation, statutes, ordinances and other\nlaws including the South African Common Law, and regulations and by-laws of any legally constituted\npublic authority.\n3.2 Change in legislation\nIf after the commencement of the Contract, the cost or duration of the Services is altered as a result of\nchanges in, or additions to, any statute, regulation or bye-law, or the requirements of any authority\nhaving jurisdiction over any matter in respect of the Project, then the Contract Price and time for\ncompletion shall be adjusted in order to reflect the impact of those changes, provided that, within 14\ndays of first having become aware of the change, the Service Provider furnished the Employer with\ndetailed justification for the adjustment to the Contract Price.\nC1-18\n\n3.3 Language\n3.3.1 The language of the Contract and of all communications between the Parties shall be English.\n3.3.2 All reports, recommendations and reports prepared by the Service Provider under the Contract\nshall be in English.\n3.4 Notices\nAny notice, request, consent, or other communication made between the Parties pursuant to the Contract shall\nbe in writing and shall be deemed to have been made when delivered in person to an authorized representative\nof the Party to whom the communication is addressed, or when delivered to such Party at the address stated\nin the Contract Data, or one week after having been sent by registered post.\n3.4.2 If the sender requires evidence of receipt, he shall state such requirement in his communication\nand, wherever there is deadline for the receipt of the communication, he may demand evidence\nof receipt of his communication. In any event, the sender shall take all the necessary measures\nto ensure receipt of communications.\n3.4.3 A Party may change its address for receipt of communications by giving the other Party 30 days\nadvance notice of such change.\n3.5 Location\nThe Services shall be performed at such locations as are specified in the Contract Data, and where the\nlocation of a particular task is not so specified, at such locations as the Employer may approve.\n3.6 Publicity and publication\nUnless otherwise agreed, the Service Provider shall not release public or media statements or publish\nmaterial related to the Services or Project within two (2) years of completion of the Services without the\nwritten approval of the Employer, which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld.\n3.7 Confidentiality\nBoth Parties shall keep all commercially sensitive information obtained by them in the context of the\nContract confidential and shall not divulge it without the written approval of the other Party except to the\nextent strictly necessary to carry out obligations under this Contract or to comply with applicable laws.\n3.8 Variations\n3.8.1 The Employer may, without changing the objectives or fundamental scope of the Contract, order\nvariations to the Services or may request the Service Provider to submit proposals, including the\ntime and cost implications, for variations to the Services. No variation shall have any force or\neffect unless reduced to writing and signed by both Parties.\n3.8.2 The reasonable cost of preparation and submission of such proposals and the incorporation into\nthe Contract of any variations to the Services ordered by the Employer, including any change in\nthe Contract Price, shall be agreed in writing between the Service Provider and the Employer,\nprior to the change being implemented.\n3.8.3 Where a variation is necessitated by default or breach of Contract by the Service Provider, any\nadditional cost attributable to such variation shall be borne by the Service Provider.\n3.9 Sole agreement\nThe Contract constitutes the sole agreement between the Parties for the performance of the Services\nand no representation not contained therein shall be of any force or effect. No amendments will be of\nany force or effect unless reduced to writing and signed by both Parties.\n3.10 Indemnification\nThe Service Provider shall, at his own expense, indemnify, protect and defend the Employer, its agents\nand employees, from and against all actions, claims, losses and damage arising from any wilful or\nnegligent act or omission by the Service Provider or his subcontractors in the performance of the\nC1-19\n\nServices, including any violation of legal provisions, or rights of others, in respect of patents, trademarks\nand other forms of intellectual property such as copyrights.\n3.11 Penalty\n3.11.1 In the event that due to his negligence, or for reasons within his control, the Service Provider does\nnot perform the Services within the Period of Performance, the Employer shall without prejudice\nto his other remedies under the Contract, be entitled to levy a penalty for every day or part thereof,\nwhich shall elapse between the end of the period specified for performance, or an extended\nPeriod of Performance, and the actual date of completion, at the rate and up to the maximum\namount stated in the Contract Data.\n3.11.2 In the event that due to his negligence or for reasons within his control, the Service Provider does\nnot meet the specified target of work to be sublet stated in the Contract Data to the Targeted\nEnterprise the Employer shall be entitled to levy a penalty as stated in the Contract Data.\n3.11.3 In the event that due to his negligence or for reasons within his control, the Service Provider does\nnot disclose subcontracting arrangements the Employer may be entitled to levy a penalty as\nstated in the Contract Data.\n3.11.4 In the event that due to his negligence or for reasons within his control, the Service Provider does\nnot perform the service of capturing structures inventory and condition updates within the\nspecified period of time, the Employer shall be entitled to levy a penalty as stated in the Contract\nData.\n3.11.5 In the event that the draft documentation and/or design is of sub-standard/poor quality, the\nEmployer shall be entitled to levy a penalty for the additional time spent by the Employer to correct\nthe documentation and/or design to the required acceptable standard at the hourly tariffs as\nspecified under Additional Duties.\n3.11.6 If the Employer has become entitled to the maximum penalty amount referred to in 3.11.1, he may\nafter giving notice to the Service Provider:\na) terminate the Contract\nb) complete the Services at the Service Provider’s cost.\n3.12 Equipment and materials furnished by the Employer\n3.12.1 Equipment and materials made available to the Service Provider by the Employer, or purchased by\nthe Service Provider with funds provided by the Employer for the performance of the Services shall be\nthe property of the Employer and shall be marked accordingly. Upon termination or expiration of the\nContract, the Service Provider shall make available to the Employer an inventory of such equipment\nand materials and shall dispose of them in accordance with the Employer’s instructions.\n3.12.2 Unless otherwise stated in the Contract Data, the Service Provider shall, at his own expense, insure\nthe equipment and materials referred to in 3.12.1 for their full replacement value.\n3.13 Illegal and impossible requirements\nThe Service Provider shall notify the Employer immediately, should he become aware that the Contract\nrequires him to undertake anything which is illegal or impossible.\n3.14 Programme\n3.14.1 The Service Provider shall, within the time period set out in the Contract Data, submit to the\nEmployer a programme for the performance of the Services which shall, inter alia, include:\na) the order and timing of operations by the Service Provider and any actions required of the\nEmployer and Others;\nb) the dates by which the Service Provider plans to complete work needed to allow the\nEmployer and Others to undertake work required of them; and\nc) other information as required in terms of the Scope of Work or Contract Data.\nC1-20\n\n3.14.2 The Employer may, during the course of the Contract, request the Service Provider to amend the\nprogramme. Where this is not practicable, the Service Provider shall advise the Employer\naccordingly and advise him of alternative measures, if any, which might be taken.\n3.14.3 The Service Provider shall regularly update the programme to reflect actual progress to date and\nexpected future progress.\n3.15 Severability\nIf a court of competent jurisdiction holds that any provision of this Contract is severable by reason of it\nbeing invalid, illegal, unlawful or unenforceable and as a consequence of which prevents the accom-\nplishment of the purpose of this Contract, the Employer and the Service Provider shall meet and review\nthe matter to adopt means to fulfil the purpose of the Contract.\n3.16 Waiver\nNo defence of a waiver of any of the provisions of this contract shall be effective unless it is expressly\nstated in writing, by the Party against whom such defence is raised, to be a waiver of such provisions\nand is communicated to the other Party in writing in accordance with the provisions of Clause 3.4.\n1. EMPLOYER’S OBLIGATIONS\n4.1 Information\n4.1.1 The Employer shall timeously provide to the Service Provider, free of cost, all available infor-\nmation and data in the Employer’s possession which may be required for the performance of the\nServices.\n4.1.2 The Employer shall provide the Service Provider with any assistance required in obtaining other\nrelevant information that the latter may require in order to perform the Services.\n4.1.3 The Service Provider shall be entitled to rely on the accuracy and completeness of all information\nfurnished by or on behalf of the Employer.\n4.2 Decisions\nThe Employer shall, within a reasonable time, give his decision on any matter properly referred to him\nin writing by the Service Provider so as not to delay the performance of Services.\n4.3 Assistance\nThe Employer shall co-operate with the Service Provider and shall not interfere with or obstruct the\nproper performance of the Services. The Employer shall as soon as practicable:\n4.3.1 authorise the Service Provider to act as his agent insofar as may be necessary for the perfor-\nmance of the Services;\n4.3.2 provide all relevant data, information, reports, correspondence and the like, which become\navailable;\n4.3.3 procure the Service Provider’s ready access to premises, or sites, necessary for the performance\nof the Services;\n4.3.4 assist in the seeking of all approvals, licenses and permits from state, regional and municipal\nauthorities having jurisdiction over the Project, unless otherwise stated in the Contract Data;\n4.3.5 designate in writing a person to act with his complete authority in giving instructions and receiving\ncommunications on his behalf and interpreting and defining his policies and requirements in\nregard to the Services.\n4.4 Services of others\n4.4.1 The Employer shall, at his own cost, engage such others as may be necessary for the execution\nof work necessary for the completion of the Project, but not included in the Services.\n4.4.2 The Employer shall not enter into an agreement or contract with others which describes any of\nthe duties and responsibilities of the Service Provider in terms of the Contract or which imposes\nobligations on him, without first obtaining the Service Provider’s written agreement thereto.\nC1-21\n\n4.4.3 Where the provision of services by others in terms of Clause 4.4.1 is necessary for the proper\nperformance of the Services then the Employer shall ensure that such provision of services by\nothers is given in sufficient time so as to allow the Service Provider to proceed in accordance with\nthe programme. The Service Provider shall co-operate with the suppliers of such services but\nshall not be responsible for them or their performance.\n4.5 Notice of change by Employer\nOn becoming aware of any matter which will materially change, or has changed, the scope, cost or\ntiming of the Services, or on becoming aware of any defect or deficiency in the Services, the Employer\nshall immediately advise the Service Provider thereof.\n4.6 Issue of instructions\nWhere the Service Provider is required to administer the work or services of Others, or any contract or\nagreement, on behalf of the Employer, then the Employer shall issue instructions related to such work,\nservices, contract or agreement only through the Service Provider.\n4.7 Payment of Service Provider\nThe Employer shall pay the Service Provider the Contract Price in accordance with the provisions of the\nContract.\n1. SERVICE PROVIDER’S OBLIGATIONS\n5.1 General\n5.1.1 The Service Provider shall perform the Services in accordance with the Scope of Work with all\nreasonable care, diligence and skill in accordance with generally accepted professional tech-\nniques and standards and shall be responsible for breach of professional duty by reason of any\nerror, omission or neglect in connection with delivering the services.\n5.1.2 Where the Services include the exercise of powers to certify, decide or otherwise exercise dis-\ncretion in regard to a contract or agreement between the Employer and Others, then the Service\nProvider shall act in respect of that contract or agreement as an independent professional.\n5.1.3 If the Service Provider is a joint venture or consortium of two or more persons, the Service\nProvider shall designate one person to act as leader with authority to bind the joint venture or\nconsortium. Neither the composition nor the constitution of the joint venture or consortium shall\nbe altered without the prior consent in writing of the Employer, which shall not be unreasonably\nwithheld.\n5.1.4 Unless specifically instructed differently, the Service Provider is delegated as the Employer's\n"Mandatary" in terms of the Occupational Health and Safety Act (Act no. 85 of 1993), and as the\nEmployer's Agent as defined in Construction Regulations 5(5), (6) and (7) of the said Act.\n5.1.5 The Service Provider, if requested by the Employer, shall provide any plans and calculations for\nchecking by others.\n5.1.6 All design calculations shall be kept and safeguarded for the duration of the insurance(s) required\nin terms of Clause 5.4.1.\n5.1.7 The Service Provider shall enter into a contract over the full contract duration (either through\npartnership, joint venture or sub-contracting) with a Targeted Enterprise(s) to perform a per-\ncentage of work as stated in the Contract Data.\n5.1.8 The Service Provider undertakes to pay the Targeted Enterprise(s) the full value due in each\ninterim fee claim. The Service Provider further undertakes to make payment within 30 (thirty) days\nof presentation by the Service Provider of the interim fee claim to the Employer for payment, or\nby the 30th of the month following that in which the Targeted Enterprise(s) completed the work,\nwhichever date is earlier.\n5.2 Exercise of authority\nC1-22\n\n5.2.1 The Service Provider shall have no authority to relieve others appointed by the Employer to under-\ntake work on the Project of any of their duties, obligations, or responsibilities under their respective\nagreements or contracts, unless expressly authorised by the Employer in response to an\napplication by the Service Provider in writing to do so.\n5.2.2 Whenever necessary to enter upon land for the performance of the Contract the Service Provider\nshall do so in accordance with the provisions of the SANRAL Act, (Act 7 of 1998) and shall in-\ndemnify the Employer from all costs arising from any transgression committed by the Service\nProvider.\n5.3 Designated representative\nThe Service Provider shall designate in writing a person to act as his representative and such person\nshall have complete authority to receive instructions from and give information to the Employer on behalf\nof the Service Provider.\n5.4 Insurances to be taken out by the Service Provider\n5.4.1 The Service Provider shall, at his own cost, unless otherwise agreed, take out and maintain in\nforce such insurance policies in respect of their own risks in performing the Services as are\nstipulated in the Contract Data, subject to the approval of the Employer, which approved shall not\nbe unreasonably withheld.\n5.4.2 The Service Provider shall, at the Employer’s request, provide evidence to the Employer showing\nthat the insurance required in terms of Clause 5.4.1 has been taken out with an insurance\ncompany registered in the Republic of South Africa, or as otherwise approved by the Employer\nand maintained in force.\n5.5 Service Provider’s actions requiring Employer’s prior approval\nThe Service Provider shall obtain the Employer’s prior approval in writing before taking, inter alia, any\nof the following actions:\na) appointing Subcontractors for the performance of any part of the Services,\nb) appointing Key Persons not listed by name in the Contract Data.\nc) any other action that may be specified in the Contract Data.\n5.6 Co-operation with Others\nIf the Service Provider is required to perform the Services in co-operation with Others he may make\nrecommendations to the Employer in respect of the appointment of such Others. The Service Provider\nshall, however, only be responsible for his own performance and the performance of Subcontractors\nunless otherwise provided for.\n5.7 Notice of change by Service Provider\nOn becoming aware of any matter which will materially change or has changed the scope, cost or timing\nof the Services, the Service Provider shall give notice thereof to the Employer, save that the Service\nProvider is empowered to make minor changes or variations within the overall programme or budget\nand within such parameters as are defined by the Employer, provided that such changes are reported\ntimeously to the Employer.\n5.8 Safeguarding the Employer’s data/property\n5.8.1 Anything supplied by or paid by the Employer for the use of the Service Provider, shall be the\nproperty of the Employer and, where applicable.\n5.8.2 The Parties shall take reasonable precautions (each having regard to the nature of the other‘s\nrespective obligations under the Contract) to preserve the integrity of the Employer’s\ndata/property including appropriate back-up procedures unit completion of the services and/or\nreturn of such data/property to the Employer.\n5.8.3 In the event that the Employer’s data is corrupted or lost as a result of any default by the Service\nProvider, the Employer shall at the Service Provider’s expense, have the option to:\n- require the Service Provider to restore or procure the restoration of such data; or;\nC1-23\n\n- itself restore or procure restoration of such data.\n5.9 Performance Security\nWhere required, the Service Provider shall obtain (at his cost) a Performance Security for proper\nperformance in the amount and currencies stated in the Contract Data. If an amount is not stated in the\nContract Data, this sub-clause shall not apply.\nThe Service Provider shall deliver the Performance Security to the Employer within 14 days of the date\nof issue of the Letter of Acceptance. The Performance Security shall be issued by an insurance company\nor bank registered or licensed as an insurance company or bank to do business in the Republic of South\nAfrica and approved by the Employer and having an office or banking facility in the Republic of South\nAfrica. The Performance Security shall be subject to approval by the Employer and shall be in the form\nprescribed in the tender documents.\n1. CONFLICTS OF INTEREST, CORRUPTION AND FRAUD\n6.1 Service Provider not to benefit from commissions, discounts, etc.\nThe remuneration of the Service Provider under the Contract shall constitute the Service Provider’s sole\nremuneration in connection with the Contract, or the Services, and the Service Provider shall not accept\nfor his own benefit any trade commission, discount, or similar payment in connection with activities\npursuant to the Contract, or in the discharge of his obligations under the Contract, and shall use his best\nefforts to ensure that the Personnel, any sub-contractors, and agents of either of them shall, similarly,\nnot receive any additional remuneration.\n6.2 Royalties and the like\nThe Service Provider shall not have the benefit, whether directly or indirectly, of any royalty or of any\ngratuity or commission in respect of any patented or protected article or process used in or for the\npurposes of the Contract, or Project, unless so agreed by the Employer in writing.\n6.3 Independence\nThe Service Provider shall refrain from entering into any relationship which could be perceived as\ncompromising his independence of judgment, or that of Subcontractors or Personnel.\n6.4 Corruption and Fraud\n6.4.1 The Service Provider shall neither:\n(a) Offer or give or agree to give any person of the Employer any gift or consideration of any\nkind as an inducement or reward for doing or forbearing to do or for having done or forebore\nto do any act in relation to the obtaining or performance of this contract or any other contract\nwith the Employer or for showing or forbearing to show favour or disfavour to any person\nin relation to this contract; nor\n(b) Enter into this contract if in connection with its commission or a reward of any type has\nbeen paid, offered or agreed to be paid to any person of the Employer by the Service\nProvider or on his behalf or knowledge.\n(c) The Service Provider shall immediately give notice to the Employer with full particulars in\nthe event that the Service Provider receives a request from any public official requesting\nillicit payments.\n(d) The Service Provider shall at the Employer’s request demonstrate that it adheres to a\ndocumented code of conduct in respect to the prevention of corruption and bribery.\n(e) The Service Provider declares that as the date of signature of the Agreement there are no\ncircumstances or matters that may give rise to a conflict of interest in the performance of\nits obligations under the Agreement. The Service Provider shall inform the Employer\nimmediately if it becomes aware of any such circumstances or matters. If a conflict of\ninterest arises then the Parties shall agree, in good faith, on measures to manage such\nconflict.\nC1-24\n\n6.4.2 In the event that the Employer, in good faith and on reasonable grounds, comes to the opinion,\nthat a breach of any of the provisions of Clause 6.4.1 has been perpetrated by the Service\nProvider or anyone employed by him or acting on his behalf in relation to this contract or any other\nagreement with the Employer, the Employer may:\ni. summarily suspend the operation of this Contract by notice in writing to the Service\nProvider, informing him of the Employer’s opinion and the grounds and reasons upon which\nit is based, and calling upon the Service Provider to show cause, in writing, within fourteen\n(14) days of receipt of the written notice why the Employer should not terminate this\nContract on the grounds of the alleged breach(es) of Clause 6.4.1 of this Contract;\nii. withhold all payments due;\niii. terminate this Contract by notice in writing to the Service Provider, if the Service Provider\nfails to respond to the Employer’s written response, to satisfy the Employer that his opinion\nis unfounded. Provided always that such termination shall not prejudice or affect any right\nof action or remedy, which shall have accrued or shall accrue thereafter to the Employer\nand provided also that the Employer may recover from the Service Provider such sum as\nthe Employer deems equivalent to the amount or value of any such gift, consideration or\ncommission.\n6.4.3 In the event that the Employer, in good faith and on reasonable grounds, comes to the opinion\nthat any contract with a Government or public sector body has been or was obtained by the\nService Provider through actions that mutatis mutandis are similar in nature to those barred in\nterms of Clause 6.4.2 by the Service Provider or by anyone employed by the Service Provider or\nacting on the Service Provider’s behalf in relation to such Contract, the Employer may:\n(a) summarily suspend the operation of this Contract by notice in writing to the Service\nProvider, informing him of the Employer’s opinion and the grounds and reasons upon which\nit is based, and calling upon the Service Provider to show cause, in writing within fourteen\n(14) days of receipt of the written notice why the Employer should not terminate this\nContract on the grounds of the alleged breach(es) of Clause 6.4.1 of this contract;\n(b) withholds all payments due\n(c) terminate this Contract by notice in writing to the Service Provider, if the Service Provider\nfails to respond to the Employer’s written notice within the prescribed time, or fails, in his\nwritten response, to satisfy the Employer that this opinion is unfounded. Provided always\nthat such termination shall not prejudice or affect any right of action or remedy, which shall\nhave accrued or shall accrue thereafter to the Employer.\n1. SERVICE PROVIDER’S PERSONNEL\n7.1 Provision of Personnel\n7.1.1 The Service Provider shall provide appropriate Personnel for such time periods as required in\nterms of the Contract and shall enter all data pertaining to Personnel including titles, job\ndescriptions, qualifications and estimated periods of engagement on the performance of the\nServices in the Personnel Schedule.\n7.1.2 The Services shall be performed by the Personnel listed in the Personnel Schedule for the periods\nof time indicated therein. The Service Provider may, subject to the approval of the Employer,\nmake such adjustments to the data provided in terms of Clause 7.1.1 above as may be\nappropriate to ensure the efficient performance of the Services, provided that the adjustments will\nnot cause payments to exceed any limit placed on the Contract Price.\n7.1.3 The Service Provider shall:\na) forward to the Employer for approval, within 15 days of the award of the Contract, the\nPersonnel Schedule and a timetable for the placement of Personnel.\nb) inform the Employer of the date of commencement and departure of each member of\nPersonnel during the course of the Project.\nc) submit to the Employer for his approval a timely request for any proposed change to\nPersonnel, or timetables.\n7.2 Staff and equipment\n7.2.1 The Service Provider shall employ and provide all qualified and experienced personnel required\nto perform the Services.\nC1-25\n\n7.2.2 Where required in terms of the Contract, the Service Provider shall provide Key Persons as listed\nin the Contract Data to perform specific duties. If at any time, a particular Key Person cannot be\nmade available, the Service Provider may engage a replacement who is equally or better qualified\nto perform the stated duty, subject to the Employer’s approval, which approval shall not be\nunreasonably withheld.\n7.2.3 Where the Service Provider proposes to utilise a person not named in the Personnel Schedule,\nhe shall submit the name, relevant qualifications and experience of the proposed replacement\nperson to the Employer for approval.\n7.2.4 Where the fees for the Services are time-based, the fee payable for a person provided as a\nreplacement shall not exceed that which would have been payable to the person replaced.\n7.2.5 Except in the case of replacement resulting from death or where the Employer requests a\nreplacement not provided for by the Contract, the Service Provider shall bear all additional costs\narising out of or incidental to such replacement.\n7.2.6 The Service Provider shall take all measures necessary and shall provide all materials and\nequipment necessary to enable Personnel to perform their duties in an efficient manner.\n7.3 Working hours, overtime and leave\nWhere the fees for the Services are time-based, working hours, leave entitlement and holidays for\nPersonnel provided in terms of Clause 7 are to be as stated in the Contract Data, or, if not stated, to be\ndetermined by the Service Provider. The Employer will not be responsible for overtime payments to\nPersonnel unless so specifically provided for in the Pricing Schedule.\n1. COMMENCEMENT, COMPLETION, MODIFICATION, SUSPENSION AND TERMINATION OF THE\nCONTRACT\n8.1 Commencement\nThe effective date of the contract shall be the date of the Form of Acceptance. Contract shall come into\neffect on the date that it is signed by both Parties or such later date as may be stated in the Contract\nData. The Service Provider shall commence the performance of the Services within thirty (30) days after\nthe date that the Contract becomes effective, or such date as may be specified in the Contract Data.\n8.2 Completion\n8.2.1 Unless terminated in terms of the Contract, or otherwise specified in the Contract Data, the Con-\ntract shall be concluded when the Service Provider has completed all Deliverables in accordance\nwith the Scope of Work.\n8.2.2 The Service Provider, may request an extension to the Period of Performance if he is or will be\ndelayed in completing the Contract by any of the following causes:\na) variations to Services ordered by the Employer.\nb) failure of the Employer to fulfil his obligations under the Contract.\nc) any delay in the performance of the Services which is not due to the Service Provider’s\ndefault.\nd) Force Majeure.\n8.2.3 The Service Provider shall within 14 days of becoming aware that a delay may occur, notify the\nEmployer of his intention to make a request for the extension of the Period of Performance to\nwhich he considers himself entitled and shall within 30 days thereafter deliver to the Employer full\nand detailed particulars of the request, in order that it may be investigated at the time.\n8.2.4 The Employer shall, within 30 days of receipt of a detailed request, grant such extension to the\nPeriod of Performance as may be justified, either prospectively or retrospectively, or inform the\nService Provider that he is not entitled to an extension. Should the Service Provider find the\ndecision of the Employer to be unacceptable he shall, nevertheless, abide by such decision in the\nperformance of the services and the matter shall be dealt with as a dispute in terms of Clause 12.\n8.3 Force Majeure\n8.3.1 In this clause “Force Majeure” means an exceptional event or circumstance:\nC1-26\n\n(a) which is beyond party’s control,\n(b) which such a party could not reasonably have provided against before entering into the\nContract,\n(c) which, having arisen, such Party could not reasonably have avoided or overcome, and\n(d) which is not substantially attributable to the other Party\nForce Majeure may include, but is not limited to, exceptional events or circumstances of the kind\nlisted below;\n(i) natural catastrophes such as earthquake, hurricane, typhoon or volcanic activity\n(ii) war, hostilities (whether war to be declared or not), invasion, act of foreign enemies,\n(iii) rebellion, terrorism, revolution, insurrection, military or usurped power, or civil war,\n(iv) riot, commotion, disorder, strike or lockout by persons other than the Service Provider’s\nPersonnel or other employees of the Service Provider and Sub-contractors,\nAn event or circumstance which is attributable to a wilful act, neglect or failure to take reasonable\nprecautions by the affected party, his employee’s agents, sub-contractors or others shall, under\nno circumstances, be considered Force Majeure.\n8.3.2 The failure of a Party to fulfil any of its obligations under the Contract shall not be considered to\nbe a breach of, or default under, this Contract insofar as such inability arises from an event of\nForce Majeure, provided that the Party affected by such an event has taken all reasonable\nprecautions, due care and reasonable alternative measures in order to meet the terms and\nconditions of this Contract, and has notified the other Party within 10 days of its occurrence and\nwithin a reasonable time of its estimated duration and consequences. Failure to so notify shall\nterminate that Party’s right to release from his obligations.\n8.3.3 In the event that the performance of the Services has to be suspended on the grounds of Force\nMajeure, the time for completion shall be extended by the extent of the delay plus a reasonable\nperiod for the resumption of work or, if the speed of performing certain Services has to be reduced,\nthe time for their completion shall be extended as may be necessary in the circumstances.\n8.3.4 During the period of his inability to perform the Services as a result of an event of Force Majeure,\nthe Service Provider shall be entitled to continue to be paid under the terms of the Contract and\nshall be reimbursed for additional costs reasonably and necessarily incurred by him in\nsuspending, delaying and reactivating the performance of the Services.\n8.3.5 If the Force Majeure event continues for more than 90 (ninety) days, either Party shall have the\nright to terminate this Contract with immediate effect.\n8.4 Termination\n8.4.1 The Employer may in addition to his rights under sub-clauses 6.4.2, 6.4.3 and 10.5 terminate the\nContract by giving not less than thirty (30) days written notice thereof to the Service Provider after\nthe occurrence of any of the following events:\n(a) if the Service Provider does not remedy a failure in the performance of his obligations under\nthe Contract within thirty (30) days after having been notified thereof, or within any further\nperiod as the Employer may have subsequently approved in writing;\n(b) if the Service Provider becomes insolvent or bankrupt;\n(c) if, as the result of Force Majeure, the Service Provider is unable to perform a material\nportion of the Services for a period of not less than sixty (60) days;\n(d) Committing an offence in terms of clauses 6.1 and/or 6.4;\n(e) if the Service Provider acts in such a way, under this contract or any other contract with the\nEmployer, that a statute relating to the combating of fraud, corruption, uncompetitive\npractice and the like can be invoked; or\n(f) if the Employer, at any time in its sole discretion determines that it no longer requires the\ncompletion of the Service by the Service Provider.\n(g) in the event that the directors of the Service Provider are found guilty by a Court of Law of\nfraud charges against them, SANRAL reserves the right to terminate the contract and to\nassert all legal recourse available to it.\nUpon delivery of such notice by the Service Provider he shall immediately vacate the site and\ndeliver to the Employer all drawings, documents and papers relating to the Services and shall\nwithin fourteen (14) days after the date of termination submit an account for the Services\nsatisfactorily performed prior to the date of notice. The Employer shall not be liable to the Service\nC1-27\n\nProvider for any loss of profit or damages suffered as a result of cancellation of the contract as\ncontemplated in this clause.\n8.4.2 The Service Provider may terminate the Contract, by giving not less than thirty (30) days written\nnotice to the Employer after the occurrence of any of the following events:\n(a) if the Employer fails to pay any monies due to the Service Provider in terms of the Contract\nand not subject to dispute pursuant to Clause 12 within forty-five (45) days after receiving\nwritten notice from the Service Provider that such payment is overdue; or\n(b) if, as the result of Force Majeure, the Service Provider is unable to perform a material\nportion of the Services for a period of not less than sixty (60) days; or\n(c) when the Services have been suspended under Clause 8.5 and the period of suspension\nexceeds 6 months, or it is clear to the Service Provider that it will be impossible or\nimpractical to resume the suspended Services before the period of suspension has\nexceeded 6 months; or\n(d) if the Employer is in material breach of a term of the Contract and fails to rectify such breach\nwithin 30 days of the receipt of written notice requiring him to do so.\n8.4.3 Upon termination of this Contract pursuant to Clauses 8.4.1 or 8.4.2, the Employer shall make\nthe following payments to the Service Provider:\n(a) remuneration in terms of the Contract for Services satisfactorily performed prior to the\neffective date of termination less any costs arising from termination.\n(b) except in the case of termination pursuant to events (a) and (b) of Clause 8.4.1, reim-\nbursement of any reasonable cost incident to the prompt and orderly termination of the\nContract.\n8.4.4 Should the Service Provider, being an individual or the last surviving principal of a partnership or\nbody corporate, die or be prevented by illness or any other circumstances beyond his control from\nperforming the obligations implied by the Contract, the Contract shall be terminated without\nprejudice to the accrued rights of either Party against the other.\n8.4.5 Where required in terms of the contract data the surety delivered by the Service Provider in terms\nof this Contract shall remain in force until the completion of the project.\n8.5 Suspension\n8.5.1 The Employer may temporarily suspend all or part of the Services by notice to the Service\nProvider who shall immediately make arrangements to stop the performance of the Services and\nminimise further expenditure.\n8.5.2 When Services are suspended, the Service Provider shall be entitled to pro-rata payment for the\nServices carried out and reimbursement of all reasonable cost incident to the prompt and orderly\nsuspension of the Contract.\n8.6 Rights and liabilities of the Parties\nCompletion, suspension or termination of the Contract shall not prejudice or affect the accrued rights or\nliabilities of the Parties.\n1. OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND COPYRIGHT\n9.1 Copyright of all documents prepared by the Service Provider in accordance with the relevant provisions\nof the copyright Act (Act 98 of 1978) relating to Project shall be vested in the party named in the Contract\nData. Where copyright is vested in the Service Provider, the Employer shall be entitled to use the\ndocuments or copy them only for the purposes for which they are intended in regard to the Project and\nneed not obtain the Service Provider’s permission to copy for such use. Where copyright is vested in\nthe Employer, the Service Provider shall not be liable in any way for the use of any of the information\nother than as originally intended for the Project and the Employer hereby indemnifies the Service\nProvider against any claim which may be made against him by any party arising from the use of such\ndocumentation for other purposes.\n9.2 The ownership of data and factual information collected by the Service Provider and paid for by the\nEmployer shall, after payment by the Employer, lie with the Employer. This shall include intellectual\nproperty gained during the project and any research work, papers and presentations done using the\nEmployer’s resources and information.\nC1-28\n\n9.3 The Employer shall have no right to use any documents prepared by the Service Provider whilst the\npayment of any fees and expenses due to the Service Provider in terms of the Contract is overdue.\n1. SUCCESSION AND ASSIGNMENT\n10.1 Except as defined in Clause 8.4.4 above, each Party binds itself and its partners, successors, executors,\nadministrators, assigns and legal representatives to the other Party and to the other partners,\nsuccessors, executors, administrators, assigns and legal representatives of the other Party in respect\nof all obligations and liabilities of the Contract.\n10.2 An assignment including that of a sub-contractor, shall be valid only if it is a written agreement, by which\nthe Parties transfer their rights and obligations under the Contract, or part thereof, to others.\n10.3 The Service Provider shall not, without the prior written consent of the Employer, assign the Contract or\nany part thereof, or any benefit or interest there under, except in the following cases:\n(a) by a charge in favour of the Service Provider’s bankers of any monies due or to become due\nunder the Contract; or\n(b) by assignment to the Service Provider’s insurers of the Service Provider’s right to obtain relief\nagainst any other person liable in cases where the insurers have discharged the Service\nProvider’s loss or liability.\n10.4 The approval of an assignment by the Employer shall not relieve the Service Provider of his obligations\nfor the part of the Contract already performed or the part not assigned.\n10.5 If the Service Provider has assigned his Contract or part thereof without authorization, the Employer\nmay forthwith terminate the Contract.\n1. SUB-CONTRACTING\n11.1 A sub-contract shall be valid only if it is a written agreement by which the Service Provider entrusts\nperformance of a part of the Services to others.\n11.2 The Service Provider shall not sub-contract to nor engage a Sub-contractor to perform any part of the\nServices without the prior written authorization of the Employer. The services to be sub-contracted and\nthe identity of the Sub-contractor shall be notified to the Employer. The Employer shall, within 14 days\nof receipt of the notification and a full motivation why such services are to be sub-contracted, notify the\nService Provider of his decision, stating reasons, should he withhold such authorization, failing which\nthe Sub-contractor shall be deemed to be approved by the Employer. If the Service Provider enters into\na sub-contract with a Sub-contractor without prior approval, the Employer may forthwith terminate the\nContract.\n11.3 The Employer shall have no contractual relationships with Sub-contractors. However, if a Sub-contractor\nis found by the Employer to be incompetent, the Employer may request the Service Provider either to\nprovide a Sub-contractor with qualifications and experience acceptable to the Employer as a\nreplacement, or to resume the performance of the relevant part of the Services himself.\n11.4 The Service Provider shall advise the Employer without delay of the variation or termination of any sub-\ncontract for performance of all or part of the Services.\n11.5 The Service Provider shall be responsible for the acts, defaults and negligence of Sub-contractors and\ntheir agents or employees in the performance of the Services, as if they were the acts, defaults or\nnegligence of the Service Provider, his agents or employees. Approval by the Employer of the sub-\ncontracting of any part of the Contract or of the engagement by the Service Provider of Sub-contractors\nto perform any part of the Services shall not relieve the Service Provider of any of his obligations under\nthe Contract.\n11.6 The Service Provider undertakes to pay the sub-contractor the full value as certified as being due in\neach interim monthly account. The Service Provider further undertakes to make payment within 7\n(seven) days after payment by the Employer or by the 25th of the month following that in which the sub-\ncontractor invoiced for the work, whichever date is earlier.\n11.7 The Employer may request the Service Provider to supply reasonable evidence that the subcontractor\nhas received all amounts due in accordance with previous accounts, less deductions or otherwise.\nUnless the Service Provider:\nC1-29\n\n(i) submits this reasonable evidence to the Employer, or\n(ii) satisfies the Employer in writing that the Service Provider is reasonably entitled to withheld or refuse to\npay these amounts, and\n(iii) submits to the Employer reasonable evidence that the subcontractor has been notified of the Service\nProvider's entitlement,\nthen the Employer may (at his sole discretion) pay, direct to the subcontractor, part or all of such amounts\npreviously certified (less applicable deductions) as are due to the Subcontractor for which the Service Provider\nhas failed to submit evidence described in sub-clauses (i) to (iii) above. The Service Provider shall then repay\nthe Employer, the amount which the subcontractor was directly paid by the Employer.\n11.8 It is a condition of this contract that the Service Provider sub-contracts a minimum of 30% to Targeted\nEnterprise/s.\n1. RESOLUTION OF DISPUTES\n12.1 Settlement\n12.1.1 Any dispute between the Parties shall arise by either party presenting to the other in writing the\nnature of the dispute and the facts to be dealt with. The Parties shall negotiate in good faith with\na view to settling any dispute or claim arising out of or relating to the Contract and may not initiate\nany further proceedings until either Party has, by written notice to the other, declared that such\nnegotiations have failed.\n12.1.2 Any dispute or claim arising out of or relating to the Contract which cannot be settled between the\nParties shall in the first instance be referred by the Parties to either mediation or adjudication as\nprovided for in the Contract Data.\n12.2 Mediation\n12.2.1 If the Contract Data does not provide for dispute resolution by adjudication, not earlier than ten\nworking days after having advised the other Party, in terms of Clause 12.1, that negotiations in\nregard to a dispute have failed, an aggrieved Party may require that the dispute be referred,\nwithout legal representation, to mediation by a single mediator. The mediator shall be selected by\nagreement between the Parties, or, failing such agreement, by the person named for this purpose\nin the Contract Data. The costs of the mediation shall be borne equally by the Parties.\n12.2.2 The mediator shall convene a hearing of the Parties and may hold separate discussions with any\nParty and shall assist the Parties in reaching a mutually acceptable settlement of their differences\nthrough means of reconciliation, interpretation, clarification, suggestion and advice. The Parties\nshall record such agreement in writing and thereafter they shall be bound by such agreement.\n12.2.3 The mediator is authorised to end the mediation process whenever in his opinion further efforts\nat mediation would not contribute to a resolution of the dispute between the Parties and shall do\nso, if after a period of 90 (ninety) days from entering into the mediation process, no resolution has\nbeen achieved.\n12.2.4 If either Party is dissatisfied with the opinion expressed by the mediator or should the mediation\nfail, then such Party may require that the dispute be referred to arbitration or litigation in a\ncompetent civil court, as provided for in the Contract Data; provided that the dissatisfied Party\nnotifies the other in writing within 28 Days of mediator’s decision being issued or the mediator\ndeclaring the mediation to have ended, as provided for in the Contract Data. Claims not brought\nwithin the time periods set out herein will be deemed to be waived.\n12.3 Adjudication\n12.3.1 If the Contract Data does not provide for dispute resolution by mediation, an aggrieved Party may\nrefer the dispute to adjudication. Adjudication shall be in accordance with the latest edition of the\nseparately published CIDB Adjudication Procedures.\nC1-30\n\n12.3.2 The adjudicator shall be appointed in terms of the Adjudicator’s Agreement bound in the\nConstruction Industry Development Board’s Adjudication Procedure.\n12.3.3 The Adjudicator shall be any person agreed to by the parties or, failing such agreement, shall be\nnominated by the person named in the Contract Data. The Adjudicator shall be appointed in\naccordance with the Adjudicator’s Agreement contained in the CIDB Adjudication Procedure.\n12.3.4 If a Party is dissatisfied with the decision of the Adjudicator, the Party may give the other Party\nwritten notice of dissatisfaction within 28 days of the receipt of that decision and refer the dispute\nto arbitration or litigation in a competent civil court as provided for in the Contract Data. If notice\nof dissatisfaction is not given within the specified time, the decision shall be final and binding on\nthe Parties.\n12.4 Arbitration\n12.4.1 Arbitration, where provided for in the Contract Data, shall be by a single arbitrator in accordance\nwith the provisions of the Arbitration Act of 1965 as amended and shall be conducted in\naccordance with such procedure as may be agreed between the Parties or, failing such\nagreement, in accordance with the Rules for the Conduct of Arbitrations, current at the date of\nthe contract, published by the Association or Arbitrators.\n12.4.2 The arbitrator shall be mutually agreed upon or, failing agreement, to be nominated by the person\nnamed in the Contract Data.\n1. LIABILITY\n13.1 Liability of the Service Provider\n13.1.1 The Service Provider shall be liable to the Employer arising out of or in connection with the\nContract if a breach of Clause 5.1 is established against him.\n13.1.2 The Service Provider shall correct a Defect on becoming aware of it. If the Service Provider does\nnot correct a Defect within a reasonable time and the Defect arose from a failure of the Service\nProvider to comply with his obligation to provide the Services, the Service Provider shall pay to\nthe Employer the amount which the latter assesses as being the cost of having such Defect\ncorrected by Others.\n13.2 Liability of the Employer\n13.2.1 The Employer shall be liable to the Service Provider arising out of or in connection with the Contract\nif a breach of an obligation of his in terms of the Contract is established. The Service Provider shall\nhave no separate delictual right of action against the Employer.\n13.2.2 In the event where the design is found to be incomplete or inconclusive and results in delay\ndamages and/or additional expenditure during the works contract period, the Employer shall be\nentitled to claim against the Services Provider’s Professional Indemnity.\n13.3 Compensation\nIf it is established that either Party is liable to the other, compensation shall be payable only on the\nfollowing terms:\n(a) Compensation shall be limited to the amount of reasonably foreseeable loss and damage suffered\nas a result of the breach.\n(b) The compensation payable by either Party shall be reduced by the arbitrator/mediator/adjudicator\nto such extent as is deemed just and equitable having regard to the degree in which the other\nParty or any third party was at fault in relation to the loss or damage. The liability of the Parties is\nnot joint and each Party shall only be liable for that proportion of the compensation which is\nattributable to his fault.\n(c) In any event, the amount of compensation will be limited to the amount specified in Clause 13.5.\nC1-31\n\n13.4 Duration of Liability\nNotwithstanding the terms of the Prescription Act No. 68 of 1969 (as amended) or any other applicable\nstatute of limitation neither the Employer nor the Service Provider shall be held liable for any loss or\ndamage resulting from any occurrence unless a claim is formally made within the period stated in the\nContract Data or, where no such period is stated, within a period of three years from the date of\ntermination or completion of the Contract.\n13.5 Limit of Compensation\n13.5.1 The maximum amount of compensation payable by either Party to the other in respect of liability\nunder the Contract is limited to:\na) the sum insured in terms of 5.4. in respect of insurable event; or\nb) the sum stated in the Contract Data or, where no such amount is stated, to an amount\nequal to twice the Contract Price (inclusive of VAT) payable to the Service Provider under\nthe Contract.\n13.5.2 Each Party agrees to waive all claims against the other insofar as the aggregate of compensation\nwhich might otherwise be payable exceeds the aforesaid maximum amount payable.\n13.5.3 If either Party makes a claim for compensation against the other Party and this is not established,\nthe claimant shall reimburse the other for his reasonable costs incurred as a result of the claim or\nif proceedings are initiated in terms of Clause 12 for such costs as may be awarded.\n13.6 Indemnity by the Employer\nThe Employer shall indemnify the Service Provider against all claims by third parties which arise out of\nor in connection with the performance of the Services save to the extent that such claims do not in the\naggregate exceed the limit of compensation in Clause 13.5 or are covered by the insurances arranged\nunder the terms of Clause 5.4.\n13.7 Exceptions\n13.7.1 Clauses 13.5 and 13.6 shall not apply to claims arising from deliberate misconduct.\n13.7.2 The Service Provider shall have no liability whatsoever for actions, claims, losses or damages\noccasioned by:\na) the Employer omitting to act on any recommendation, or overriding any act, decision or\nrecommendation, of the Service Provider, or requiring the Service Provider to implement a\ndecision or recommendation with which the Service Provider disagrees or on which he\nexpresses a serious reservation; or\nb) the improper execution of the Service Provider’s instructions by agents, employees or\nindependent contractors of the Employer.\n1. REMUNERATION AND REIMBURSEMENT OF SERVICE PROVIDER\nThe Employer shall remunerate and reimburse the Service Provider for the performance of the Services\nas set out in the Pricing Data and Scope of Works. If not otherwise stated in the Pricing Data, the\nfollowing shall apply:\n14.1 The Service Provider shall be entitled to render interim monthly accounts for fees and reimbursements\nthroughout the duration of the Contract. Interim amounts of lump sum fees due (or any specified fixed-\nprogressed payments due that have been delayed by the acts of the Employer) shall be based on\nprogress.\n14.2 Amounts due to the Service Provider shall be paid by the Employer within thirty (30) days of receipt by\nhim of the relevant invoices. If the Service Provider does not receive payment by the due date, he shall\nbe entitled to charge interest on the unpaid amount, which is payable by the Employer on receipt by him\nof the relevant invoice for interest, at the Prime overdraft rate plus 2% per annum, compounded monthly\nand calculated from the due date of payment.\n14.3 If any item or part of an item in an invoice submitted by the Service Provider is disputed by the Employer,\nthe latter shall, before the due date of payment, give notice thereof with reasons to the Service Provider,\nbut shall not delay payment of the balance of the invoice. Clause 14.2 shall apply to disputed amounts\nwhich are finally determined to be payable to the Service Provider.\nC1-32\n\n14.4 In respect of Services charged for on a time basis and all other reimbursable expenses the Service\nProvider shall maintain records in support of such charges and expenses for a period of six months after\nthe completion or termination of the Contract. Within this period the Employer may, on not less than 14\ndays’ notice, require that a reputable and independent firm of accountants, nominated by him at his\nexpense, audit any claims made by the Service Provider for time charges and expenses by attending\nduring normal working hours at the office where the records are maintained.\n14.5 All accounts presented for payment shall be according to the Employers prescribed format with proof of\nITIS data capture and shall be submitted to the Employer electronically and when required, delivered in\nhard copy to the Employer’s relevant regional office.\n14.6 Payments due shall be subject to the deduction of retention monies as stated in the Contract Data. The\nretention monies so deducted shall be released in accordance with the phases and conditions stated in\nthe Contract Data.\n14.7 The Employer shall be entitled to make direct payment to a subcontractor as stated in Clause 11.7 and\ndeduct the amount from the Service Provider’s interim account.\n14.8 The Service Provider unconditionally authorises SANRAL to set off any debt agreed to which is due and\npayable by the Service Provider on this or any other SANRAL contract.\n14.9 If applicable, Contract Price Adjustment will be paid according to the method stated in the Contract Insurance against Professional indemnity\nCover is: R 7.2million without a limit on the number of claims (The limit of liability is only provided\nto cover the Employer’s damages and does not allow for the Service Provider’s costs for\ndefending any claim.)\nPeriod of cover: Structures – 25 years: all other instances – 10 years\n1. Insurance against general public liability\nCover is: R 7.2 million Period of cover: Structures – 25 years: all other instances – 10 years\n1. Third Party Liability\nCover is: R 7.2 million\nPeriod of cover: Duration of contract only.\n5.5 The Service Provider is required to obtain the Employer’s prior approval in writing before taking\nany of the following actions:\n1. Appointing Key Personnel not listed by name in the Contract Data.\n2. Appointing sub-contractors for the performance of any part of the Service.\n3. Expending any Prime Cost and/or Provisional Sum in the Contract or Works Contract.\n4. Authorising any work under a Variation (Works Authorisation) for the Works Contract.\n5. Authorising any work outside the contract limits.\n6. Over-expenditure on the Works Contract.\n7. Changing the scope of work for the Works Contract.\n8. C2-8\nC2-2\n\nC2.1 PRICING INSTRUCTIONS\nC2.1.1 For the purposes of this Pricing Schedule, the following words shall have the meanings hereby\nassigned to them.\nUnit: The unit of measurement for each item of work as defined in the Scope of Works.\nQuantity: The number of units of work for each item as provided by the Employer or as\ntendered by the Service Provider.\nRate: The payment per unit of work for which a rate has been provided by the Employer\nor for which the Service Provider tenders to do the work.\nAmount: The product of the quantity and the rate tendered for an item.\nLump Sum: An amount tendered for an item, the extent of which is described in the Pricing\nSchedule, the Scope of Work or elsewhere, but of which the quantity of work is\nnot measured in units.\nProvisional Sum: An amount allowed for in the Pricing Schedule, for which the quantity of work is\nnot known.\nPrime Cost: Is a specific type of Provisional Sum where payment is made on the production\nof invoices showing the cost price of the implementation or installation of the\nservice required. Services rendered in this manner carry a cost for which a rate\nor a lump sum is offered at tender stage to cover all the tenderer’s handling,\nsupervision and liability costs in providing the item or services.\nC2.1.2 The quantities provided by the Employer in the Pricing Schedule are only approximate quantities.\nThe quantities of work finally accepted and certified for payment, and not the quantities given in the\nPricing Schedule, will be used to determine payments to the Service Provider.\nThe validity of the Contract shall in no way be affected by differences between the quantities in the\nPricing Schedule and the quantities finally certified for payment. Work is valued at the rates or lump\nsums tendered.\nC2.1.3 Rates and lump sums shall include full compensation for overheads, profits, incidentals, tax (other\nthan VAT), etc., and for the completed items of work as specified in the Scope of Works and Contract\nData and for all the risks, obligations and responsibilities specified in the General Conditions of\nContract, Particular or Special Conditions of Contract, except in so far as the quantities given in the\nPricing Schedule are only approximate.\nC2.1.4 The tenderer shall fill in a quantity or a rate or a lump sum for each item where provision is made.\nItems against which no rate or lump sum has been entered in the tender will not be paid for when\nthe work is executed, as payment for such work will be regarded as being covered by other rates or\nlump sums in the pricing schedule.\nThe tenderer shall fill in a rate against all items where the words “rate only” appears in the amount\ncolumn. Although no work is foreseen under such item and no quantities are consequently given in\nthe quantity column, the tendered rate shall apply should work under this item actually be required.\nTenders should note the provisions of clause C2.1.10 of this preamble.\nThe tendered lump sums and rates shall be valid irrespective of any change in the quantities during\nthe execution of the contract.\nC2.1.5 The short descriptions of the payment items in the Pricing Schedule are only given to identify the\nitems and to provide specific details. Reference shall, inter alia, be made to the Contract Data,\nGeneral Conditions of Contract and Particular/Special Conditions of Contract and Scope of Works\nfor more detailed information regarding the extent of work entailed under each item.\nC2.1.6 Where so indicated under measurement and payment, prices or rates will be subject to adjustment\nfor escalation as provided for below:\n• The prices or rates shall be fixed for the first 12-month period determined from the tender base\ndate and no change during this period will be allowed for escalation.\nC2-3\n\n• On the 12-month anniversary date of the Contract base date the rates or sums shall be\nadjusted by the 12-month year on year CPI index (as published in the monthly bulletin PO141.\nof Statistics South Africa under table B) and fixed at this value for the following 12-month\nperiod. Subsequent 12-month periods shall be dealt with on the same basis.\n• Adjustment of lump sum prices for escalation shall only be applicable to that portion of the\nrelevant Service which is incomplete at the end of the 12-month anniversary date and shall\nnot be applicable to any progress payments already claimed.\nAdjustment for escalation shall only be applicable for services or portions thereof, that are still within\nthe prescribed programme and any approved extensions of time.\nC2.1.7 Interim payments for lump sum payment items may be permitted. Such interim payments shall\nhowever be limited to proven progress achieved for that particular service deliverable. The sum of\nany progress payments made under a particular lump sum payment item shall be deducted prior to\ncalculating any adjustments for escalation as described in clause C2.1.6 above.\nC2.1.8 Provisional and Prime Cost Sums: Each Sum shall only be used, in whole or in part, in accordance\nwith the Employer’s instructions and the Contract Price shall be adjusted accordingly. The total sum\npaid to the Service Provider shall include only such amounts, for the work, supplies or services to\nwhich the Sum relates, as the Employer shall have instructed.\nFor each Sum, the Employer may instruct plant, materials or services to be procured by the Service\nProvider in accordance with the Employer’s policies, and for which there shall be included in the\nContract Price:\n- The actual amounts paid (or due to be paid) by the Service Provider under the Sum, and\n- An item for compilation and printing of procurement documentation, quotation/tender process\nand evaluation, and all overhead charges and profit, tendered in the Pricing Schedule.\nProvided that for Prime Cost Sums only, where a percentage mark-up or lump sum mark-up\nis tendered, which shall exclude profit.\nThe Service Provider shall produce all quotations, invoices, vouchers and accounts or receipts in\nsubstantiation of any claim under a Sum.\nAny percentage adjustment or lump sum mark-up against the Sum for handling fee, profits, etc. shall\nnot be negative.\nC2.1.9. Subject to the conditions stated in Clause C2.1.10 below, the rates and lump sums filled in by the\ntenderer in the pricing schedule shall be final and binding with regard to submitting the tender and\nmay not be adjusted should there be any mistakes in the extensions thereof and in the total sums\nappearing in the tender. Should there be any discrepancies between the tender sum and the correctly\nextended and totalled pricing schedule, the rates and the lump sums will be regarded as being\ncorrect, and the Employer shall have the right to make adjustments to the tender sum to reconcile\nthe tender sum with the total of the pricing schedule. In such an event the tenderer will be consulted\nbut, failing agreement between the parties, the decision of the Employer shall be final and binding.\nAdjustment of the tender sum will take place prior to the signing of the contract. In their own interest\ntenderers must make doubly sure of the correctness of their tendered rates and lump sums, the\nextensions and the tender sum.\nC2.1.10 A tender shall be deemed non-responsive if the unit rates or lump sums for some of the items in the\nPricing Schedule are, in the opinion of the Employer, unreasonable or out of proportion, and if the\ntenderer fails, within a period of seven (7) days of having been notified in writing by the Employer to\nadjust the unit rates or lump sums for such items, to make such adjustments.\nC2.1.11 All rates and sums of money quoted in the Pricing Schedule shall be in South African Rand and\nwhole cents\nC2.1.12 The item numbers appearing in the Pricing Schedule refer to the corresponding item numbers in the\nScope of Work.\nC2-4\n\nC2.2 The tenderer will score 0 (zero) points if:\nThe B-BBEE certificate is not submitted or submitted B-BBEE certificate that has expired\nor is not valid; or\nThe tenderer submits a B-BBEE certificated that does not comply with requirements (e.g.\nnot SANAS); or\nThe tenderer submits the Scorecard assessment report only; or\nIn a case of a Joint Venture, the tenderer submits an unincorporated consolidated Joint\nVenture B-BBEE certificate which is not project specific; or\nC2-8\n\nIn a case of a Joint Venture, the tenderer submits an unincorporated consolidated Joint\nVenture B-BBEE certificate does not have a contract description and/or a tender number;\nor\nA tenderer only submits 1 (one) B-BBEE certificate, where multiple tenders were issued by\nSANRAL; or\nAn EME (not start-up) submits a Sworn Affidavit with total revenue above R1.8 million\ninstead of a B-BBEE certificate; or\nA QSE submits a Sworn Affidavit instead of a B-BBEE certificate.\nThe Sworn Affidavit is not valid. A valid Sworn Affidavit must contain the following:\ni. Name/s of deponent as they appear in the identity document and the identity number.\nii. Designation of the deponent as either the director, owner or member must be\nindicated in order to know that person is duly authorised to depose of an affidavit.\niii. Name of enterprise as per enterprise registration documents issued by the CIPC,\nwhere applicable, and enterprise business address.\niv. Percentage black ownership, black female ownership and whether they fall within a\ndesignated group.\nv. Indicate total revenue for the year under review and whether it is based on audited\nfinancial statements or management accounts.\nvi. Financial year-end (day, month and year) as per the enterprise’s registration\ndocuments, which was used to determine the total revenue.\nvii. B-BBEE status level. An enterprise can only have 1 (one) status level.\nviii. Date deponent signed and date of Commissioner of Oath must be the same.\nix. Commissioner of Oath cannot be an employee or ex-officio of the enterprise because,\na person cannot by law, commission a sworn affidavit in which they have an interest.\nA valid BBBEE Certificates shall contain:\n- Name of enterprise as per enterprise registration documents issued by CIPC, and\nenterprise business address.\n- Value-Added Tax number, where applicable.\n- The B-BBEE Scorecard against which the certificate is issued, indicating all\nelements and scores achieved for each element. The actual score achieved must\nbe linked to the total points as per the relevant Codes.\n- B-BBEE status with corresponding procurement recognition level.\n- The relevant Codes used to issue the B-BBEE verification certificate.\n- Date of issue and expiry (e.g. 9 June 2018 to 8 June 2019). Where a measured entity\nwas subjected to a re-verification process, due to material change, the B-BBEE\nVerification Certificate must reflect the initial date of issue, date of re-issue and the\ninitial date of expiry. Re-verification does not extend the lifespan of the B-BBEE\nVerification Certificate.\n- Financial period which was used to issue the B-BBEE Verification Certificate.\nC2-9\n\nPREFERENCE POINTS CLAIM FORM IN TERMS OF THE PREFERENTIAL PROCUREMENT\nREGULATIONS 2022 (SBD 6.1)\nThis preference form must form part of all tenders invited. GENERAL CONDITIONS\n1.1. The following preference point systems are applicable to invitations to tender:\nThe 80/20 system for requirements with a Rand value of up to R50 000 000 (all applicable taxes\nincluded); and\nThe 90/10 system for requirements with a Rand value above R50 000 000 (all applicable taxes\nincluded).\n1.2. To be completed by the organ of state:\nEither the 90/10 or 80/20 preference point system will be applicable in this tender. DEFINITIONS\na) “tender” means a written offer in the form determined by an organ of state in response to an\ninvitation to provide goods or services through price quotations, competitive tendering process or\nany other method envisaged in legislation;\nb) “price” means an amount of money tendered for goods or services, and includes all applicable\ntaxes less all unconditional discounts;\nc) “rand value” means the total estimated value of a contract in Rand, calculated at the time of bid\ninvitation, and includes all applicable taxes;\nd) “tender for income-generating contracts” means a written offer in the form determined by an\norgan of state in response to an invitation for the origination of income-generating contracts\nthrough any method envisaged in legislation that will result in a legal agreement between the\norgan of state and a third party that produces revenue for the organ of state, and includes, but is\nC2-10\n\nnot limited to, leasing and disposal of assets and concession contracts, excluding direct sales\nand disposal of assets through public auctions; and\ne) “the Act” means the Preferential Procurement Policy Framework Act, 2000 (Act No. 5 of 2000).\n1.
