C3-96\nC3-2\n\nC3 SCOPE OF WORKS\nC3.1 GENERAL REQUIREMENTS\nC3.1.1 Scope\nThis section covers matters that relate to the project as a whole. Definitions, phrases or wording that would\notherwise require repetition in other sections of this document are also covered in this section. Matters covered\nby the General and/or Particular Conditions of Contract are not repeated in this section, except to provide more\ndetailed information.\nC3.1.2 Location of the Project\nThe proposed project is located on Kloppersbos/Pyramid Road, Section under the City of Tshwane\nMetropolitan Municipality from (km 0.0) to (km 12.9). A locality plan including details of the construction history\nand existing pavement structure along this portion of the route are available in SARDS (South African Road\nDesign System) online. A user manual is included in Appendix M in Part C4 of this document.\nC3.1.3 Description of the Project\nThe envisaged scope of the works have been established through the SANRAL Asset Management System\n(AMS) verified by regional panel inspections that have either formed part of the Employer’s normal business\npractices or have been the subject of previous separate assignments by the same or another Service Provider.\n.\nNew Facilities: The Employer’s AMS currently indicates that additional capacity is required that may take the\nform of upgrading the existing single carriageway to a dual carriageway and the construction of new grade-\nseparated interchanges indicating the closing off of existing access for public roads.\nTraffic/Transport\nPerform a detailed traffic/transport analysis study to incorporate at least the following:\n• Determine the current traffic patterns and composition along the entire road section and provide\nforecasts for a 20-year design horizon;\n• Determine the current public transport operations and assess future public transport infrastructure\nneeds\n• Determine current Non-Motorised Transport (NMT) activities along the road section and determine\nfuture NMT infrastructure needs with appropriate Universal Access where feasible;\n• Undertake the capacity analysis (LOS) along the road section using the Highway Traffic Model\n(HTM) for the current and design year\n• Registration of the project under the general authorization.\nGeometrics:\n• Determine road reserve requirements if applicable;\n• Develop a cross section for the design period;\n• Identify need for re-alignments – vertical and/or horizontal;\n• Upgrade of existing intersections and providing pedestrian safety facilities including providing bus\nstop areas.\n• Provide for new intersections where required.\nDrainage:\n• Check capacity of drainage structures as well as road surface and bridge decks to handle storm\nwater;\n• Address issues where capacity does not compare to standards;\n• Provide appropriate subsoil drainage system; and\n• Provide appropriate surface drainage system.\nC3-3\n\nStructures:\n• Do a visual assessment of all structures;\n• Determine compliance with standards with respect to hydraulic capacity, geometrics, balustrades,\netc.\n• Determine whether structures need to make provision for pedestrian traffic;\n• Widening of bridges and other structures where required for capacity improvements;\n• Rehabilitation of bridges and other structures where required; and\n• Carry out post rehabilitation/ construction Inspections.\nPavement:\n• Identify required pavement capacity for design life;\n• Determine capacity of existing pavement;\n• Determine pavement reconstruction and/or new pavement required, utilising the existing capacity\noptimally;\n• Provide options for flexible and rigid pavements;\n• Provide surfacing options; and\n• Provide pavement design life strategy, e.g. maintenance actions over design life.\nMaterial Investigation and Utilisation:\n• Identify material sources required;\n• Optimise the utilisation of project identified borrow pits/ hard rock quarries vs commercial material\nsources for earthworks and pavement layers;\n• Where applicable, provide a Mass Haul Plan/Diagram;\no Provide information on commercial sources:Contact details.\no Types of material available.\no Compliance of the material with the specifications.\n• Identify potential water sources for construction purposes;\n• The possibility of using alternative material from Mine waste dumps, Tailings and or other recycled\nmaterials should be explored. This will entail liaison/negotiating with Mining companies or other\nlandowners.\n• Identify material sources required;\n• Optimise the utilisation of project identified borrow pits/ hard rock quarries vs commercial material\nsources for earthworks and pavement layers;\n• Where applicable, provide a Mass Haul Plan/Diagram;\n• Provide information on commercial sources:\n• Contact details\n• Types of material available\n• Compliance of the material with the specifications.\n• Alternative mining waste material if available.\n• Identify potential water sources for construction purposes.\n• The possibility of using alternative material from Mine waste dumps, tailings, and/or other recycled\nmaterials should be explored. This will entail liaison/negotiating with Mining companies or other land\nowners.\nGeotechnical:\n• Determine stability of existing cuts and fills;\n• Determine stability of existing retaining structures;\n• Identify remedial actions if required;\n• Provide appropriate geotechnical solutions for new cuts and/or fills and/or widening of existing cuts\nand/or fills as well as for new and existing retaining structures (E.g. Slope Failure);\n• Provide appropriate geotechnical solutions for new structures’ and/or widening of existing structures’\nsubstructures (E.g. Piling); and\n• Assist with the hard rock quarry investigations where verification is required in terms of quality of the\nhard rock/aggregates.\nC3-4\n\nAncillary Works:\n• Assess and address road safety issues;\n• Provide for adequate fencing where required;\n• Provide for appropriate roads signs and road markings; and\n• Where value engineering solutions are considered provide mitigation measures for road safety\nissues that may arise such as the protection of bridge balustrades and piers where insufficient\nhorizontal clearance is available.\nTraffic Accommodation:\n• Provide accommodation of traffic options taking into account traffic volumes, peak hour traffic,\nrequirements of the Employer, etc. and\nLand Acquisition:\n• When land needs to be required, the Service Provider must perform its duties timeously and compile\nthe required documentation to enable the Property Service Provider to acquire the land according to\nprogramme.\nServices:\n• Investigate and identify the services that will be affected by the project. These could include amongst\nothers:\no Telkom\no Eskom\no ICT fibre optic cables (Including provision of sleeves for future services)\no Farmer irrigation pipes/Canals\n• Determine the existence of current wayleaves of affected service/s and obtain copies thereof.\n• Determine the entity responsible for the relocation and the cost thereof.\n• Where the Employer has no wayleave for the service, the Service Provider must complete a\nwayleave application on behalf of the service owner to the Employer.\nEnvironmental:\nIt is envisaged that the following processes and/or authorizations will be required for this project:\n• Department of Water and Sanitation (DWS).\n• Department of Forestry, Fisheries and Environment (DFFE).\no Environmental Impact Assessment (EIA) of roadworks.\n• Department of Mineral Resources and Energy (DMRE).\no Application with Department of Minerals Resources (Borrow pits and Quarries)\n• Specialist Studies\nThe Services required of the Service Provider are divided into the following distinct stages:\n− Project Assessment.\n− Investigations for Design Development.\nDesign Development, which shall include the following phases:\no Concept\no Preliminary\no Detailed design\n− Tender Documentation.\n− Clarification Meeting, Tender Period and Tender Evaluation.\nC3-5\n\n− Administration and Monitoring of the Works Contract.\n− Additional duties, Special Services and Specialist Advice.\n− Quality Control: Works Contract.\n− Close Out,.\nC3.1.4 Determination of Remuneration\nThe basis for determining remuneration shall be based on 4 (four) different methods, which are not necessarily\nmutually exclusive, namely:\n• Fee based on the cost of works;\n• Separate payment for services that are additional to those provided for in the normal fee-based\ncalculation;\n• Time based fees; and\n• Reimbursable expenses.\na) Fee determination\nThe determination of fees will be based on different construction work types, i.e. road works, structures and\nother works (e.g. mechanical, electrical, electronic, buildings, etc.). The estimated cost of construction for each\nwork type is provided by the Employer. This cost of works is a forecasted estimate escalated to the planned\ncommencement date of the works contract (excluding contingencies, CPA during the construction period and\nVAT). specified in the project programme.\nC3.1.5 Employer’s Cost Estimate\nFor the purposes of this project, the current budget for the Works is based on the development of a new facility,\nfor upgrading from gravel to surfaced road (DNUSC) .\nThe estimated costs of the different construction work types with a construction start date of two years after\nthe commencement of this contract:\nRoad works R 103 000 000.00\nStructures (bridges and other major structures) R 17 000 000.00\n(EST) Total project value (excluding contingencies,\nCPA during the construction period and VAT) R 120 000 0000.00.\nThe sum of the estimated costs of the relevant work types (including pro-rata of Chapter 1.3 (Contractor’s Site\nEstablishment and General Obligations) of COTO Draft Standard Specifications) forms the basis on which the\nService Provider shall tender his fee to cover all his costs to develop the detailed design including training.\nProject assessment costs and production of tender documentation including evaluation of tenders received\nare not considered to contribute to the development of the design and are separately billed. The final cost of\nthe Works will, however, depend on the strategy selected by the Employer based on the most appropriate and\ncost-effective options identified by the Service Provider during the project assessment and design phases.\nScope of work changes may cause an increase or decrease in the fee offered to produce the design.\nC3-9\n\nC3.1.6 Standards, Manuals and Guideline Documents\nThe standards, manuals and guideline documents to be used in the project are as follows (latest revision to\nbe used as applicable):\n- TMH 3: Specifications for the Provision of Traffic and Geotechnical and Materials Investigation,\nDesign and Documentation)\n- SANRAL’s Code of Procedure for the Planning and Design of Highway Structures in South Africa\n- SANRAL’s Geometric Design Manual\n- SANRAL’s Drainage Manual\n- SANRAL’s Land Acquisition Guideline Manual for Consulting Engineers\n- SANRAL’s Statutory Control Guideline Manual\n- The Highway Capacity Manual\n- SANRAL's best practice\n- Conditions of Contract for Construction: FIDIC; 1999\n- Draft Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works: COTO; October 2020\n- The Employer’s relevant proforma documentation for services, sub-services, evaluations, etc.\n- Guidelines to formalise SANRAL’s jurisdiction on National Roads (N14/5/6 revised manual)\n- SANRAL’s Structures Tracking spreadsheet (refer to Structures under Part C4)\nThe style, format and presentation of the documents prepared by the Service Provider shall be in accordance\nwith the requirements of the Employer.\nThe latest versions and editions of these standards shall always be used. These standards are generally\navailable on the Employer’s website (www.sanral.co.za) or other industry role players’ websites, e.g. CSIR\nwebsite (www.csir.org.za). Others can be purchased from SANRAL and organisations such as CESA, SAICE,\netc.\nGuidelines for drawings are contained in Annexure 21: CAD Symbols, Signs and Weights of Technical\nMethods for Highways TMH 11: Standard Survey Methods. This reference contains all the relevant information\nrelating to grid intervals, topographical detailing, symbols, colours, weight and letter sizes etc.\nThe above is not an exhaustive list but shall be viewed as being the minimum standards applicable to the\nproject. The Service Provider must apply his own knowledge and experience and recommend to the Employer\nother appropriate standards for his consideration.\nC3.1.7 Stakeholder and Community Liaison and Social Facilitation\na) Purpose\nTo give effect to the need for transparency and inclusion in the process of delivering services, the\nService Provider shall liaise with the project Stakeholders and affected Communities for the duration of\nC3-10\n\nthe contract’s life cycle. This shall be achieved through structured engagement with the PLC which was\nestablished for this purpose.\nb) Existing contracts\nThe Employer currently has a routine road maintenance (RRM) contract in progress along the route.\nThe Service Provider shall not visit the site for purposes of undertaking any investigation without first\ninforming the Route Manager of the visit and what type of investigation is intended. The RRM contractor\nneeds to be able to plan his accommodation of traffic duties and no inspection or investigation can take\nplace without acquiring assistance from the RRM for safe conduct through the site or protection when\nundertaking intrusive or out-of-vehicle surveys. The Service Provider shall timeously liaise with the\nEmployer and his appointed Route Manager in order to program the assistance required. The contact\ndetails of the parties involved in the RRM contract are as follows:\nRoute Manager: TBC\nContractor: TBC\nc) Stakeholders\nAny Stakeholder who is affected by the Employer’s operations in the Target Area(s) and/or who has an\ninterest or concern in the project, either as a decision maker, participant or affected party and may\ninclude, amongst others, the following entities:\na) Provincial departments;\nb) Municipal departments;\nc) Traditional authorities;\nd) Community interest groups;\ne) Organised youth representation;\nf) Organised women representation\ng) Organised disabled people representation;\nh) Organised labour representation;\ni) Other structured community groups such as religion, education, farming, etc.;\nj) Transport industry forums;\nk) Business sector forums;\nl) Road user forums;\nm) Environmental interest groups;\nn) Road safety interest groups; and\no) Any other recognised relevant and representative structure\nThe project is located within the City of Tshwane Metropolitan Municipality, for the upgrade of\nKloppersbos/Pyramid road and is in Gauteng Province.\nd) Target Area for the sourcing of labour for Construction Contract\nThe target area for the sourcing of labour only needs to be defined in consultation with the Project\nLiaison Committee (PLC) and may typically include:\n(i) One or more Provinces;\n(ii) One or more Metropolitan and District Municipalities;\n(iii) One or more Local Municipalities; or\n(iv) One or more Wards that are wholly located within an area within a predefined radius of the\nconstruction activity.\n(v) One or more of the areas listed in the definition of Designated Groups.\ne) Designated Group for Sub-contracting to Works Contractor\nUnless otherwise stated in the Works Contractor’s tender documents, a minimum of 30% (thirty percent)\nsub-contracting provision will apply as a contractual obligation, and will be restricted to:\n(i) Black designated groups as defined in the Codes of Good Practice issued in terms of Section\n9(1) of the Broad-Based Black Economic Empowerment Act, 2003 (Act 53 of 2003);\n(ii) Black people as defined in Section 1 of the Broad-Based Black Economic Empowerment Act,\n2003 (Act 53 of 2003);\n(iii) Black people who are women and who are South African citizens;\n(iv) Black people who are youth as defined in Section 1 of the National Youth Development Agency\nAct, 2008 (Act 54 of 2008);\n(v) Black people who are people with disabilities as defined in Section 1 of the Employment Equity\nAct, 1998 (Act 55 of 1998)\nC3-11\n\n(vi) Black people who are military veterans as defined in Section 1 of the Military Veterans Act, 2011\n(Act 18 of 2011);\n(vii) Black people who are living in rural or under-developed areas or townships;\n(viii) Small enterprises as defined in Section 1 of the National Small Enterprise Act, 1996 (Act 102 of\n1996);\n(ix) Exempted Micro Enterprises (EMEs) as defined in terms of the Code of Good Practice on black\neconomic empowerment issued in terms of Section 9(1) of the Broad-Based Black Economic\nEmpowerment Act, 2003 (Act 53 of 2003), with an annual turnover of R10 million or less\n(Construction Sector Amended Codes of Good Practice); and\n(x) Qualifying Small Enterprises (QSEs) as defined in terms of the Code of Good Practice on black\neconomic empowerment issued in terms of Section 9(1) of the Broad-Based Black Economic\nEmpowerment Act, 2003 (Act 53 of 2003), with an annual turnover of R10 million and R50 million\n(Construction Sector Amended Codes of Good Practice).\nf) Project Liaison Committee (PLC) and Project Management Team (PMT)\nA Project Liaison Committee (PLC) has either been established prior to commencement of the contract\nor shall be established as soon as possible by the Service Provider. The PLC represents the project’s\nStakeholders. The PLC consist of representatives of project Stakeholders and affected communities\nfrom the following local municipalities, City of Tshwane Metropolitan Municipality as well as the Project\nManagement Team (PMT) and their representatives.\nThe PMT comprises representation from the Employer, the Service Provider and the Contractor (during\nconstruction phase). Together with the PLC, the PMT is responsible for successful project Stakeholder\nand community liaison and successful implementation of the Employer’s Contract Participation Goals.\nWhere a PLC has not been established, the Service Provider, under the guidance of the Employer, shall\nestablish such a committee within the boundaries of the Local and/or District Municipality. Allowance\nhas been made for these requirements in the Pricing Schedule under Additional Duties.\ng) Duties of the PLC\nThe PLC is the official communication channel through which the PMT communicates with project\nStakeholders and affected communities on project matters, as well as to communicate the impact that\nthe project has or might have on project Stakeholders and affected communities.\nThe PLC is also the official communication channel through which project Stakeholders and affected\ncommunities communicates with the PMT on the impact that the project has on them, or is anticipated\nto have on them, or on any other project matters.\nThe Standard Terms of Reference (TOR) for PLCs requires of the PLC to execute specific duties during\neach stage of the project, i.e. from project initiation to project completion. Some of these duties overlap\nproject stages and hence, a full description is provided here.\nThe PLC shall execute the following duties:\nProject Initiation and Design Stages:\nNote: The principles of the TOR shall not be amended, but duties and procedures may be altered to be\nproject specific and to improve the functionality of the PLC.\n(i) Meet as often as required with the Employer and the Service Provider, to discuss and resolve the\nproject’s initiation and design stage matters, which are of interest or concern to project\nStakeholders and affected communities, the Employer and the Service Provider;\n(ii) Peruse the standard TOR for PLCs and make recommendations on the duties of, and procedures\nto be followed by, the PLC to fulfil its duties;\nNOTE: The principles of the TOR shall not be amended, but duties and procedures may be\naltered to be project specific and to improve the functionality of the PLC.\n(iii) Act in accordance to the agreed TOR for the PLC;\n(iv) Inform the Employer of any training that members of the PLC require to execute its duties;\n(v) Assist the Employer and Service Provider to source suitable candidates for the position of Project\nLiaison Officer (PLO);\nC3-12\n\n(vi) Observe and verify that the procedures applied by the Employer and Engineer to select and\nemploy the PLO were executed in a fair and transparent manner and were within the prescripts\nof the relevant legislation and regulations;\n(vii) Make recommendations to the Employer and Engineer in identifying the project’s Target Area(s),\nfrom which Targeted Labour will be employed, for inclusion in the Tender Documents and endorse\nthe identified Target Area(s); and\n(viii) Make recommendations to the Employer and Engineer in identifying the project’s Target Groups\nfor inclusion in the Tender Documents and endorse the identified Target Groups.\nProject Construction Stage:\nThe points listed below are in accordance with the Employer’s 14-point plan.\nPoint 3: Community Liaison Officer (CLO) or Project Liaison Officer (PLO) selection to be done under\nthe auspices of the PLC.\n(i) Assist the Employer and the Engineer to source suitable candidates for the position of PLO.\n(ii) Observe and verify that the procedures applied by the Employer and Engineer to select and\nemploy the PLO were executed in a fair and transparent manner and were within the prescripts\nof the relevant legislation and regulations.\nPoint 4: Definition of a target area (sometimes referred to as a local area or traffic area) to be done\nunder the auspices of the PLC.\n(i) Make recommendations to the Employer and Engineer in identifying the project’s Target Area(s),\nfrom which Targeted Labour will be employed for inclusion in the tender documents and endorse\nthe identified Target Area(s).\n(ii) Make recommendations to the Employer and Engineer in identifying the project’s Target Groups\nfor inclusion in the tender documents and endorse the identified Target Groups.\nPoint 5: Setup a database of contractors and suppliers (and consultants where relevant) to be done\nunder auspices of the PLC. The final database to be signed off by the PLC.\n(i) Make recommendations to the PMT in establishing the eligibility criteria and tendering processes\nand procedures to be followed to employ Targeted Labour and sub-contract Targeted Enterprises;\nendorse the agreed criteria and employment and sub-contracting methodology(ies).\nPoint 6: Setup of database of local labour for the targeted area to be done under auspices of the PLC.\nThe final list to be signed off by the PLC. An agreed system of labour selection from the database is to\nbe agreed at the PLC.\n(i) Peruse and endorse the Project Database(s) compiled by the PMT from which Targeted Labour\nwill be employed.\n(ii) Verify that the criteria and methodology(ies) applied by the contractor to employ Targeted Labour\nand sub-contract Targeted Enterprises were executed in a fair and transparent manner, and within\nthe Employer’s and Government’s Supply Chain Management Policies.\nPoint 7: Hand-over of signed-off databases for sub-contracting and labour to contractor for open tender\nprocess and recruitment respectively done by the PLC.\nPoint 8: Tender to be conducted by contractor using government principles (e.g. public opening of\nreceived bids, announcement of bidders and prices). Tabling of winning bidders in the PLC.\n(i) Receive reports and ensure transparency in the appointment of Targeted Enterprises in local\nprojects of the Employer notwithstanding that the authority to appoint such Targeted Enterprises\nshall remain with the Employer. (Observer status)\nPoint 9: Appeals on the tender process to be escalated to the Employer for an independent review.\n(i) Agree with the PMT on a dispute resolution mechanism to resolve any disputes that may arise\nbetween the PMT and the PLC, project Stakeholders and/or affected communities.\n(ii) Assist the PMT to liaise with project Stakeholders and the affected communities to resolve any\ndisputes between the Employer, Engineer and/or contractor and project Stakeholders and the\naffected communities, which occurred due to the project.\n(iii) The Employer’s ruling on any dispute regarding the tender process shall considered to be final.\nC3-13\n\nPoint 10: Capability assessments of contractors and suppliers to be done under auspices of PLC prior\nto tender stage, to identify any deficiencies in skills and experience. For labour, skills assessments are\nto be done at recruitment stage.\nPoint 11: Contractor development support and training to be coordinated and conducted, ahead under\nthe auspices of the PLC, prior to project commencement.\n(i) Make recommendations to the PMT on the training needs, eligibility criteria and selection criteria,\nfor the provision of training to Targeted Labour, Targeted Enterprises, project Stakeholders and\nthe affected communities.\n(ii) Observe and verify that training programmes and support programmes, which the contractor\ncommitted to, were implemented and executed as intended.\nPoint 12: Identification of works areas that are deliverable by local Service Providers, and areas where\ncapabilities are not available locally. All works areas where capabilities are not available locally shall be\nimported and locals will be given an opportunity to learn.\nPoint 13: Formal contracting arrangements to be ensured for all projects.\n(i) Verify that the conditions of employment and the conditions of sub-contracting, in the employment\nof Targeted Labour and sub-contracting of Targeted Enterprises were applied in a fair and trans-\nparent manner and according to the Employer’s employment and sub-contracting requirements.\nPoint 14: Communication to be streamlined through the PLC and used to manage expectations of local\nbusiness and communities.\nAdditional Duties of the PLC:\n(i) Inform the entities whom they represent of any project matters which the PMT wishes to\ncommunicate with project Stakeholders and the affected communities.\n(ii) Inform the entities whom they represent of any project matters that are impacting or may impact,\neither positively or negatively, on project Stakeholders and the affected communities.\n(iii) Inform the PMT of Stakeholder and/or community requests and/or needs which could possibly be\naddressed within the project’s Scope of Work.\n(iv) Inform the PMT of any road safety concerns within the project’s Targeted Area(s) and advise the\nPMT of possible mitigating measures and/or road safety programs that will be most feasible for\nacceptance by the affected communities to promote road safety.\n(v) Inform the PMT of any project matters that are impacting, or anticipated to impact, negatively on\nproject Stakeholders and the affected communities.\n(vi) Meet prior to the monthly site meeting, or as may be required, to discuss and resolve project\nmatters, which are of interest or concern to project Stakeholders and the affected communities,\nthe Employer, the Engineer and/or the contractor.\n(vii) Inform the Employer of any training that members of the PLC require to execute its duties.\n(viii) The PLC shall have full powers to decide on any matter which they are empowered to decide on\nfalling within the Project Area and outlined duties.\n(ix) The PLC may assign members to report back to specific sectors and/or constituencies within the\nPLC.\n(x) The PLC may establish working groups and/or ad-hoc committees to fulfil its work subject to fulfil\ntasks as per contract. This must be recommended by the PLO and authorized by the Employer.\nh) Duties of the PMT\nThe PMT, which consists of the Employer, Service Provider and the Contractor, or their representatives,\nis a party to the PLC and hence, is co-responsible for successful project Stakeholder and community\nliaison. The PMT is also responsible for the successful implementation of the Employer’s Targeted\nLabour and Targeted Enterprise utilisation and development goals.\nIn terms of implementing the Employer’s Targeted Labour and Targeted Enterprise utilisation and\ndevelopment goals, the PMT shall, where required, execute the following duties:\n(i) Make recommendations to the Contractor in the identification and structuring of the work\npackages to be sub-contracted to Targeted Enterprises (if any) and agree to the scope and extent\nof the work packages;\nC3-14\n\n(ii) Verify that the Project Database(s) compiled by the Service Provider from which Targeted Enter-\nprises will be sub-contracted, has been updated prior to the letting of every new set of sub-\ncontracts;\n(iii) Approve all Databases, tender procedures, tender documents, tender submission requirements\nand adjudication processes for the sub-contracting of Targeted Enterprises;\n(iv) Review all tender adjudication reports and monitor that the criteria and procedures applied by the\nContractor to sub-contract Targeted Enterprises are executed in a fair and transparent manner\nand are within the Employer’s and Government’s Supply Chain Management Policies;\n(v) Approve sub-contract agreements and ensure that the conditions of sub-contracting with Tar-\ngeted Enterprises are fair and transparent and within the prescripts of the contract requirements;\n(vi) Monitor the management and mentoring programmes of Targeted Enterprise sub-contracts and\nensure that conditions such as the application of penalties, the termination of contracts, etc. are\napplied in a fair and transparent manner and within the prescripts of the agreement, including\nsigning off a monthly report agreed with each Targeted Enterprise sub-contractor on mentoring\noutcomes and further needs identified;\n(vii) Verify that the Project Database(s) compiled by the Service Provider from which Targeted Labour\nwill be employed is updated prior to every new labour intake;\n(viii) Monitor that the criteria and procedures applied by the Contractor to employ Targeted Labour are\nexecuted in a fair and transparent manner and is within the contract requirements;\n(ix) Monitor that the conditions of employment of Targeted Labour are applied in a fair and transparent\nmanner and within the prescripts of the current and relevant Labour Legislation;\n(x) Make recommendations to the Contractor in the identification of the training requirements of\nTargeted Labour and Targeted Enterprises and approve the proposed training programmes; and\n(xi) Monitor that training programmes and support programmes, which the Contractor committed to,\nare implemented and executed as intended.\ni) Targeted Labour Database\nA Project Database of Targeted Labour will be compiled by the PLO, with input from the PLC for the\nTarget Area(s). Once endorsed by the PLC the PLO shall utilise this Database to source Targeted\nLabour as required by the Contractor.\nThe Project Database shall be updated as and when required to reflect new employment seekers in the\nlabour market. Only labour recruited from the Project Database will be measured for Contract\nParticipation Performance (CPP).\nj) Targeted Enterprises Database\nA Project Database of Targeted Enterprises will be developed by the Service Provider during the Design\nPhase, from the National Treasury Central Supplied Database.\nThe Service Provider shall also assist Targeted Enterprises from the Target Area with registration on\nthe National Treasury Central Supplier database.\nk) Social Facilitator\nThe Service Provider shall be required to procure the services of a specialised sub-Service Provider for\nsocial facilitation in terms of Clause C3.1.18 of the Scope of the Works. Alternatively, the Service\nProvider may propose use of in-house social facilitator subject to approval of such proposal by the\nEmployer.\nAllowance has been made for these requirements in the Pricing Schedule under Additional Duties.\nC3.1.8 Permits and Authorisations\nAny Environmental Management Plans/Programmes (EMP's) over and above the Employer's standard plan\n(EMPl) that may be required will be treated as a specialised additional service. Conversely, the compilation of\nany plans/reports necessary to comply with the relevant environmental legislation pertaining to applications to\noperate quarries and borrow pits will be treated as a normal sub-service. Procurement of sub-Service Providers\nin such instances shall be in accordance with the requirements of clause C3.1.18. As approvals of any EMPr's\netc. are essential prior to the commencement of the Works, the Service Provider shall be responsible to ensure\nthat all submissions to the relevant approving authorities Penalties and Delays\nPenalties shall be applied for poor performance as specified in the Contract Data. The quantum of the penalty\nshall be as listed in the Contract Data. Draft reports and documents shall be submitted to the Employer prior\nto any meetings scheduled for the discussion and finalisation thereof as specified in the documentation\nrequirements. In the event of such instances arising, any extensions of time\ngranted shall be limited to the equivalent number of calendar days attributable to each instance.\nPenalty changes payable shall be as follows:\na) Failure to meet milestone dates in the Project programme = R2,000.00/day (excl. VAT) subject to a\nmaximum of 10% of the Contract Value.\nb) Failure to capture structures inventory and condition updates onto ITIS before issue of Taking-Over-\nCertificate (TOC) = R5,000.00/structure (bridges and major culverts) per week or part thereof subject to a\nmaximum of R50,000.00 (excl. VAT) per structure.\nc) Failure to disclose Sub-contractors = up to 10% of the Contract value.\nd) Failure to meet the tendered target of work for Targeted Enterprises = 100% of the value by which the\ncumulative value of payments to the Targeted Enterprises fails to meet the tendered percentage.\nC3-16\n\ne) Failure to issue the Employer approved and signed drawings at either the Works Contract Tender Stage\n(as required under Clause C3.5.3: Compilation of Final Tender Document) or at the Commencement of\nthe Works Contract Stage shall result in a penalty of R5,000.00 (ex. VAT) per drawing.\nf) Late capture of monthly ITIS PIM date = R2,000.00/day (excl. VAT) applicable from the first day of the\nnew month following the previous month end, subject to a maximum of 10% of the Contract Value.\ng) Unresolved errors in monthly ITIS PIM data captured without evidence of logging issues = R2,000.00/day\n(excl. VAT) applicable from the first day of the new month following the previous month end, subject to a\nmaximum of 10% of the Contract Value.\nh) Late processing and submission of Contractor’s Payment Certificate = R2,000.00/day (excl. VAT)\napplicable from the first day of the new month following the previous month end, subject to a maximum of\n10% of the Contract Value.\ni) Any audit finding raised against a contract by Internal Audit, AGSA, DOL/DFE related to duties of the\nService Provider = R20,000.00/finding (excl. VAT), subject to a maximum of 10% of the Contract Value.\nj) Late issuing of Job Instructions for contractor using ITIS Desktop/Mobile App for:\n(i) Pothole reports on Vala Zonke App by public for which a Job Instruction has not been issued to\nContractor;\n(ii) Drains/culverts/bridges that are silted up or where flood debris is blocking waterways within road\nreserve as identified from Bridge Inspections;\n(iii) Grass cutting causing unsafe conditions = R2,000.00/day (excl. VAT) for each calendar day that Job\nInstruction is issued late, subject to a maximum of 10% of the Contract Value.\n.\nk) CE/ACE not performing route verification inspection of quality of work done using the ITIS Mobile App\non a monthly basis = the monthly sum to fulfil the duties of the Engineer as per Payment Item 35.01(a)(i).\nl) Late submission of draft as-built records = R5,000.00/day (excl. VAT) for every calendar day later than\n3 (three) months after issuing the Taking-Over-Certificate, subject to a maximum of 10% of the Contract\nValue.\nC3.1.11 Personnel Requirements\na) Key persons\nThe Service Provider’s key persons become a contractual commitment upon award. 10\nMaterials National Diploma in civil\n5\nTechnician engineering\nPr Techni Eng1 2\nTrainee Technician Candidate Technician 0\n1 Registered with Engineering Council of South Africa (ECSA) or any other international body\nrecognised by ECSA, i.e. Washington Accord, Dublin Accord and Sydney Accord.\n2 Registered with Environmental Assessment Practitioners of South Africa (EAPASA).\n3 Relevant experience is the actual number of years, measured from the date of acquiring the base\nNQF qualification (either B.Eng / BSc.Eng / B.EngSci or BSc / B.Eng Tech (Hon) PGDip (Engineering)\n/ M.Eng for Pr.Eng or National Higher Diploma / National Diploma Eng / Masters Diploma / BTech /\nB.Eng Tech / Dip Eng / Adv Dip Eng / Adv Cert (Engineering Practice) plus Adv Dip Eng for Pr.Tech\nEng and National Diploma / Dip Eng / Dip Eng Tech / Adv Cert (Engineering Practice) / Higher\nCertificate (Eng) / Adv Cert (Engineering) / Adv Cert (Engineering Practice) for Pr.Techni Eng), or\nmeasured from the date of acquiring the minimum required professional registration worked in the field\nof the specified position. For the road safety audit team leader, the field shall be road safety design\nand/or traffic and transportation engineering and/or geometric design.\n4 Full member of the South African Institute for Engineering Geologist.\n5 Registered with the South African Council for Natural Scientific Professions (SACNASP).\n6 South African Council for Project and Construction Management Professions (SACPCMP)\n7 South African Geomatics Council (SAGC)\ne) Personnel Category Definitions\nThe category of personnel necessary to undertake the work shall be approved by the Employer in accordance\nwith the following definitions:\ni) Category A shall mean a top practitioner whose expertise is nationally or internationally recognised and\nwho provides advice at a level of specialisation where such advice is recognised as that of an expert.\nii) Category B shall mean a partner, a sole proprietor, a director, or a member who, jointly or severally\nwith his other partners, co-directors, or co-members, bears the risks of business, takes full responsibility\nfor the liabilities of such practice, performs work of a conceptual nature in engineering design and\ndevelopment, provides strategy guidance in planning and executing a project and/or carries\nresponsibility for quality management pertaining to a project. (e.g. The Engineer for the project)\niii) Category C shall mean all salaried senior professional and technical staff performing work of an\nengineering nature and who carry the direct technical responsibility for one or more specific activities\nrelated to the project. A person referred to in Categories A or B may also fall in this category if such\nperson performs work of an engineering nature at this level. (e.g. the resident engineer for the project)\niv) Category D shall mean all other salaried technical staff performing work of an engineering nature under\nthe direction and control of any person contemplated in categories A, B or C.\nf) Limitation on commitments of Key Persons and Other Required Resources (Design Specialists)\nKey Persons and additional required resources (Design Specialists) other than Key Persons will be limited to\nparticipate in a maximum number of 6 (six) conventional contracts in design phase.\nEach proposed candidate shall complete the relevant B1 and B2 forms included in the Returnable Schedules.\nThe Project Leader (PL) and the Assistant Project Leader (APL) (if applicable) will be limited to participate in\na maximum number of 6 (six) conventional contracts in active design phase which will be complete after the\naward of the Works Contract tender.\nDesign Specialists (DS) and Assistant Design Specialists (ADS) (if applicable) will be limited to participate in\na maximum number of 6 (six) conventional contracts in the design phase with the Employer which will be\ncomplete after the Detailed Design report is accepted by the Employer.\nC3.1.12 Meetings and Liaisons\nC3-19\n\na) Meetings and liaisons between the Employer and the Service Provider\nMeetings between the Employer and the Service Provider are formal occasions. The Employer shall perform\nthe duties of chairperson and the Service Provider the secretarial services. The Service Provider shall submit\ndraft minutes to the Employer for review before distribution which shall not be later than 14 (fourteen) calendar\ndays after the meeting. Meetings and liaisons shall be scheduled according to the Service Provider’s approved\nprogramme to discuss and record the progress of the Services.\nDraft copies of all reports, design and tender documents submitted for review shall be discussed at specially\nconvened meetings prior to their finalisation.\nAttendance at the meetings and liaisons shall include joint venture members and/or Targeted Enterprise(s) (if\nany), sub-Service Providers and the designated key persons. A key person shall not be substituted by another\nService Provider employee unless express permission for this has been sought from and approved by the\nEmployer in writing. The Employer shall have the right to delay a meeting because of the non-availability of a\nkey person and any delay costs so incurred shall be for the account of the Service Provider.\ni) Project hand-over meeting\nThe date of the meeting will have been fixed and notice for it and an agenda included with the\nletter of acceptance of the Service Provider’s offer.\nThe Service Provider shall come to this meeting prepared by his desktop assessment of the\nproject details. The meeting provides a platform for the Service Provider to explain in detail how\nhe intends going about the design development with specific reference to the methods, manuals\nand systems he will apply. For example, the relevant chapters dealing with pavement composition\nand behaviour, materials testing, standards, materials utilisation, design, etc. contained in the\nSouth African Pavement Engineering Manual (SAPEM) and the supporting suite of TMH and TRH\npublications have particular significance. With the release of SARDS the process to be followed\nwill be dictated by the SARDS software process flow. Similarly, the Code of Procedure for the\nPlanning and Design of Highway Structures in South Africa shall form the basis of the Service\nProvider’s intended design development for structures. The Service Provider shall identify those\nhe thinks are relevant and anticipates he will use in developing the project design.\nIn addition, the Service Provider shall explain and demonstrate his awareness of the encom-\npassing social, natural, economic, safety and statutory environment within which the project is\nsituated and how each impacts on it.\nFurther, the Service Provider shall demonstrate his understanding of the Topographical survey\nand Land Acquisition process and the critical aspects that would give rise to material delays in\nthe project proceeding to the Design and Construction Phase.\nThe Employer has a standing agreement with a Comprehensive Property Management Service\nProvider and hereinafter referred to as the “PSP” and may elect to have the PSP present at this\nmeeting.\nThis meeting also provides the opportunity for administration details to be finalised. Included\namong these, but without providing limits to what may be required shall be:\n− Signing and initialling of the contract document;\n− Confirmation of prescribed insurance cover, including that of joint venture members (if\nany);\n− Confirmation of vendor registration;\n− Delivery of a compact disc (CD) containing all relevant forms and procedures needed for\neffective project administration.\nii) Progress meetings\nThe date for the first progress meeting shall be fixed at the hand-over meeting mindful that it\ncannot take place until the Service Provider has conducted his in loco inspection of the project\nsite. The first progress meeting shall not be scheduled longer than six calendar weeks after the\nhand-over meeting. Proposals for investigation and intrusive testing will be discussed at this first\nmeeting.\nC3-20\n\nThe Service Provider’s programme, as envisaged at the hand-over meeting shall be fine-tuned\nand approved at the first progress meeting. The approved programme shall identify the number\nand frequency of progress meetings. They may be reduced or increased as necessary to achieve\nthe milestone date of delivery of the approved detailed design.\nTopographical surveys and the Land Acquisition process must form an integral part of the Service\nProvider’s programme and must be listed separately in their proposed programme.\nIn addition, the Service Provider’s programme must have careful regard to the need to produce\nProperty Reports at the earliest possible time. This aspect may necessitate the presence of the\nPSP at this meeting.\nThe purpose of progress meetings is to discuss the development of the project design and the\nminutes produced shall form the record of progress against programme. The minutes shall record\nthe discussions held and the decisions made.\nDuties of some sub-Service Providers are not directly related to the development and production\nof the project design; nevertheless, because of the severe impact their progress can place on the\nprogramme, their presence at, and participation in, design progress discussions is not negotiable.\nThey shall attend progress meetings and any delays caused by postponements due to their non-\navailability shall be to the account of the Service Provider.\niii) Gateway Review meetings\nAll major capital projects having an estimated capital expenditure of more than R100 million, but\nless than R500 million shall have a Regional Gateway Review (RGR) process at the end of\nConcept or Preliminary Design Phase (whichever is applicable), prior to the acceptance of such\napplicable design.\nAll major capital projects having an estimated capital expenditure equal to or above R500 million\nshall have a National Gateway Review (NGR) process at the end of Concept or Preliminary\nDesign Phase (whichever is applicable), prior to the acceptance of such applicable design.\nThe review meeting shall be initiated by the Employer and the focus of such review meeting shall\nin the first instance be on the quality of the documentation and thereafter on:\n1. deliverability (the extent to which a project is deemed likely to deliver the expected benefits\nwithin the declared cost, time and performance envelope);\n1. affordability (the extent to which the level of expenditure and financial risk involved in a\nproject can be taken up on, given the Employer’s overall financial position, both singly and\nin the light of other current and projected commitments); and\n1. value for money.\nThe time allocations for RGR and NGR meetings is restricted, with a total time allocation of 2\n(two) hours.\nPresentations must be done within 90 minutes to allow for discussion and summaries at the end.\nPresentations must be direct and specific (do not read them), include all important information\nand optimize the use of diagrams, with questions from the team being dealt with on-demand.\nThe following is a guide towards the logical sequencing of a presentation.\nNot all projects are the same, so not all the items below may be applicable, and there may be\nelements that are not in the list but are important in the development of the concepts.\nPlease prepare accordingly and test your timing before the meeting.\nThe proposed guide (which is not prescriptive) is as follows:\n• Ensure that the presentation if in a logical order:\no Location – where is it? What is the environment (land use) it passes through?\no Functional classification – based on the above – not what the road looks like.\n• What infrastructure is there? – (Summary of Assessment Report)\no Cross section.\no Pavement and condition – residual life.\nC3-21\n\no Drainage and structures\n• Traffic data\no Available information and additional counts/information collected. Display where it comes from on\na map.\no Summary of traffic data and definition of traffic sections along the route (when done and\nseasonality adjustments).\no Line diagram of counts – traffic per traffic section – based on electronic and manual counts – light\nand heavy vehicles.\no Forecasts – determination method for light and heavy and provided light and heavy growth rates\n(A&B).\no Line diagram of design year forecasts – light and heavy.\no Highway capacity analysis – HTM results – x-section requirements – if passing/climbing lanes are\nrequired show their location on a sketch.\no Intersection capacity analysis – proposals for upgrades.\no Sensitivity tests (variation on A).\no E80 assumptions and calculations per traffic section.\no Pedestrians and NMT – comment and what must be incorporated in the design.\no Public transport – comment and what must be incorporated in the design.\no Access management – spacing.\n• Geometrics\no Existing posted speeds – show limits on a sketch and why they are there.\no Desired posted speeds – based on functional classification.\no Horizontal and vertical alignment – issues related to existing posted speeds and issues related to\nthe desired posted speed.\no Cross-section – general cross section based on traffic and specifications.\no If passing/climbing lane are required – show their location on a sketch.\no NMT/PT inclusion.\no Road reserve implications.\no Centre line and width through structures.\no Option 0 – base case – as per spec – existing posted/design speeds fix where necessary.\no Option 1 – recommended alignment conforming to desired speeds (where feasible).\no\n• Geotech and Materials\no Geology overview.\no Problematic areas.\no Material sources.\n• Pavements\no Existing pavement – analysis.\no Loading and sensitivity per traffic section – based on heavy vehicle E80 above. Pavement class.\no Pavement design options per section (if loading is significantly different over traffic sections).\no Pavement design option 2.\no Pavement design option 3 … etc. if more.\no Recommendation – with reasons.\n• Drainage\no Existing drainage and condition – sketch where they are.\no Hydrology – methods and results\no Improvements necessary due to existing condition, cross section widening, hydrology issues.\no Impact on road level – where is it impacted?\no Recommendations.\n• Structures\no Existing condition – where they are.\no Condition.\no Existing cross section.\no Hydrology/clearance diagrams.\no Bridge type/construction method.\no Options.\no Recommendations.\n• Utilities and services impacted\n• Constructability/construction method – risk assessment\n• Environmental\no Considerations – scan/risks.\no Mitigations.\no Recommendations – impact on design.\nC3-22\n\n• Costs\no Construction cost breakdown – earthworks, roadworks, structures, relocation of services, other.\no Consultant’s fees.\no Total costs per scenario.\n• Economics\no Confirmation of inputs HDM4.\no Base case description – including traffic volumes, speeds cross sections.\no Scenario descriptions.\no Benefits – future cost savings (operations and maintenance, travel time, accidents).\no Costs – financial to economic.\no HDM4 results IRR for each scenario.\nb) Other project related meetings\nThe Service Provider must ensure that proceedings of meetings (whether formal or informal) are formally\ndocumented. These meetings may be between the Service Provider, sub-Service Providers, the PSP and/or\nother Stakeholders. The Employer may attend these meetings.\ni) Project Liaison Committee (PLC) meeting\nThe PLC will meet on an ad-hoc basis during the Design Stage of the project to discuss and\nresolve project related issues and matters of interest or concern to project Stakeholders and\naffected communities, the Employer and the Service Provider.\nThe PLC will meet prior to the monthly site meetings or as may be required from time to time\nduring the Construction Phase to discuss and resolve project related issues and matters of\ninterest or concern to project Stakeholders and affected communities, the Employer and the\nService Provider.\nii) Works Contract Hand-over meeting\nIt is required that the Contract Engineer and the Engineer’s Representative (Resident Engineer)\nattend the works contract hand-over meeting.\nC3.1.13 Risk Management\nThe concept of risk shall be discussed at all meetings, starting with the hand-over meeting. The Service\nProvider shall identify restrictions and limitations on the design development that the various processes may\nimpose, estimate the probability that they will impact on performance, advise what measures are proposed to\navoid them and what mitigating measures could be taken to mitigate in the event they do occur. This creates\na risk register that must be raised and discussed at each successive progress meeting.\nUsed properly, the risk register will predict or pre-warn of change as the probability of an event increases so\nwill the need for a Works Authorisation to approve extensions of time or allocation of more funds become more\ncertain.\nA base risk register shall be discussed and recorded at the hand-over meeting. It shall be re-visited at each\nsuccessive progress meeting to confirm the status of each and record any changes. The opportunity to add\nmore or remove others will also be discussed at the progress meetings.\nA fundamental element of risk assessment, but by no means the only one, will be economic affordability\nmeaning that each design strategy must be separately and broadly estimated for comparison against the basis\nof the Employer’s budget for the project. In essence, this analysis relates to testing the economic and technical\nfeasibility of the Employer’s envisaged design strategy. Similarly, other identified risks will have a cost element\nto them, and these must also be brought to the Employer’s attention for discussion and decision. The Employer\nmay require the Service Provider to make us of its standard risk reporting format.\nC3.1.14 Document Management\nThree (3) hard copies (or otherwise agreed with the Employer) of draft reports and/or documents shall be\nsubmitted 10 (ten) calendar days prior to the meeting dates for discussion purposes. All changes emanating\nfrom these meetings shall be incorporated into a final version. The record of the changes shall be by means\nof track changes to the draft version and submitted in electronic format.\nC3-23\n\nWhen a volume consists of more than 1 book, the number of the volume appears on the cover and on the title\npage, together with the number of the book, e.g. Volume 3: Book 1 of ……, or Volume 3: Book 2 of ……...\nAll reports and/or documents shall utilise the Employer’s proforma formatting without alteration unless specific\nrequest for a change has been submitted to and approved by the Employer. Proforma report formats are\ncontained in Part C4: Site Information.\nThe table below lists the requirements of documentation that the Service Providers shall apply, unless\notherwise agreed with the Employer.\nTable 3.1.14: Documentation Requirements\nDocument Draft Final Reports/Documents Books of Drawings\nCharacteristics Reports/Documents\nPage A4 80g/m² A4 80g/m² A2 80g/m²\nCover page layout Employer’s proforma works document cover page with appropriate wording for the\nspecific report/document compiled\nCover White 80g/m² Reports: Tokai Blue 160g/m² Draft: White 80g/m²\nDocuments: Red 160g/m² Final: Red 160g/m²\nPrinting Back-to-back pages Reports: back-to-back All on separate pages\nAll left-hand Contract document: back-to-back\nmargins to be except:\n2.5cm - New sections\nPrinting to be left - All returnable schedules\njustified - Pricing schedule\nBinding Plastic ring binders Reports: plastic ring binders Draft: Stapled and\nMaximum thickness Contract documents: stapled, bound\nto be 3cm glued and bound Final: stapled, glued\nand bound\nNumber of paper 3 (three) Reports: 1 (one) Draft: 3 (three)\ncopies Tender documents: 1 (one) Final: 2 (two) original\nTender evaluation: 2 (two) for signature and 1\nContract documents: 2 (two) (one) copy\noriginal for signature and 1 (one)\ncopy\nNumber of 1 (one) CD/DVD 1 (one) CD/DVD 1 (one) CD/DVD\nelectronic copies 1 (one) CD/DVD of signed\ncontract\nElectronic format MS Office 2013 MS Office 2013 and PDF PDF\nand PDF\nC3.1.15 Management of Drawings\nWhen the Service Provider produces drawings, he shall, where applicable, use the Employer’s typical details\nand when doing so they shall become integrated into the Service Provider’s detail design for which he will\nassume full professional responsibility.\nThe requirements shown on the Employer’s proforma drawing, available on the Employer’s web site, shall be\nused. Drawings shall incorporate what has been discussed and agreed at the various progress meetings. All\ndrawings issued to the contractor shall be fully signed and any amendments thereof shall also carry full original\nsignatures.\nC3.1.16 Building Information Modelling (BIM)\nAs part of SANRAL transition to Building Information Modelling (BIM) for roads and bridges, all CAPEX projects\nincorporating improvements (i.e. paved shoulders, additional lanes, horizontal and vertical realignment) and\nnew projects (new carriageway, new bridge, new brownfields/green fields) to be delivered in 3D engineered\nmodels and no longer in 2D. SANRAL is currently initiating a research project to develop open data standards\n(Model View Definition) compliant with Industry Foundation Classes (IFC) data models. IFC is the industry\nstandard in the vertical industry and is well established, and currently has global initiatives working toward\nimplementing BIM for roads and bridges in other countries, making a natural alignment with IFC the most\nappropriate approach process. SANRAL will make these open standards available as soon as finalised. The\nService Provider need to ensure that the Software Packages utilised are able to deliver 3D engineered models.\nC3-24\n\nC3.1.17 Safety\nThis part of the specification has the objective to assist the Service Provider entering into contracts with the\nEmployer that they comply with the Occupational Health and Safety (OH&S) Act, No 85 of 1993, as well as all\napplicable Regulations. Compliance with this document does not absolve the Service Provider from complying\nwith minimum legal requirements and the Service Provider remains responsible for the health and safety of\nhis employees and those of his Mandataries. The Service Provider shall therefore include this part of the\nspecification to any contract that he may have with sub-Service Providers and/or suppliers.\nThis part also covers the development of a health and safety specification that addresses all aspects of occu-\npational health and safety as affected by this contract. It provides the requirements that the Service Provider\nshall comply with in order to reduce the risks associated with this contract, which may lead to incidents causing\ninjury and/or ill health. In this matter the spirit and intention of Regulation 5(1)(l) of the Construction Regu-\nlations, 2014, regarding negotiations between the parties, related to the contents and approval of the Health\nand Safety Plan, must be complied with.\nThe Service Provider shall on award of the contract become the Client’s Agent in terms of Construction Regu-\nlation 5(5), (6) and (7) of the Occupational Health and Safety Act, (Act 85 of 1993). The duties and responsibi-\nlities as the Client’s Agent are as prescribed in Construction Regulation 5(7).\nFor purposes of clarity typical duties required of the Service Provider are given in (b) and (c) below, but they\nshall not be interpreted as being exhaustive and if any conflict between them and the legislated Construction\nRegulations occurs the latter shall rule.\na) Project Specific Requirements\nThe following is a list of Baseline Risk Assessment and project specific health and safety specifications\nprepared by the Client in terms of Construction Regulation 5(1)(a) and 5(1)(f).\ni) Baseline Risk Assessment for Design Phase\n• Working in elevated positions (e.g. Bridge Inspections)\n• Working over water environments (e.g. Bridge Inspections)\n• Excavation – locating existing underground services, digging test pits\n• Manual handling – setting up surveying equipment\n• Ergonomic risks\n• High & Low voltage power lines – overhead & underground\n• Work in close proximity to railway lines\n• Uneven ground surfaces\n• Personal safety & security risks\n• Traffic Control (Ensure Correct Signage at Correct Distances)\n• Working from ladders\n• Exposure to noise\n• Working in close proximity to traffic\n• Working with hot materials\n• Radio Active equipment\n• Hazardous Chemical Substances\n• Flammable Materials\n• Driving to and from site\n• Biological risks e.g. bees, snakes, spiders, etc.\n• Hazardous Biological Agents\n• Environmental risks\n- Bad weather conditions,\no rain,\no lightning,\no wind,\n- poor visibility\n- heat exposure – dehydration\n- cold environment\nii) Baseline Risk Assessment for Supervision Phase\n• Clearing and grubbing of the area/site\n• Site establishment including:\nC3-25\n\n- Office(s)\n- Secure/safe storage for materials, plant and equipment\n- Ablutions\n- Sheltered eating area\n- Maintenance workshop\n- Vehicle access to the site\n- Temporary fuel storage, where applicable\n- Laboratory establishment, where applicable (Client responsibility)\n• Dealing with existing structures – possibility of asbestos\n• Location of existing services, e.g. gas, telecommunications, electrical supply and similar\n• Installation and maintenance of temporary construction electrical supply, lighting and\nequipment\n• Adjacent land uses/surrounding property exposures\n• Boundary and access control/public liability exposures (NB: The Employer is also re-\nsponsible for the OH&S of non-employees affected by his/her work activities)\n• Health risks arising from neighbouring as well as own activities and from the environment,\ne.g. threats by dogs, bees, snakes, lightning, etc.\n• Exposure to a water environment\n• Exposure to noise\n• Exposure to vibration\n• Protection against dehydration and heat exhaustion\n• Protection from wet and cold conditions\n• Hazardous Biological Agents that could lead to epidemics and pandemics\n• Dealing with HIV/Aids and other diseases such as silicosis or asbestosis, where applicable\n• Use of portable electrical equipment including:\n- Angle grinder\n- Electrical drilling machine\n- Circular saw\n- Generator\n• Excavations including:\n- Ground/soil conditions\n- Trenching\n- Shoring\n- Drainage of trenches\n• Welding including:\n- Arc welding\n- Gas welding\n- Flame cutting\n- Use of LP gas torches and appliances\n• Loading and off-loading of trucks\n• Aggregate/sand and other materials delivery\n• Manual and mechanical handling\n• Lifting and lowering operations\n• Driving and operation of construction vehicles and mobile plant including:\n- Trenching machine\n- Excavator\n- Sit on roller\n- Plate compactor\n- Front end loader\n- Mobile cranes and the ancillary lifting tackle\n- Grader\n- Parking of vehicles and mobile plant\n- Towing of vehicles and mobile plant\n• Use and storage of flammable liquids and other hazardous substances, e.g. petrol, diesel,\ncement, asphalt, bituminous materials and similar\n• Layering and bedding\n• Installation of pipes in trenches\n• Pressure testing of pipelines\n• Backfilling of trenches\n• Protection against flooding\n• Gabion work\n• Use of explosives\n• Protection from overhead power lines (High and low)\n• Work adjacent or in proximity of railway lines\nC3-26\n\n• Work adjacent or in proximity of traffic\n• Working at heights\n• Working in confined spaces – tunnelling\n• Formwork and support work (temporary works) including scaffolding\n• Demolition work, where applicable\n• Bulk mixing plant, where applicable\n• Environmental impacts such as pollution of water, air or soil\nb) Design Phase\nThe Service Provider or his registered Professional Construction Health & Safety Agent must identify elements\nof the design that are inherently dangerous or hazardous to the health and safety of Contractor employees\nduring the construction phase and design in such a manner as to mitigate or eliminate the risk where possible.\nTo this avail the Service Provider (or his Agent as outlined above) must prepare a baseline risk assessment\nfor the intended construction work project and make the same available to the Employer. This duty of\nidentification continues into the construction phase of the project regardless that the designer may not be\nmonitoring the construction phase. All identified dangers/hazards are to be listed and brought to the attention\nof potential contractors by means of the baseline risk assessment as outlined above and envisaged in\nConstruction Regulations 5(1)(a); (b) and (c).\nFor example, staging for bridge decks or shoring of unstable excavations is the designer’s responsibility to\nidentify and notify of the health and safety risk. It is also the designer’s responsibility to undertake inspections\nat critical phases of construction to ensure that the identified risks are continuously and appropriately mitigated.\nc) Construction Phase\nDuring the construction phase the supervising Service Provider must ensure that the Employer’s duties are\ncontinuously fulfilled, meaning that the Service Provider has to include amongst its permanent monitoring staff\nat least one appropriately trained member. In addition, the Service Provider must conduct monthly internal\naudits to ensure the site personnel are adhering to the statutory requirements. Costs for performing this duty\nare recoverable via the rate offered for this scheduled item of work. An Occupational Health and Safety Audit\nQuestionnaire is included in Appendix D in Part C4 for this purpose. This audit may be conducted by either the\nappointed registered Professional Construction Health and Safety Agent (Pr. CHSA) or a registered\nConstruction Health and Safety Manager that is contracting-in or employing a Pr. CHSA or who is contracted-\nin or employed by a Pr. CHSA.\nThe Employer may order external audits, the costs of which are separately recoverable as a disbursement to\nthe specialist sub-Service Provider selected to conduct such audits.\nFurthermore, the Service Provider must, in compliance with Section 24 of the Occupational Health and Safety\nAct (Act 85 of 1993) report immediately to the Employer’s Health and Safety specialist and the relevant project\nmanager the details of a Section 24 incident, including confirmation that the construction contractor has\nsimilarly reported the same incident directly to the Department of Labour.\nd) Health related Epidemics and Pandemics\nThe Service Provider shall, as far as reasonably practicable make provision for health-related epidemics and\npandemics that is declared by authorities. The employer is aware that this provision will not speak to specific\ncases. Once the nature and scale of the epidemic or pandemic is known, the Service Provider must ensure\nthat the requirements stipulated in the Hazardous Biological Agents (HBA) Regulation are adhered to and in\nparticular the following as described in the mentioned Regulation:\n• Information and training of employees\n• Duties of person that may be exposed to HBA’s\n• Risk Assessments by the employer (Service Provider)\n• Monitoring exposure at the workplace\n• Medical surveillance of employees\n• Keeping of records\n• Control of Exposure to HBA’s\n• Personal Protective Equipment and facilities\n• Maintenance of control measures and facilities\n• Prohibitions\ne) General Occupational Health and Safety Provisions\nC3-27\n\nSite specific health and safety specifications for the intended construction work based on the Baseline Risk\nAssessment in included in Appendix D in Part C4.\nC3.1.18 Procedure for Procurement of sub-Service Providers\nA sub-service is taken to mean any service necessary for the production of the project design and later con-\nstruction, which is performed by someone other than the Service Provider. A sub-service procured directly by\nthe Service Provider requires the Service Provider to enter into a sub-service agreement with that sub-Service\nProvider. The relationship between the Service Provider and sub-Service Provider is that of contractor/sub-\ncontractor.\nAnother type of sub-service is less direct because the service required has already been procured by the\nEmployer under a separate agreement. The role of the Service Provider in the management of this sub-service\nis that of the Employer’s agent.\nIn both cases the Service Provider is responsible for the performance of the sub-service.\nProcurement of a sub-service shall be undertaken by means of a quotation/tender process. The Service Pro-\nvider shall compile and issue relevant and approved terms of reference, together with the relevant work/pricing\nschedules, in accordance with the Employer’s standard proformas and Supply Chain Management and pro-\ncurement policies. This will require the Service Provider to advertise for an open tender process or invite\nquotations from identified potential sub-Service Providers and submit tender documents to the relevant\nregional office for collection and receipt of the tenders by that office. Submitted quotations/tenders, in a sealed\nenvelope, directly to the Employer's regional office by the date and time agreed, will be opened in public by\nthe Employer’s delegated staff. The Service Provider shall remove under signature all submitted offers for\nanalysis and submit a report with recommendations to the Employer for approval prior to the appointment of\nany sub-Service Provider. Once approved, the Service Provider shall enter into an agreement with the sub-\nService Provider with a copy of same submitted to the Employer.\nService Providers appointed to a project are not permitted to bid for any sub-service related to the project, with\nthe exception to laboratory services. In the case of laboratory services, the appointed Service Provider must\ndeclare the conflict of interest and shall not be involved in the evaluation and adjudication of the resultant\ntender. The Employer will resume this responsibility.\nThe minimum requirements for different types of sub-services to be procured under provisional and/or prime\ncost sums are described in Part C4: Annexure J.\nC3.1.19 Participation of Targeted Enterprise(s)\nThe Employer may stipulate the involvement of Targeted Enterprise(s) in the project design and construction\nas a mechanism to broaden the economic share of the national spend on engineering services and as a means\nto hasten and improve the transfer of technical skills.\nThe Service Provider’s Targeted Enterprise(s) become a contractual commitment upon award.\na) The Targeted Enterprise(s) shall be involved throughout the project stages and the percentage tendered\nin the Contract Data shall be applicable to actual work split (excluding all provisional and prime cost\nsums but including site staff salaries) amongst the following stages:\n• Project Assessment stage\n• Investigation for Design Development\n• Design Development stage\n• Tender Documentation\n• Clarification Meeting, Tender Period & Tender Evaluation\n• Administration and Monitoring of the Works\n• Additional duties, special services and specialist advice\n• Quality control\n• Close out\nb) The use of Targeted Enterprise personnel as site staff during the administration and monitoring of the\nWorks phase is included as part of the percentage tendered in the Contract Data.\nC3-28\n\nC3.1.20 Training\na) Service Provider’s staff and Targeted Enterprise\nThe Employer encourages training of candidate engineers or undergraduates requiring experiential training on\nthis project. Training of the Service Provider’s own permanent staff, temporary staff or Targeted Enterprise\ninvolved in this project becomes a reportable monthly event in terms of time and cost attributable to the\nEmployer’s spend from its allocated budget. Separate allowance has been made to train the Service Provider’s\nand Targeted Enterprise’s staff as assistants to certain required positions.\nb) Employer’s trainees\nThe Employer may enter into a separate arrangement with the Service Provider for training of its own\npermanent employees as a secondment for the express purpose of providing its candidate engineers with\ndesign and/or site supervision experience. The Employer may also enter into an arrangement with the Service\nProvider to provide experiential training to students.\nThe design phase provides the opportunity for students and candidate engineers to receive training. Three\ndistinct categories of training are recognised. The first, and most formal, is the placement of the Employer’s\ncandidate engineers with the Service Provider for training distinctly geared to professional registration with the\nEngineering Council of South Africa (ECSA). The training provided shall be predominantly, but not exclusively,\nrelated to this project and may be subject to scrutiny and inspection in loco by the Employer who is responsible\nfor candidates’ remunerations. The trainees are expected to work according to the Service Provider’s terms\nand conditions of employment, especially those related to confidentiality. They are to be treated as if they were\nthe Service Provider’s own full-time personnel. No allowances shall be made for working overtime. Where\ncircumstances justify need to pay for the accommodation of students, this shall be discussed with the\nEmployers project manager.\nLess formal will be experiential training the Service Provider is requested to provide to university or university\nof technology undergraduate students. The training provided must be in accordance with the academic\ninstitution requirements.\nThe Service Provider, apart from providing the technical training, shall also provide trainees with all the tools\n(including appropriate information technology hardware and software) and space necessary to carry out\nengineering or survey work as if they were the Service Provider’s own permanent staff.\nReporting on training progress of each candidate engineer and student shall be compiled according to the\nformats and intervals set by ECSA (for engineering students) or SAGC (The South African Geomatics Council).\nC3.1.21 Payment and Monthly Reporting using the Employer’s Integrated Transportation Information\nSystem\nWhen submitting interim certificates for payment the Service Provider shall use the Employer’s standard forms\nand formats. No payment can be made before the Service Provider is registered as a vendor on the Employer’s\nsystem.\nThe Service Provider shall submit payment certificates for all work rendered in the Employer’s financial year\nwithin that specific year.\nThe Service Provider shall submit and update on a monthly basis a cash flow forecast for the remuneration of\nthe full service to be rendered.\nThe Service Provider shall complete monthly reports regarding training, empowerment, capacity building, small\ncontractor development, labour and staff returns and any such aspects on the Employer’s Integrated\nTransportation Information System (ITIS).\nThe Employer has developed a comprehensive information management tool called Integrated Transportation\nInformation System (ITIS) to address all facets of its strategic and tactical planning, design, construction and\nmaintenance of the entire road network. This provides support for the management tasks of the Employer and\nto allow the personnel to make technical decisions more quickly and efficiently.\nITIS is an integrated approach to the sharing and inter-relating of technical performance information for the\nEmployer and relies on Service Provider’s people following procedures to populate the system with data. ITIS\ncurrently consist of the following platforms:\nC3-29\n\n• ITIS Web – Web enabled portal providing online access to various functions, workflows and reports.\n• ITIS Desktop – Offline data capture tool enabling the capture of information offline, validation and then\nsynchronisation of data with the ITIS database.\n• ITIS Mobile – Application (Android 6 or later) that allows the in-field capture of information using a smart\nphone or tablet (must have camera and GPS), validation and then synchronisation of data with the ITIS\ndatabase.\nThe Employer has several ITIS modules running on any of the above ITIS platforms which affect the Service\nProvider and Contractor, who will need to use some of these modules to perform certain procedures and to\nprovide required information. The current modules applicable to this contract and their description are as\nfollows:\n• Contract Module – management of contracts;\n• Project Information Module – uploading of employment and training data;\n• Structures Module – uploading of structure’s details.\nUser manuals for the various functions can be downloaded from\nhttps://itis.nra.co.za/Portal/MyAccount/UserManuals after the successful registration as a public user. This ITIS\npublic user registration procedure is explained in the document as attached in Part C4: Appendix K.\nAllowance has been made for these requirements in the Pricing Schedule under Additional Duties. Failure to\ncomply may result in payments being withheld which may require a new invoice to be issued.\nC3.1.22 Contract(s)/Agreement(s) with Targeted Enterprise(s)\nThe Service Provider shall enter into a contract/agreement with the Targeted Enterprise(s) to provide them\nwith the opportunity to participate in SANRAL projects under their guidance. Copies of the contract/agree-\nment(s) shall be provided to the Employer.\nC3.1.23 Multiple Strategies\nService Provider appointments by the Employer premise the fundamental concept that the Service Provider is\ncapable of providing a design that is not just fit for purpose but also carries the hall mark of value for money.\nThe design strategy on which the Employer has based his budget has been explained in section C3.1.5. It is\nexpected of the Service Provider to interrogate the appropriateness of this design and to offer alternatives to\nit for consideration by the Employer.\nC3.1.24 Service Provider’s Estimated Cost of the Works\nThe Service Provider shall at the completion of each stage or phase where applicable, provide the Employer\nwith an updated realistic estimate of the cost of the works. Despite this requirement, the Service Provider shall,\nat the end of September of each year, provide the Employer with an updated realistic estimate of the cost of\nthe works as well as the foreseen duration of the works.\nC3.1.25 Economic Feasibility Analysis\nDevelopment of the design shall include continuous testing of the feasibility of the project so that in the unlikely\nevent of a total misreading of the project scope as prescribed a decision can be made to abandon, change or\ncontinue with the design development.
