The earlier the warning the earlier change decisions can be made.\nWhere applicable for a stage, the feasibility outcome shall be accompanied by the output of HDM 4 analyses\nand even at the first progress meeting comments on the feasibility must be discussed, however unrefined the\ninput parameters might be.\nThe Service Provider shall undertake economic feasibility analysis utilising HDM 4 at the end of the following\nstages/phases:\n• Project Assessment\n• Concept Design\n• Preliminary Design\n• Detailed Design\n• Clarification Meeting, Tender Period and Tender Evaluation\nIn performing the economic feasibility analysis, the Service Provider shall:\n• Utilise the latest release of HDM-4 or any other software as instructed by the Employer.\nC3-30\n\n• Divide the project into uniform sections that as minimum considers changes in geometry/alignment,\npavement condition, construction/maintenance history, climate and traffic characteristics.\n• For each identified uniform section determine the appropriate input parameters for geometry/alignment,\npavement condition, construction/maintenance history, climate, traffic characteristics and calibration\nparameters as per the latest HDM-4 Configuration Setup where applicable. The Employer will provide\nthe general network input parameters.\n• Define the default Employer vehicle fleet with associated inputs as within the latest HDM-4 Configuration\nSetup. This process will also include defining appropriate traffic growth sets for the project or uniform\nsections if applicable.\n• Define a “Do Minimum” maintenance standard consisting of the following routine maintenance activities:\npatching, crack sealing, edge repair and road reserve maintenance, as per the latest HDM-4\nConfiguration Setup where applicable.\n• Define various maintenance and improvement standards under consideration for the various uniform\nsections on the project, as per the latest HDM-4 Configuration Setup where applicable.\n• Perform economic cost benefit analysis, including accident costs and acceleration effects as per the\nlatest HDM-4 Configuration Setup where applicable.\n• Analyse the economic analysis results obtained and submit to the Employer for discussion and review.\nFor this project an HDM-4 economic analysis will be required.\nC3.1.26 Communication Management\nCommunication management must be identified early in the life of the project and discussed with the Employer\nas to who shall be responsible for liaising with which party and to define the limits or authority that either party\nhas to speak for or commit the other.\nAt the earliest stage of design development, the Service Provider should have already identified the relevant\nand potential stakeholders in the project and list those that may play a sanctioning role that could accelerate\nor delay delivery of the project.\nC3.1.27 Services\nThe early identification of services is a critical element in the project. The Service Provider shall identify those\nthat are observable during the project assessment stage and those resulting from further later investigations.\nDuring the design development phase, the Service Provider shall continuously liaise with the identified service\nowners (who form part of the strategic partners) and with the Employer. A first step is to search for any\nwayleave or similar authorisation agreement for the service to be placed within, alongside or across the project\nsite. All liaisons shall be formal and in writing.\nService relocation may be required. If any design is required to temporarily or permanently relocate the service\nsuch design shall not be to the Employer’s account unless agreed during liaison with the service owner.\nRegardless that any agreement is reached the Employer shall not take responsibility for the subsequent\ndesign, it being deemed that it cannot assume liability for property that it does not own or operate. However,\nthe Service Provider is at liberty to offer its design capabilities to the service owner under an agreement\nbetween them. The service owner’s design cost recovery may be via the contract should the Employer and\nservice owner so agree, but no design liability shall pass to the Employer.\nC3-31\n\nC3.2 PROJECT ASSESSMENT STAGE\nC3.2.1 Scope\nThis section covers the project assessment stage which precedes any design development. Thereafter, the\nactual designs will be produced in a single or multiple phases depending on the complexity of the project, This\nstage requires assessment of the project through perusal of relevant reports and previous studies, designs or\nsurveys relating to the project, undertaking new inspections, surveys, studies and/or intrusive investigations,\nincluding subsequent testing, and analysis of test results.\nWhilst the aspect of Land Acquisition may still be premature in the sense that the work required to determine\nwhat land will be required is only done later, it is nevertheless important that the Service Provider is always\nfamiliar with the contents of the SANRAL Land Acquisition Guideline Manual for Consulting Engineers\n(hereinafter referred to as the “LAC Manual’).\nThe stage ends with the production of a report detailing the findings of the assessment and the identification\nof multiple design options to be developed in the following stage.\nC3.2.2 Assessment in Phases\nAssessment of the project shall be conducted in the sequence described hereunder.\nIn the first part of project assessment the Service Provider shall undertake an initial assessment, i.e. a desktop\nassessment of the information provided, which may also include additional data, reports, studies etc. issued\nwith the letter of acceptance of an offer. This part culminates with attendance at the project hand-over meeting\nat which the Service Provider will put forward for discussion and approval of the planned assessments in the\ncontext of an overall design development strategy.\nThe second part of project assessment is the visual assessment and thorough analysis of information provided,\nwhereafter the Service Provider shall arrange for the first progress meeting with the Employer for the purpose\nof confirming the design strategy and acquiring approval for the type, quantity and positioning of intrusive and\nother investigations which form the third part of project assessment.\nFirst and second parts of the assessment requirements are summarised below and expanded in the following\nparagraphs:\n• Review and analyse information provided\n• Conduct visual assessment of the road\n• Identify statutory and regulatory requirements\n• Identify other stakeholders with whom consultations may be needed\n• Consider feasibility of various construction strategies\n• Evaluate risk that the project presents to the Employer\n• Other criteria\nC3.2.3 Initial Assessment\nExisting information with respect to the relevant project is given in Part C4: Site information and/or Part C5:\nAnnexures. The information provided there is deemed sufficient both for purposes of tendering and for initial\nassessment (i.e. desktop assessment). This information shall be studied and used to determine the extent of\nintrusive investigations that need to be undertaken to prove pavement or existing ground or structural\nconditions. In the case of reports, studies or designs previously undertaken and not able to be condensed into\nPart C4: Site Information, these must be collected from the Employer by the Service Provider and returned\ntogether with the submission of the Detailed Design report. Failure to return information for which the Service\nProvider will have signed out from the Employer’s registry will constitute reason for the Employer to withhold\npayment until such information has been returned in an acceptable condition for continued archiving.\nThe Service Provider should not take the existing data at face value only but shall interrogate its integrity and\naccuracy as well as determine the need to access any additional data such as as-built drawings, plans, traffic\ncounts etc. and enquire from the Employer whether such additional information does exist and assist in\nsourcing same if possible.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the available electronic information will be made available online for review\nduring the tender period to assist the Service Provider.\nC3-32\n\nExisting data is, by itself, insufficient to conduct a detailed assessment and must be amplified by visual\ninspections and subsequent surveys and investigations.\na) Traffic assessment\nThe Service Provider shall review the traffic data provided to determine any potential need for capacity and\npavement improvement and identify any need for additional traffic counts or studies.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Capacity Analysis will be performed by the Service Provider using the\nSARDS software.\nb) Geometric assessment\nThe Service Provider shall compare the existing horizontal and vertical alignment data and accompanying\ngeometric values with the Employer’s geometric standards to identify any potential need for capacity\nimprovements.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Capacity Analysis will be performed by the Service Provider using the\nSARDS software.\nc) Pavement assessment\nIdentify uniform sections within the project site.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the identification of uniform sections will be performed by the Service Provider\nusing the SARDS software.\nd) Structures assessment\nAssess the available information and conduct a site inspection of all structures located on the route in order to\nundertake a condition assessment and to gain a general appreciation for the structures and the structural\nissues that may arise as a result of the envisaged work.\nIdentify which structures require capacity improvement (hydrologically, traffic, strength, etc.) and mainte-\nnance/rehabilitation interventions. All structures shall be assessed, however, in addition the following bridges\nhave been identified by the Employer that require assessment for defects to be repaired as part of this project.\ne) Drainage assessment\nAssess all drainage systems and compare to drainage manual guidelines.\nf) Geotechnical assessment\nIdentify potential geotechnical risks from the provided Slope Management System data or any other\ninformation provided.\ng) Assessment of material sources\nAssess information provided from previous contracts (if available).\nh) Safety assessment\nAssess Incident Management System (IMS) data for potential hazardous locations.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the identification of potential hazardous locations will be performed by the\nService Provider using the SARDS software.\ni) Environmental assessment\nThe Employer anticipates that the appropriate environmental authorisation process is a basic assessment.\nFrom data available assess whether the Employer’s expected authorisation process is correct, using the\nservices of an environmental specialist who shall confirm the type of environmental approval, (i.e. basic\napplication, or full environmental investigation including all other permits and authorisations) that must be\napplied for from the relevant Authorities. In addition, the Service Provider; will also assist in identifying the\ncontent for an independent Environmental Assessment Practitioner’s (EAP) sub-service tender document.\nC3-33\n\nj) Land Assessment\nAt this stage, the identification of informal settlements that may impact on the project must be carried out.\nWhere the project traverses land under the control of a Tribal (Traditional) Authority then the identification of\nclusters of homes and/or other structures that may require relocation should be made. The above, if evident,\nmust immediately be reported to the Employer.\nC3.2.4 Visual Survey\nThe Service Provider shall carry out a comprehensive visual assessment survey for the project with the\npurpose of comparing the desktop assessment assumptions against visible reality. Apart from an overall\nimpression the visit shall be used to assess the existing condition of the pavement, all drainage and other\nstructures, roadside furniture, slopes, road safety and all other aspects in order to ascertain the current overall\ncondition. This survey shall be conducted by the Service Provider’s designated design specialist(s) and cannot\nbe delegated to others.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the data from the Visual Assessment based on TMH 9 (Pavements) will need\nto be uploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format in TMH 18.\nThe visual survey shall also include the identification of any other associated aspects located within the road\nprism affecting the functionality/structural integrity of the road and/or road safety issues including but not limited\nto:\n• Surface drainage\n• Sub-surface drainage (including pipe culverts)\n• Present or potential geotechnical instability\n• Excessive shoulder drop-off\n• Intersection details\n• Pedestrian issues\n• Any evident or suspected road safety issues\n• Condition of bridge joints and balustrades/handrails\n• All identifiable services\n• Any other issues that may be glaringly evident\nThe visit should include an inspection of all borrow pits and quarries identified during the initial assessment\nand act on any other relevant information emanating from the hand-over meeting discussions. Where any\nborrow pit or quarry is in any way affected by an informal settlement, the information gathered must\nimmediately be brought to the attention of the Employer.\nThe condition of all existing structures shall be inspected. The BMS inspection sheets shall be forwarded to\nthe Service Provider, who shall verify the accuracy thereof and report on the advancement of the identified\ndefects and the development of any new defects. Rehabilitation and maintenance requirements are to be\nidentified and incorporated into the designs in discussion with the Employer. Any structures that are not listed\non SANRAL’s BMS that are found within the project limits are to be included. SANRAL is to be notified of such\ninclusions in order to update the BMS. The structures are also to be assessed in order to gain a general\nappreciation for the structures and structural issues that may arise as a result of the envisaged works.\nC3.2.5 Identification of Statutory and Regulatory Controls\nThe Service Provider shall identify legislation applicable to this project and bring to the notice of the Employer\ntogether with recommendations how compliance thereto can be incorporated into the project design.\nThe following aspects must be dealt with at this point:\n• Road closures arising from the design;\n• The closure of public places that would arise from the design;\n• The impact must be determined that the project/design will have on zoning criteria for properties in a\ntownship. This is in particular reference to where such properties will be reduced to a size as a result of\nthe design and where such remaining portions will be in conflict with such zoning criteria; and\n• Statutory provisions relating to Mining Rights, Proclamations and De-proclamations of Provincial and\nMunicipal Roads, the closure of streets and public places, the existence of Informal Property Rights,\nTraditional Authorities, etc. need to be clearly identified at this stage.\n• Agreements with Municipality(ies) – the Employer have prepared a “generic” agreement with respect to\nthe following:\no Take-over of Access Roads by a Municipality; and\nC3-34\n\no Declaration of National Road through “Towns”.\nThe above-named agreements are annexures to the LAC Manual and Appendix H of this document.\nThe Service Provider will be required to facilitate the conclusion of the above agreement(s) and where such\nagreement(s) shall be concluded prior to the commencement of any construction phase of the project.\nAll of the above may give rise to the need to appoint other professionals such as a Land Surveyor, Town\nPlanner, etc. in order to ensure formal compliance at the time of Detailed Design. The Service Provider must\ntherefore set out the Terms of Reference with regards to each such statutory requirement in a report to the\nEmployer having specific regard to the time frames involved and an estimate of costs in obtaining such\nstatutory authorisation. In this regard the Employer may instruct the PSP to assist the Service Provider if it\nbecomes evident that there are complexities that would require specialist input.\nC3.2.6 Other Authorities and Stakeholders\nThe Service Provider shall identify all authorities and stakeholders who should be consulted about the design\nand implementation of this project and the degree to which liaison with each is expected. In addition, the\nService Provider shall compile a list of the relevant persons, their capacity to act for such authority or body and\ntheir contact details, with whom liaison must take place but shall not commit to any meetings without first\nengaging the Employer to confirm how and when such meetings should be held.\nWhere the Project traverses land under the control of a Tribal (Traditional) Authority then the Department of\nRural Development and Land Reform is to be notified and a determination and record of which officer is\nresponsible for the area affected must be made.\nWhere a Project Liaison Committee (PLC) has not been established as a community structure, the Service\nProvider under the guidance of the Employer shall establish such a PLC within the boundary of the\nLocal/District Municipality(ies) consisting of members as specified in C3.1.7.\nDuring the development of the design and the construction phases the Service Provider shall continue these\nsame liaison duties, including representing the Employer if so authorised and providing the Employer with\nwritten records of all liaison meetings and conversations.\nAllowance has been made for these requirements in the Pricing Schedule under Additional Duties.\nC3.2.7 Other Criteria\nThe above aspects of project assessment should not be considered as exhaustive. The Service Provider shall\nundertake the first phase of assessment to identify as many items as possible that will need to be considered\nwhen developing the design and the emanating scope of work, including consultation with the relevant routine\nmaintenance Service Provider.\nC3.2.8 Presentation of Visual Survey\nThe Service Provider shall, at the first progress meeting, present for discussion the findings of the visual survey\nthat will ultimately be included in the project assessment report. This meeting also serves the purpose of\naffording the Service Provider the opportunity to further demonstrate his understanding of the project and seek\napproval for proposed investigations and/or studies.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the presentation of the visual survey will be based on utilising the SARDS\nportal by Service Provider.\nThe Service Provider shall also present initial design strategy proposals at this meeting.\nC3.2.9 Market Analysis and Databases\nThe Service Provider shall undertake a market analysis in terms of the CIDB guidelines for undertaking a\nfeasibility study (CIDB grading, Central Supplier Database (CSD), RRM database, local/district municipality\ndatabase, consider to have open session with contractors from project area sharing project information, etc.)\nof availability and type of Targeted Enterprises for the Works Contract. The Service Provider shall further set\nup databases for Targeted Enterprises and Targeted Labour, assist local Targeted Enterprises to register on\nthe CSD and maintain and update these databases on a monthly basis. Provision for this service has been\nmade under Additional Duties.\nC3-35\n\nC3.2.10 Project Assessment Report\nA project assessment report shall be submitted for discussion at the progress meeting. The Service Provider\nshall, using the findings of the Initial Assessment and Visual Survey already conducted, identify problem areas\nand provide comprehensive evidence that their underlying cause is understood and propose appropriate\ncorrective or reparative remedies. These proposals will accompany others for multiple design methodologies\nthat are considered appropriate to the project including identification of their respective technical inputs. All\nproposals shall be discussed with the Employer. At least 3 (three) different methodologies must be proposed\nin the assessment report in cases where a Concept and/or Preliminary Design is not required so that the\nEmployer is presented with an array of design options from which to make decisions for progression to Detail\nDesign. The content of this report will depend on the available information and the scope of the work and may\ninclude the following aspects where applicable:\n• Document initial scope of works and the methods and procedures required to maintain the condition of\ninfrastructure\n• Establish the scale and cost plan for the project\n• Identify risks that need to be mitigated\n• Identification of additional studies, e.g. geotech, survey, traffic, etc.\n• X-section development strategy – 20 years\n• Intersection/interchange improvements\n• Appropriate Legislation\n• Identify stakeholders\n• Identify services\n• Existing pavement condition\n• Geotechnical stability\n• Road furniture condition and compliance\n• Confirm BMS assessment with recommendations\n• Economic Feasibility Analysis (where applicable)\n• Preliminary Land Report\n• Market Analysis and Databases\nAlthough all aspects are important, particular emphasis should be given to the materials making up the existing\npavement, their current reported condition and their respective sources.\nA separate Structures Assessment Report (which includes bridges, major culverts, lesser culverts, walls,\ngantries, light masts and buildings) must be submitted for new structures and existing structures. The report\nmust discuss the available information, describe each structure, report on the condition assessments\nundertaken and the need for any maintenance/rehabilitation of existing structures and describe the structural\nconstraints as a result of the envisage works.\nDuring the first part of the assessment and based purely on the information given and/or collected, the Service\nProvider must devise other feasible strategies for further discussion with the Employer.\nC3.2.11 Measurement and Payment\nItem Unit\n3.2.1 Initial Assessment\n(a) Initial Assessment lump sum (LS)\n(b) Disbursements lump sum (LS)\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.1(a) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum shall include full\ncompensation for all time related costs associated with undertaking the initial assessment.\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.1(b) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum tendered shall include\nfull compensation for all disbursements, materials, printing, travel (including toll fees) and subsistence and all\nother costs associated with the initial assessment.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.2.2 Visual Survey\nC3-36\n\n(a) Visual survey lump sum (LS)\n(b) Disbursements lump sum (LS)\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.2(a) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum shall include full\ncompensation for all time related costs associated with undertaking the visual survey.\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.2(b) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum tendered shall include\nfull compensation for all disbursements, materials, printing, travel (including toll fees) and subsistence and all\nother costs associated with the visual survey.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.2.3 Project Assessment Report\n(a) Project assessment report lump sum (LS)\n(b) Disbursements lump sum (LS)\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.3(a) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum shall include full\ncompensation for time related costs associated with the compilation of the project assessment report.\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.2.3(b) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum tendered shall include\nfull compensation for all disbursements, materials, printing and all other costs associated with the project\nassessment report.\nThe Service Provider shall be entitled to render interim monthly fee accounts based on progress for up to 70%\nof the lump sum. A further 20% of the lump sum is claimable upon delivery of the draft report. The remaining\n10% of the lump sum is claimable upon delivery of the final report.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with clause C2.1.6.\nC3-37\n\nC3.3 INVESTIGATIONS FOR DESIGN DEVELOPMENT\nC3.3.1 Scope\nThis section covers the investigations preceding any design development. This process requires investigations\nto be undertaken either in-house or through the appointment of relevant sub-Service Providers.\nC3.3.2 Investigations, Sampling and Testing\nIntrusive investigations are deemed to be any type of investigation that probes into or removes from the\npavement layers, sub-grades, structures’ elements and other areas, e.g. cuttings, etc.\nSupervision of intrusive investigations shall not be delegated, they shall be supervised and logged by the key\nperson listed as the relevant design specialist.\nProvision for investigations, sampling and testing has been allowed for in the Pricing Schedule.\nThe Service Provider shall submit for approval a schedule of proposed work for all field work, sampling and\ntesting prior to commencement.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format.\nC3.3.3 Survey Services\nAll survey requirements must be identified by the Service Provider as early as possible during the Project\nAssessment stage in order to ensure that there will be sufficient time to undertake these surveys. It is important\nthat the Service Provider familiarises himself with the requirements of TMH11: Standard Survey Methods in\norder to ensure that the survey deliverables will conform to the Service Provider’s requirements.\nThe Employer has a continuous standing agreement with a property Service Provider (PSP) who is tasked\nwith providing survey services when instructed. In view of the need for all survey controls used on the national\nroad network to be available in a single managed database the Employer requires that its PSP remains fully\nresponsible for checking and the approval of all survey controls used on all projects.\nUnless otherwise agreed the Service Provider is required to make use of the PSP for its survey needs. The\nService Provider is required to provide the type and extent of the survey and determine a reasonable time\nperiod for its production. A request for survey work form is included in Part C4, Appendix G for this purpose.\nThis request must be sent to the relevant Employer’s Regional Survey representative for approval.\na) Procurement of the survey sub-service\nThe Service Provider shall identify all potential survey requirements during the initial and visual assessment\nstages, including the survey of potential borrow pits and/or quarries. Discussion of purpose and extent of the\nsurvey shall be included in the agenda of the first progress meeting to which the PSP may be invited should it\nbe required.\nWhile interaction between the Service Provider and the PSP will be necessary, the survey tender documents,\nadvertising, evaluation, appointment of surveyor, payment of the surveyor, the survey project management\nand quality control shall be undertaken by the PSP. The Service Provider will agree to a program with the PSP,\nwho shall be responsible for the performance of their sub-Service Providers.\nHowever, if agreed that the Service Provider have to procure the survey services, a prime cost sum has been\nprovided in the Pricing Schedule. In such a case the latest standard survey proforma tender document, which\ncovers all survey types, will be made available by the PSP. Tenders shall be invited only from surveyors who\nare suitably registered in terms of the Geomatics Profession Act, Act 19 of 2013. The procurement of survey\nwork shall conform to the Employer’s procurement policies and procedures. The procedures as set out below\nshall apply after appointment of the survey contractor. The Service Provider shall then become responsible for\nthe performance of their sub-Service Providers.\nAll surveys are to be carried out to TMH11 standards as set out in the survey proforma tender document.\nC3-38\n\nb) Management of the survey sub-Service Provider\nWhenever the Service Provider procures the survey services, the following processes shall be applied:\n• Call for and evaluate regular progress reports and attend relevant progress meetings.\n• Attend to enquiries from the survey company.\n• It is a requirement for the survey sub-Service Provider to submit a survey verification report for existing\nbeacons prior to using their values. The verification entails the following:\n- Evaluate the verification survey methodology\n- Evaluate the verification residuals\n- Advise the survey sub-Service Provider to re-survey the existing control beacons or to adopt their\nvalues for the rest of the survey\n• Receive the newly established permanent survey control survey data and co-ordinates for approval prior\nto the acceptance of the values for any further surveys.\nThe approval of the co-ordinates will entail the following:\n- Evaluate survey methodology for height and position determination\n- Evaluate the reduction for height and position\n- Evaluate the survey datums and the connection to other control\nWhen receiving completed survey data from the survey sub-Service Provider the following processes listed\nbelow shall be followed:\n• Check that all deliverables have been submitted by the survey sub-Service Provider in terms of the\ntender requirements.\n• Submit the complete data set to the PSP for quality control.\n• Receive quality control reports from the PSP and forward it to the survey sub-Service Provider for\ncorrections. The date by when the corrections have to be completed must be advised by the Service\nProvider.\n• Re-submit the corrected data to PSP for verification. The entire survey has to be re-submitted each time\ncorrections have been undertaken in order for all previous data to be destroyed.\n• Make payment to the survey sub-Service Provider.\nC3.3.4 Traffic Monitoring Services\nAll traffic monitoring requirements must be identified by the Service Provider as early as possible during the\nProject Assessment stage in order to ensure that there will be sufficient time to undertake these surveys. It is\nimportant that the Service Provider familiarises himself with the requirements of TMH3: Specifications for the\nProvision of Traffic and Weigh-in-Motion Monitoring Services and TMH14: South African Standard Traffic Data\nCollection Format in order to ensure that the traffic monitoring deliverables will conform to the Service\nProvider’s requirements.\nThe Employer has a continuous standing agreement with a Traffic Monitoring Service Provider (TMSP) who is\ntasked with providing traffic monitoring services when instructed. In view of the need for all traffic monitoring\ndata used on the national road network to be available in a single managed database the Employer requires\nthat its TMSP remains fully responsible for checking and the approval of all traffic monitoring data used on all\nprojects.\nUnless otherwise agreed, the Service Provider is required to make use of the TMSP for its traffic monitoring\nneeds. The Service Provider is required to provide the type and extent of the survey and determine a\nreasonable time period for its production. A request for traffic monitoring should be submitted to the Employer\n(SANRAL Head Office – A Robinson – robinsona@nra.co.za).\na) Procurement of the traffic monitoring Service Provider (TMSP)\nThe Service Provider shall identify all potential traffic monitoring requirements during the initial and visual\nassessment stages. Discussion of purpose and extent of the survey shall be included in the agenda of the 1st\nprogress meeting.\nWhile interaction between the Service Provider and the TMSP will be necessary, the traffic monitoring tender\ndocuments, advertising, evaluation, appointment of the TMSP, payment of the TMSP, the project management\nand quality control shall be undertaken by the relevant SANRAL Head Office Project Manager (HO-PM)\nresponsible for the TMSP (M van der Walt – vdwaltm@nra.co.za). The Service Provider will agree to a program\nwith the HO-PM who shall be responsible for the performance of the TMSP.\nC3-39\n\nHowever, if agreed that the Service Provider have to procure the traffic monitoring services, a prime cost sum\nhas been provided in the Pricing Schedule. In such a case, the latest standard traffic monitoring proforma\ntender document, which covers all traffic monitoring types, will be made available by the Employer. Tender\nshall be invited only from traffic monitoring Service Providers who are suitable certified in terms of TMH3 for\nthe specific traffic monitoring system type required. The procurement of traffic monitoring work shall conform\nto the Employer’s procurement policies and procedures. The procedures as set out below shall apply after\nappointment of the TMSP. The Service Provider shall then become responsible for the performance of the\nTMSP.\nAll traffic monitoring is to be carried out to TMH3 and TMH14 standards as set out in the traffic monitoring\nproforma tender document.\nb) Management of the TMSP\nWhenever the Service Provider procures the traffic monitoring services, the following processes shall be\napplied:\n• Call for and evaluate regular progress reports and attend relevant progress meetings.\n• Attend to enquiries from the traffic monitoring company.\n• It is a requirement for the TMSP to submit a traffic monitoring report. The report entails the following:\n- Evaluate the survey methodology\n- Evaluate the positions for traffic monitoring\n- Advise the Service Provider to re-evaluate the proposed positions or advise on the suitability of\nthe traffic monitoring system to be used.\n- Take the necessary geocoded photographs to verify positions of traffic counts.\n- Compile the Station Information Report\n• Receive the traffic monitoring data for approval prior to the acceptance of the values for any further\nsurveys.\nWhen receiving completed traffic monitoring data from the TMSP the following processes listed below shall be\nfollowed:\n• Check that all deliverables have been submitted by the TMSP in terms of the tender requirements.\n• Submit the complete data set to the HO- PM for quality control.\n• Receive quality control reports from the HO-PM and forward it to the TMSP for corrections. The date by\nwhen the corrections have to be completed must be advised by the Service Provider.\n• Re-submit the corrected data to the HO-PM for verification. The entire survey has to be re-submitted\neach time corrections have been undertaken in order for all previous data to be destroyed.\n• Make payment to the TMSP.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the data from the traffic monitoring based on TMH 3 or 8 will need to be\nuploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format in TMH 14.\nC3.3.5 Environmental sub-service\nThe Service Provider shall identify all potential environmental issues relating to the envisaged scope of the\nWorks during the assessment stage, including areas where potential borrow pits and/or quarries may be\nlocated.\nThe environmental sub-Service Provider shall distinguish between the design and construction stages. The\nEmployer requires that the same sub-Service Provider fulfils the duties of the Environmental Assessment\nPractitioner (EAP) and later as the Environmental Control Officer (ECO), in accordance with the Mineral and\nPetroleum Resources Development Act (28 of 2002). The Service Provider shall use the Employer’s standard\ndocumentation for the procurement of environmental sub-services.\nSpecialist studies identified as being necessary do not form part of normal duties, and such services may have\nto be separately procured, but still in accordance with the procedures for the procurement of sub-Service\nProviders.\nC3.3.6 Geotechnical and Drilling Services\nThe Service Provider shall use the Employer’s standard documentation for the procurement of Geotechnical\nand Drilling sub-services. Once appointed, the contract will be carried out between the Geotechnical and\nDrilling sub-Service Provider and the Service Provider.\nC3-40\n\nSupervision of the sub-service work shall only be undertaken by suitably qualified and experienced personnel.\nDependant on the scope and/or scale of such sub-services, supervision may be required on a full time or part\ntime basis as determined by the Employer.\nThe logging of the cores and profiling of test pits are the Service Provider’s responsibility. All core logging and\ntest pit profiling are to be carried out, officially checked and signed by a suitably qualified and experienced\nengineering geologist as specified in Section 38 of the COLTO Standards and Specifications for Sub-service\nInvestigations (2010).\nThe Service Provider’s geotechnical engineer shall be responsible for reviewing the results of the investi-\ngations and confirming its acceptance. The geotechnical engineer will be involved in the planning, procurement\nand managing of the sub-contract. The geotechnical engineer will be responsible for the acceptance of data\ncollected during the investigation including amongst others, core logs, test pit profiles, laboratory test, etc. and\nthe production of a factual geotechnical report. In addition, the geotechnical engineer will be responsible for\nan interpretive geotechnical report as well as a design geotechnical report required for fulfilling the design. The\ngeotechnical engineer shall be involved during the construction phase in accordance with his/her obligation\nwith regard to professional responsibilities and Section C3.7.2(f) Site visits by Design Specialist(s).\nC3.3.7 Other Special Services and Specialist Advice\nThe Employer may require other special services and specialist advice which are not included in the normal\nduties. These may include:\n• road network planning studies\n• traffic, axle weight and transportation studies (including traffic counts only)\n• economic studies\n• model studies\n• soil mapping and seismic or similar surveys\n• monitoring of road and bridge elements\n• pavement surveillance surveys (e.g. FWD)\n• mediation, arbitration and litigation\n• land acquisition assistance provided by the PSP\nThe Service Provider shall, if and when required by the Employer to do so, procure such other specialist\nservices in accordance with the procedures for the procurement of sub-Service Providers. The Service\nProvider may tender for these services.\nIt is to be noted that, subject to the Employer’s approval, the land legal advice and property acquisition\nassistance provided to the Service Provider by the PSP will be paid under a separate contract administered\nby the Employer.\nIn the event of “specialist advice” the reimbursable costs shall be inclusive of the investigations and analysis\nto provide the Employer with an appropriate technical solution. In the case of “special services” the\nreimbursable cost is only for the specific survey, tests or data required and exclude the analysis thereof, the\ncost of which shall be included in the tendered rates for design.\nProvision for payment of other special services and specialist advice are provided in Section C3.8: Additional\nDuties. Where investigations are to be undertaken the Service Provider shall compile a schedule of required\nwork, which shall be subject to the approval of the Employer, before any investigation may commence.\nC3.3.8 Specialist Transportation Engineering Service\nFor projects requiring basic analyses of traffic volumes and trends, or capacity improvements involving\ngeometric designs that are basically extensions to existing road infrastructure, a design specialist will not be\nspecified as part of the key persons. However, for more complex green-fields projects, a design specialist may\nbe specified as part of the key persons. In both cases the cost for this service shall be deemed to be included\nin the rates tendered for design.\nC3.3.9 Specialist Geometric Design Service\nFor projects requiring basic geometric analyses of traffic volumes and trends, or capacity improvements\ninvolving geometric designs that are basically extensions to existing road infrastructure, a design specialist will\nnot be specified as part of the key persons. However, for more complex projects, a design specialist will be\nspecified as part of the key persons. In both cases the cost for this service shall be deemed to be included in\nthe rates tendered for design.\nC3-41\n\nC3.3.10 Materials Investigation and Testing\na) Test pits\nAll test pits shall be excavated, profiled and sampled in accordance with the M1 Manual and industry best\npractice. The dimensions of the excavation shall be sufficient to permit safe working conditions and to allow\nfor adequate quantities of material to be sampled from each horizon for the envisaged testing. All test pits shall\nbe excavated to a minimum depth of 800mm, or deeper if so specified by the design engineer.\nAll test pits shall be backfilled utilizing material which is at least of a similar quality as that removed from the\nrelevant horizon. The upper 150mm horizon shall consist of at least G2 quality material stabilized with 2%\nemulsion and 1% cement. The surfacing shall consist of hot/cold asphalt or prefabricated seal patches as\nappropriate to the existing surfacing and traffic/road class. Compaction shall be by appropriate compaction\nequipment in layers not exceeding 150mm.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nb) Dynamic Cone Penetration (DCP) testing\nDynamic Cone Penetration (DCP) testing shall be carried out in accordance with the standard test method.\nAppropriate drilling equipment shall be utilized to penetrate asphalt, highly stabilized or cemented layers prior\nto proceeding with the testing. Results of DCP testing shall be produced in a format enabling full assessment\nof the various pavement layers to be assessed.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nc) Coring in pavement layers\nWhere required and scheduled, coring shall be carried out utilizing appropriate equipment. The layer type and\ndiameter of the cores shall be as specified in the pricing schedule.\nd) Transport of samples to laboratory\nAll samples shall be securely fastened, labelled and transported to the laboratory appointed to carry out the\nrequired testing. Distinction has been made in the Pricing Schedule between large bags and small bags/cores.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\ne) Laboratory testing\nOnly SANAS accredited laboratories shall be permitted to carry out laboratory testing of materials. All testing\nshall be carried out in accordance with the methods specified in the relevant test methods.\nProvision for the costs of any laboratory testing has been included under specific tests and/or under a\nProvisional Sum item in the Pricing Schedule.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nC3-42\n\nC3.3.11 Measurement and Payment\nItem Unit\n3.3.1 Survey use of SANRAL’s Property lump sum (LS)\nService Providers\nThe unit of measurement under sub-item 3.3.1(b) and (c) shall be the lump sum.\nThe cost under sub item 3.3.1(b) shall only be applicable
