cost (PC)\n(ii) ---- Handling cost for planning, procuring lump sum (LS)\ncontracting services for geotechnical and drilling\nworks i.r.o item 3.3.4(e)(i)\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.3.4(b) shall be the lump sum. The Service Provider shall identify the\nfull scope of the sub-service investigation(s), type and estimated quantity of tests required and shall compile\nquotation/tender documents in accordance with the Employer’s standard requirements, procurement policy\nand industry best practices for procuring the relevant sub-service. The lump sum tendered shall include full\ncompensation for personnel, transport, accommodation, materials, printing and all other costs associated with\nthe compilation of quotation/tender documents, issuing quotation/tender documents, clarification meetings,\nevaluation and producing a tender evaluation report.\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.3.4(c) shall be month or part thereof and the rate tendered shall include\nfull compensation for the engineer’s cost including all subsistence, travel, accommodation, meeting attendance\nand other costs associated with administration and monitoring of the geotechnical and/or drilling investigation,\nincluding preparation and submission of contractor’s payment certificates.\nThe provisional sum under sub-item 3.3.4(d)(i) shall cover all monthly costs for personnel including all\nsubsistence and travel costs associated with providing full time supervision of the work.\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.3.4(d)(ii) shall be month or part thereof and the rate tendered shall\ninclude full compensation for all personnel costs including all subsistence and travel costs associated with\nproviding an Engineering Geologist for the logging of cores.\nThe provisional sum under sub-item 3.3.4(d)(iii) shall cover all costs associated with the accommodation for\nfull time supervision personnel and the Engineering Geologist.\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.3.4(d)(iv) shall be month or part thereof and the tendered rate shall\ninclude full compensation for all costs, including personnel, transport, accommodation and subsistence for the\nOccupational Health and Safety Specialist for fulfilling the Service Provider’s obligations as the Agent with\nC3-44\n\nrespect to the requirements of the Construction Regulations of the Occupational Health and Safety Act (Act\n85 of 1993).\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.3.4(e)(ii) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum tendered shall include\nfull compensation for personnel, transport, accommodation, materials, printing and all other costs associated\nwith the compilation of quotation/tender documents, issuing quotation/tender documents, clarification meeting,\nevaluation and producing a tender evaluation report.\nThe Prime Cost item shall be paid in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-items 3.3.4(b), 3.3.4(c), 3.3.4(d)(ii), 3.3.4(d)(iv) and\n3.3.4(e)(ii) in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.3.5 Pavement Investigation and Sampling\n(a) Establishment of personnel and equipment for lump sum (LS)\ntest pitting\n(b) Pavement test pit, sampling & profiling number\n(c) Dynamic Cone Penetration (DCP) number\n(d) Cores in asphalt layers (100/150mm dia) number\n(e) Cores in concrete layers (100/150mm dia) number\n(f) Transport of samples to laboratory:\n(i) Large bags number\n(ii) Small bags/cores number\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.5(a) shall be the lump sum. The lump sum tendered shall cover\nall costs associated in establishing an appropriately sized team on site to carry out the scheduled test pit\nprogramme.\nThe rate tendered shall be fixed irrespective of the final number of test pits excavated.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.5(b) shall be the number of test pits excavated, sampled and\nprofiled/photographed by the relevant Design Specialist. With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider\nwill be required to follow the Standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of testing\nrequired. The data shall be uploaded into the SARDS portal by the Service Provider as per the defined standard\nformat or SARDS interface.\nThe rate tendered shall include for the safeguarding of the test pits whilst open, all costs including travel,\nlabour, accommodation, subsistence, plant, materials, sample bags, etc. associated with the excavation,\nprofiling and sampling of test pits and subsequent backfilling and compaction thereof as specified.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.5(c) shall be the number of DCP tests at locations identified by\nthe relevant Design Specialist. With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow\nthe Standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data shall\nbe uploaded into the SARDS portal by the Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS\ninterface.\nThe rate tendered shall include for drilling through any “refusal” layers, completing the test and submission of\nthe DCP analysis to the relevant Design Specialist.\nThe unit of measurement under pay items 3.3.5(d) and (e) shall be the number of cores in asphalt and/or\nconcrete at locations identified by the relevant Design Specialist irrespective of the thickness of the core. With\nthe release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the Standard workflow within SARDS\nto determine the number and locality of testing required. The data shall be uploaded into the SARDS portal by\nthe Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nThe rates tendered shall include for all costs including labour, transport, coring rig, backfilling of core holes,\naccommodation, subsistence, etc. The rates tendered shall be fixed, irrespective of the length of the core\nextracted.\nC3-45\n\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.5(f) shall be the number of large bags and/or small bags/cores\ntransported.\nThe rates tendered shall include for all costs to transport the bags/cores to the laboratory selected to carry out\nthe testing, irrespective of distance.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.3.6 Laboratory Testing and Reporting\n(a) Standard tests as listed in the Pricing Schedule number\n(b) Laboratory Testing where no schedule is\navailable, or specialised testing prime cost (PC)\n(c) Handling costs i.r.o. pay item 3.3.6(b) percentage (%)\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.6(a) shall be the number of tests prescribed by the relevant\nDesign Specialist and carried out by the laboratory.\nThe rates tendered shall cover all costs associated in completing the individual tests and shall include for all\ncalculations and reporting. Any tests proven to be erroneous shall not be paid for.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.3.6(b) shall be a Prime Cost Sum. The Service Provider shall\nidentify the type and estimated quantity of tests required and shall compile quotation document in accordance\nwith the Employer’s standard requirements/policy for the purpose of procuring the relevant service.\nThe Prime Cost item shall be paid in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nThe percentage tendered under pay item 3.3.6(c) is a percentage of the amount actually spent under payment\nitem 3.3.6(b) and shall include full compensation for all costs of the Service Provider as specified.\nWhere the prior identification of materials testing requirements is not possible, a Provisional sum has been\nincluded in the Pricing Schedule for the procurement of such services. Where testing is carried out under a\nProvisional Sum the percentage handling fee tendered in the Pricing Schedule shall include for all the Service\nProvider’s costs associated with:\n- compiling a schedule of all envisaged work\n- production of a document for procurement purposes (including printing etc.)\n- calling for quotations/tenders\n- evaluation of quotations/tenders received and recommendations to the Employer\n- handling fees\n- profit\nProcurement of such services shall be in accordance with clause C3.1.12.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-item 3.3.6(a) in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nC3-46\n\nC3.4 DESIGN DEVELOPMENT STAGE\nC3.4.1 Scope\nThis section covers the requirements for the compilation and submission of a Concept and/or Preliminary\nand/or Detailed Design and the various phases that the Service Provider may have to apply so as to develop\nthe design in such a way that the Employer is provided the opportunity to select the most appropriate design\nstrategy.\nIt is a fundamental requirement that the Service Provider has a thorough working knowledge of the Employer’s\ncomplete suite of design standards, codes, manuals and guidelines (i.e. those that it publishes and those for\nwhich it provides an industry custodianship), which shall be used in the production of the designs in all their\nseparate phases. In addition, the Service Provider shall apply any other industry best practice publication that\nmay be appropriate. Furthermore, the Employer’s philosophy of appropriate standards and cost effectiveness\nshall always be considered.\nC3.4.2 Design Development Strategy\nOn completion of the project assessment stage and before findings from further investigations and sub-ser-\nvices are available the Service Provider shall discuss with the Employer and confirm the design development\nstrategy that will be applied.\nNew work (i.e. predominantly ‘greenfield’ projects that require route determination and/or new structures for\nwhich aesthetics and service are not fixed) may require development through separate multiple phases of\nconcept, preliminary and detailed designs.\nMany capital expenditure projects that require capacity improvements in addition to rehabilitation and or repair\nmay require development through the separate multiple phases of preliminary and detailed designs.\nSome capital expenditure projects, and most operational expenditure projects, will only require the single\nphase of detailed design development.\nThe purpose of confirming the total strategy to be applied is based on the precept that public funds must be\nwisely expended and it may occur that the details emerging from the project assessment dictate that\ndevelopment through multiple phases is not required. Any decision not to proceed with either the conceptual\nor preliminary phases shall not give cause for a Service Provider to request or demand an adjustment to its\npricing structure. Each development phase is distinctly separate and thus shall be priced in such a way that\nno cross-subsidisation between them occurs.\nConversely, any decision to introduce an additional development phase shall be subject to pricing variation.\nC3.4.3 Investigations, Sampling and Testing\nIntrusive investigations are deemed to be any type of investigation that probes into or removes from the\nroadbed, borrow pits, quarries, structure founding materials and other areas, e.g. cuttings, etc. Investigations,\nboth intrusive and non-destructive, sampling as well as associated laboratory testing, must be approved by\nthe Employer before they are undertaken.\nThe Service Provider shall initially identify and then determine the viability of sources for the supply of materials\nby arranging for test pits, samples, laboratory testing and reporting on them. The investigations may involve\ndrilling, both by percussion and core recovery if needed to prove new sites or extensions to existing pits.\nDepending on the locality of the project site, the investigation of materials sources shall include the use of\ncommercial sources as well as existing/new sources on, alongside or near the project site. This will require\nsampling and testing of the commercial products. When the project demands crushed stone products the\ninvestigation of more than one source shall be required. Where intrusive investigations are required to be\ncarried out on land not under the current control and/or jurisdiction of the Employer, suitable arrangements\nmust be made with the Land Owner, Authority etc. In this regard the Service Provider must refer to the LAC\nManual and in particular to those sections dealing with access onto land.\nSupervision of intrusive investigations shall not be delegated, they shall be supervised and logged by the key\nperson listed as the relevant design specialist.\nProvision for investigations, sampling and testing has been allowed for in the Pricing Schedule.\nC3-47\n\nThe Service Provider shall submit for approval a schedule of proposed work for all field work, sampling and\ntesting prior to commencement.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nC3.4.4 Materials Investigation and Testing\na) Test pits\nAll test pits shall be excavated, profiled and sampled in accordance with the M1 Manual and industry best\npractice. The dimensions of the excavation shall be sufficient to permit safe working conditions and to allow\nfor adequate quantities of material to be sampled from each horizon for the envisaged testing. All test pits shall\nbe excavated to a minimum depth of 800mm, or deeper if so specified by the Design Specialist – Pavements\n& Materials.\nAll test pits shall be backfilled utilizing excess material excavated and additional material to ensure that there\nwill be no unsafe depressions. The backfill shall be compacted sufficiently to ensure that no significant settle-\nment occurs subsequent to completion of the investigation.\nAll test pits that are required to be left open and unattended shall be secured by means of appropriate fencing\nand danger tape.\nAt the commencement of excavation, all topsoil shall be stockpiled separately and replaced after completion\nof the backfilling operation.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nb) Dynamic Cone Penetration (DCP) testing\nDynamic Cone Penetration (DCP) testing shall be carried out in accordance with the standard test method.\nResults of DCP testing shall be produced in a format enabling full assessment of the various pavement layers\nto be assessed.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nc) Hire of excavator\nWhere an excavator is required to excavate the test pits, the Service Provider shall determine the capacity of\nsuch plant appropriate to the expected material category and depth of excavation.\nThe excavation of test pits by mechanical means shall be done in such a manner so as to prevent any\ncontamination of the specific soil/gravel horizons encountered.\nd) Transport of samples to laboratory\nAll samples shall be securely fastened, labelled and transported to the laboratory appointed to carry out the\nrequired testing. Distinction has been made in the Pricing Schedule between large bags and small bags/cores.\ne) Laboratory testing\nOnly SANAS accredited laboratories shall be permitted to carry out laboratory testing of materials. All testing\nshall be carried out in accordance with the methods specified in the relevant test methods.\nProvision for the costs of any laboratory testing has been included under specific tests and/or under a Pro-\nvisional Sum item in the Pricing Schedule.\nC3-48\n\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will need to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface.\nC3.4.5 Concept Design\nThis development phase requires the Service Provider to produce multiple concepts (not less than three) for\nconsideration. Each concept shall be complete in its context with the purpose of the project (e.g. structure or\nalignment) within which there may be further smaller refinements. Refinements of a single concept are not\nconsidered to be multiple concepts.\nThe duties to be performed include but are not limited to the following:\ni. Provide a programme for documentation and for implementation of the works;\nii. Include a baseline risk assessment and a health and safety specification;\niii. Include an operations and maintenance support plan over the service life;\niv. Establish the feasibility of satisfying the original scope of works within the original budget, and if not,\nmotivate a revised scope and/or budget;\nv. Consultation with the Employer or its authorised representatives;\nvi. Inspection of the site of the Works;\nvii. Identify and discuss with the Employer all the technical inputs which will be researched and applied.\nviii. Consideration of alternatives and advising the Employer regarding advantages and disadvantages\nincluding reference to land use and acquisition feasibilities as required by the Employer. In order to\nassist the Service Provider with the Land Use and Land Acquisition Feasibilities, the Employer may\nrequest the PSP to conduct a Land Audit on the multiple routes. This will generally be determined by\nthe complexities evident from the project assessment conducted by the Service Provider and/or from\nthe initial documentation provided by the Employer to the Service Provider;\nix. Preliminary investigation, route location and design where any of these are required for determination\nof feasibility. Included in these can be traffic (including non-motorised transport and pedestrians)\ndensities, road geometry including cross-sections and longitudinal sections, drainage needs, materials\navailability, pavement requirements, structural requirements. Also establish access, utilities, services\nand connections required for the design; With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be\nrequired to follow the standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of testing\nrequired. The data will need to be uploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined\nstandard format or SARDS interface and then analysed using SARDS.\nx. Consultation with authorities having rights or powers of sanction as well as consultation with the public\nand stakeholder groups;\nxi. Advice to the Employer as to the need for surveys of any kind, analysis, tests and site or other investi-\ngations where such are to be carried out at the Employer’s expense including a risk report associated\nwith the need;\nxii. Searching for, obtaining, investigation and collation of available data, drawings and plans relating to the\nWorks;\nxiii. Investigations, as may be required, of economical and financial implications in relation to the proposals\nor feasibility studies and the confirmation of the financial sustainability thereof;\nxiv. Advice to the Employer as to regulatory and statutory requirements including environmental issues and\nmanagement;\nxv. Produce drawings depicting the designs, particularly those for route alignments. However, they need\nnot contain full design layout details; but must show the basic road geometry and cross-drainage. Full\nstructural drawings are not necessary; it shall show the basic layouts accompanied by loading\ncalculations indicating the type of foundation necessary.\nxvi. Compile a Concept Design report to be submitted at a progress meeting. The report shall, as a minimum,\ncover the aspects listed under the duties above.\nxvii. In terms of structures, the reporting shall be in terms of Clause 13.1 of the Code of Procedure for the\nPlanning and Design of Highway and Road Structures in South Africa, where structures are evaluated\nmainly with respect to road alignment issues and land matters, amongst other constraints.\nxviii. Preparing, reporting and presenting the alternatives to the Employer’s Regional Gateway Review\nCommittee for project cost higher than R100 million.\nxix. Preparing, reporting and presenting the alternatives to the Employer’s National Gateway Review\nCommittee for project cost higher than the prescribed National Treasury threshold currently at R500\nmillion\nC3-49\n\nC3.4.6 Preliminary Design\nThe underlying purpose of preliminary design is to develop more than one option for presentation to the\nEmployer for selection of that which is the most appropriate based on technical functionality and economic\nbenefit. The selected option(s) will then be further developed in the detailed design phase.\nOn projects that commence with this design phase, the Service Provider shall, using the findings of the project\nassessment and investigations or concept design already conducted, identify problem areas, provide\ncomprehensive evidence that their underlying cause is understood and propose appropriate corrective or\nreparative remedies. These proposals will accompany others for multiple design methodologies that are\nconsidered appropriate to the project including identification of their respective technical inputs. All proposals\nshall be discussed with the Employer. At least 3 (three) different methodologies must be proposed not all of\nwhich need to include identified remedies. Proposed methodologies should separately deal with elements\nrelating to capacity improvements so that these may be individually developed together with the methodologies\nso that the Employer is presented with an array of design options from which to make decisions for progression\nto detailed design.\nThe duties to be performed include but are not limited to the following:\ni. Provide a programme for documentation and implementation of the works;\nii. Include a baseline risk assessment and a health and safety specification;\niii. Include an operations and maintenance support plan over the service life;\niv. Establish the feasibility of satisfying the original scope of works within the original budget, and if not,\nmotivate a revised scope and/or budget;\nv. Include a technology (alternatives) options assessment;\nvi. Include a regulatory due diligence (compliance with various regulations and required statutory\npermissions impacting this project);\nvii. Advising the Employer as to the need for any further surveys of any kind, analysis, tests and site or\nother investigations which may be required, and arranging for these to be carried out at the Employer’s\nexpense including a risk assessment and sensitivity analysis associated with the need;\nviii. Advising the Employer regarding environmental requirements and management plans and attending to\nthe same;\nix. Advising the Employer, as may be necessary, upon the appointment of and brief to other professional\nengineers, architects and specialist advisers and arranging such appointments and consultations with\nsuch advisers in matters affecting the works;\nx. Preparation of preliminary design plans and other drawings in accordance with the Employer’s current\nCodes, Manuals and Guidelines necessary for seeking approval of statutory authorities and beaconing\nof the road reserve; With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the\nstandard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required. The data will\nneed to be uploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or\nSARDS interface and then analysed using SARDS.\nxi. Consultation on technical matters with authorities and interested and affected parties;\nxii. Making modifications to the preliminary design of the works in connection with or dictated by the\nconsultations aforesaid;\nxiii. Identification and reporting on services and owners as well as relocations/protection measures required;\nxiv. Submission of estimates of capital and life cycle costs and economic and financial implications in relation\nto the proposals and the confirmation of the financial sustainability thereof;\nxv. Conduct a Land Audit as per the LAC Manual of each Route under consideration. In this regard the\nService Provider must produce a (high level) property report for each such Route being investigated\nincluding the land required for material sources. This report must identify a the very least the following:\n* property type\n* ownership type\n* title type\n* informal rights\n* informal settlements\n* mining rights\n* real rights (servitudes, etc.)\n* jurisdiction of affected non-National Roads\n* Traditional (Tribal) Authorities and their jurisdiction\n* Municipal Authorities\n* affected streets and public places\n* proclaimed forestry, conservation, irrigation, etc. areas.\nIn addition, the Service Provider must undertake a ‘high level’ Land Acquisition cost analysis for each\nRoute being investigated inclusive of the land required for material sources.\nC3-50\n\nThe Service Provider shall at this stage ensure that relevant one-on-one consultations with affected land\nowners are conducted including the holders of formal, informal rights and all other rights as defined\nabove. Where land is in the ownership of the State (Municipal, Provincial or National) or other State\nAuthorities, then the Service Provider shall also attend on such authorities in order to give notice of the\nintention to acquire portions of or the whole of such properties. The impact of the routes on such rights\nmust be clearly documented in the report.\nIn regard to all of the above, the Employer may instruct the PSP to assist the Service Provider if it\nbecomes evident that there are complexities that would require specialist input.\nThe Employer must be advised immediately if there is evidence of unlawful occupation of land along the\nproposed Routes and on land earmarked for material sources; (\nxvi. Compilation and submission of a Preliminary Design Report and if required a Preliminary Materials\nReport.\nxvii. The requirements for structures under the Preliminary Design Phase will be split into 2 (two) stages:\n• The first stage shall comprise submission of a separate Basic Planning Structures Report (which\nincludes bridges, major culverts, lesser culverts, walls, gantries, light masts and buildings) for all\nnew and existing structures in terms of Clause 13.2 of the Code of Procedure for the Planning and\nDesign of Highway and Road Structures in South Africa. The first stage report shall be, in essence,\na road planning report in which structural issues as envisaged are addressed. This stage shall\ninclude bridge width schedules submitted for approval and is submitted in conjunction with the\nsubmission of the overall Preliminary Design Report.\n• The second stage shall comprise submission of a separate structures report (which includes\nbridges, major culverts, lesser culverts, walls, gantries, light masts and buildings) for all new and\nexisting structures in terms of Clause 13.3 of the Code of Procedure for the Planning and Design of\nHighway and Road Structures in South Africa. In the case of bridges, a separate report shall be\nsubmitted per bridge. This report shall investigate structural solutions in terms of environmental and\nsite conditions, geometric constraints, structural requirements, financial aspects and those\nconsiderations that have a bearing on the feasibility of the postulated solutions. These submissions\nshall take place after approval of the Preliminary Design Report.\nxviii. Separate Factual, Interpretive and Geotechnical Design Reports must be submitted where geotechnical\ninvestigations are required.\nxix. A separate Drainage Report will be needed for all River Bridges, Major Culverts and Lesser Culverts on\nthe project and shall be submitted before the Structures Report as described above. The purpose of this\nreport is to assess, in terms of the latest version of the SANRAL Drainage Manual, the hydraulic\ncapacities of all drainage structures for the project.\nxx. Preparing, reporting and presenting the alternatives to the Employer’s Regional Gateway Review\nCommittee for project costs higher than R100 million.\nxxi. Preparing, reporting and presenting the alternatives to the Employer’s National Gateway Review\nCommittee for project costs higher than the prescribed National Treasury threshold currently at R 500\nmillion.\nUnless the project terminates at the preliminary design stage, drawings produced to depict the various\nelements of the design methodologies need not follow the exacting standards for final drawings. However,\nthey shall be comprehensive in their detail so that they accurately reflect the design outcomes. The size of\ndrawings is similarly not prescriptive. Structural drawings shall comply with the standards set in the Employer’s\npublication Code of Procedures for the Planning and Design of Highway and Road Structures in South Africa.\nNotwithstanding the aforesaid, sketches and hand-drawn depictions will not be accepted; they shall be\nprofessionally drawn.\nC3.4.7 Detailed Design\nThe Employer will select from the outcome of the project assessment stage or preliminary design phase, the\ndesign option and other possible design elements that are to be developed to detailed design.\nThe duties to be performed include but are not limited to the following:\ni. Provide a programme for documentation and implementation of the works;\nii. Include a baseline risk assessment and a health and safety specification;\niii. Include an operations and maintenance support plan over the service life;\niv. Establish the feasibility of satisfying the original scope of works within the original budget, and if not,\nmotivate a revised scope and/or budget;\nv. Include a technology (alternatives) options assessment;\nvi. Include a regulatory due diligence (compliance with various regulations and required statutory\npermissions impacting this project);\nvii. Advise the Employer as to the necessity for setting out or staking out the works, establishment of\nconstruction beacons;\nC3-51\n\nviii. Advise the Employer as to the need for any further surveys of any kind, analysis, tests or other investi-\ngations which may be required and arranging for these to be carried out including a risk assessment\nand sensitivity analysis associated with the need;\nix. Advise the Employer regarding environmental requirements and management and attending to same\nas agreed with the Employer;\nx. Preparation of designs inclusive of all calculations, drawings, project specifications and Engineering\nschedules of quantities in accordance with the requirements of the Employer’s current appropriate\nCodes, Manuals and Guidelines; including labour maximisation/optimisation in all phase of the design\nas per COTO specifications. With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to\nfollow the standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of testing required.\nThe data will need to be uploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard\nformat or SARDS interface and then analysed using SARDS:\nxi. Estimates of the cost of the works, using the unit rates of the Employer’s database if applicable;\nxii. Identify and advise the Employer on communication channels, employment forums and issues impacting\non the design;\nxiii. The timeous arrangement for the relocation of any services which may disrupt the construction\nprogramme. This may include relocation of services prior to construction stage;\nxiv. Conduct a Detailed Land Audit as per the LAC Manual of the entire project area.\nThe Employer may instruct the PSP to assist the Service Provider if it becomes evident that there are\ncomplexities that require specialist input\nIn regard to this land audit, the Service Provider must produce a detailed report for each property along\nthe route and all land required for material sources. This report must identify, at the very least, the\nfollowing\n* property type\n* ownership type\n* title type\n* informal rights\n* informal settlements / illegal occupation of land\n* mining rights\n* real rights (servitudes, etc.)\n* jurisdiction of affected non-National Roads\n* Traditional (Tribal) Authorities and their jurisdiction\n* Municipal Authorities\n* affected streets and public places and where such streets and public places may be impacted on\nand/or closed\n* proclaimed forestry, conservation, irrigation, etc. areas\nThe Service Provider shall, at this stage, then also conduct one-on-one consultations with each land\nowner affected by the project and with the holders of formal and informal rights and indeed all other\nrights as defined above. Where land is in the ownership of the State (Municipal, Provincial or National)\nor other State Authorities, then the Service Provider shall accompany the PSP when meeting with such\nauthorities in order to deal with aspects raised by such authorities and where such aspects are of a\nTechnical/Design nature. In this regard, careful reference must be made to the formalities as described\nin the LAC Manual.\nIt is emphasised that the purpose of this interaction is to reach consensus with each land owner so\naffected with regards to the impact that the land acquisition will have on his property and to all of his\nentitlements and to clearly determine the conditions under which the land owner will be willing to sell the\nland required.\nThis is expanded on further in this document below. In this regard, the Employer must be advised\nimmediately if there is evidence of unlawful occupation of land along the proposed route and on land\nearmarked for material sources.\nThe revised road reserve must be determined and have the Employer’s approval.\nThe Service Provider shall then compile a detailed Property Report as defined in the LAC Manual and\ntransmit same to the Employer’s Project Manager and to the relevant Regional Survey Representative\nfor their consideration and formal approval. Only on approval of the Detailed Property Report will be\nsame be transmitted to the PSP who will then commence with the preparation and approval of Land\nAcquisition Diagrams and the necessary amended Declarations relating to the final road reserve;\nxv. Submit the necessary design documentation to local and other authorities for approval, if required;\nxvi. Consultation on and incorporation of, where applicable, technical matters with authorities and interested\nand affected parties;\nxvii. Submission of separate structures report(s) (which includes bridges, major culverts, lesser culverts,\nwalls, gantries, light masts and buildings) for all new and existing structures in terms of Clause 13.3 of\nthe SANRAL Code of Procedure for the Planning and Design of Highway and Road Structures in South\nAfrica. In the case of bridges, a separate report shall be submitted per bridge. This report shall\ninvestigate structural solutions in terms of environmental and site conditions, geometric constraints,\nC3-52\n\nstructural requirements, financial aspects and those considerations that have a bearing on the feasibility\nof the postulated solutions. These submissions shall take place after approval of the Preliminary Design\nReport and Bridge/Culvert Schedules which occurs at the commencement of Detailed Design.\nCompilation and submission of a Detailed Design Report and if required a Materials Report;\nIf the project requires only a Detailed Design Phase and a preliminary design on structures was\npreviously conducted, the Service Provider must confirm the findings on the Preliminary Design and re-\nsubmit a structures report if changes are required. The selected recommended option is then to be\napproved by the Employer’s Bridge Network Manager before any detail design is commenced.\nxviii. Submission of Economic Feasibility Analysis (where required) and confirm the financial sustainability of\nthe project.\nxix. Undertake a market analysis in terms of the CIDB guidelines for undertaking a feasibility study (CIDB\ngrading, Central Supplier Database, etc. of availability and type of Targeted Enterprises for the Works\nContract.\nIf the project requires only the detail design phase the Service Provider shall, using the findings of the project\nassessment and investigations or designs already conducted, identify problem areas, provide comprehensive\nevidence that their underlying cause is understood and propose appropriate corrective or reparative remedies.\nThe Service Provider shall also identify which elements of the aforesaid publications will be applied and discuss\nthem with the Employer before proceeding with development of the design to finality. Where a Preliminary\nDesign on structures was previously conducted, the Service Provider must confirm the findings on the\nPreliminary Design and re-submit a Structures Report if changes are proposed. The selected recommended\noption is then to be approved by the Employer’s Bridge Network Manager before any detail design is\ncommenced.\nDetail design requires the drafting of a final project specification that will be incorporated into a full tender\ndocument adopted from the Employer’s proforma tender document. A full set of drawings complying with the\nEmployer’s latest drawing standards shall be presented for signature. The Service Provider shall be aware of\nthe Employer’s procedures for iterative presentations of drawings for approval, signature, archiving and\namendment to include the as-built record. The process is also available on the Employer’s website\nwww.nra.co.za.\nGuidelines for drawings are contained in TMH11: Standard Survey Methods (latest version) published by the\nCommittee of Transport Offices (COTO). This reference contains all the relevant information relating to grid\nintervals, topographical detailing, symbols, colours, weight, and letter sizes etc and acts as the standard to be\nused for the production of drawings for use by a contractor.\nC3.4.8 OHS Requirements\nThe Service Provider shall at all stages of the design development identify elements of the design that are\ninherently dangerous or hazardous during the construction phase and design in such a manner as to mitigate\nor eliminate the risk where possible. At the culmination of each development phase all identified\ndangers/hazards shall be listed and brought to the attention of the Employer and agreed hazards shall be\nemphasised and given prominence by way of notification on the drawings and separately listed in the\nrespective phase-end reports. They shall appear in the drawings and the tender document for issue to a\ncontractor.\nFor example, staging for bridge decks or shoring of unstable excavations is the designer’s responsibility to\nidentify and notify of the health and safety risk.\nC3.4.9 Environmental Requirements\nThroughout the development of the project design the Service Provider shall liaise closely with the appointed\nEAP as he/she performs the environmental subservice. It is desirable that the EAP accompanies the Service\nProvider to the regular progress meetings to ensure accurate reporting on the state of the application for\nenvironmental approvals. The compilation of the various Environmental Management Programmes (EMPrs)\nthat the respective relevant authorities require shall be modelled on the Employer’s standard Environmental\nManagement Plan with as little deviation from it as possible. Any adaptation or change shall be reported to the\nEmployer for approval and later incorporation into the contract document.\nAny cause derived from these EMPrs that requires incorporation into the design shall similarly be reported to\nthe Employer for approval.\nC3.4.10 Land Requirements\nC3-53\n\na) Property Report\nA comprehensive and detailed Property Report shall be compiled in accordance with the Employer’s standard\nrequirements. This Property Report shall deal with all aspects relating to each individual portion of land\nrequired for the project including all material resources and it is emphasised that the procedures as contained\nin the Land Acquisition Guideline Manual for Consulting Engineers, which is available on the Employer’s web\npage (www.nra.co.za), shall be followed without exception.\nWith reference to all ‘material sources’ and where a “Borrow Pit” and/or “Quarry” site is identified for acquisition\n(permanent or temporary) then such site shall be named with reference to its kilometre distance of the road\ndesign and pre-fixed with a “L” or “R” e.g. Borrow Pit L29.100.\nThe Property Report will be considered by the Employer’s Project Manager and the Regional Survey\nRepresentative. Should the Property Report be found to be incomplete and/or contain erroneous information,\nit will be returned to the Service Provider for his immediate attention and action. Once the Property Report has\nbeen approved by the Employer, it will be sent to the PSP who will conduct checks to discover any errors or\nomissions.\nAlthough the PSP performs checks on property reports, the responsibility for the completeness and accuracy\nof property reports lies with the Service Provider. The PSP is able to identify errors in property reports only\ninsofar as they are reasonably discoverable and will report any such errors found to the Employer’s Project\nManager and/or Regional Survey Representative as soon as any may be found. The Employer reserves its\nright to recover the costs of any claims for delays suffered by the contractor in getting access to Site or to\nportions of the Site that are attributable to the erroneous property reports submitted by the Service Provider. It\nmust be noted that the 9 (nine) month period required to complete the Land Acquisition process to the point\nwhere “Site Access” has been achieved cannot commence until a Property Report is complete and correct in\nall respects. Any delay in correctly completing a Property Report will delay the commencement of the\nacquisition process and very often, also delay access to site. Late changes in design have exactly the same\neffect as submitting incomplete or erroneous Property Reports.\nThe Service Provider must carefully consider the relevant sections in the LAC Manual that deal specifically\nwith Tribal Land and determine if any land is subject to the authority of a Tribal (Traditional) Authority at the\nearliest possible time in the Design Process (if this has not already been determined under a previous phase).\nIndividuals hold informal and community rights over Tribal Land. Consequently, the Service Provider must\nensure that he is fully conversant with the Interim Protection of Informal Land Rights Act, Act 31 of 1996 and\nthe procedures contained therein. These rights are not registered and recorded in a Deeds Office. On-site\nmeetings must therefore be held with the affected communities and their leadership in order to determine the\nextent and nature of their rights. The exact impact that the project will have on their rights must be clearly\ndetermined in community meetings in order to avoid conflict at the time that the PSP undertakes the acquisition\nand determines the compensation due. The Property Report must define each right affected, identify the\nboundaries and ownership thereof and clearly record all information related to these rights.\nThe Employer may request the PSP to engage with the Service Provider at an early stage in the Design\nProcess in order to assist the Service Provider to deal with complex land rights and to provide specialist input\nwhere required. Where the Service Provider is in any doubt concerning how to deal with specific complexities\nrelating to the above, the Service Provider may also discuss these circumstances with the Employer who may\nthen engage the PSP for specialist assistance.\nEvery 2 (two) months, in each of its Regions, the Employer holds Land Acquisition co-ordination meetings. At\nthese meetings, every project involving Land Acquisition is discussed in detail and problems focused on. The\nService Provider shall ensure that a senior representative attends these meetings until such time as the Land\nAcquisition process has reached the stage where access to site is confirmed.\nThe Service Provider shall meet with every land property owner whose property or rights therein may be\nrequired or impacted upon by the project. Whilst the Service Provider may not engage in any negotiations\nwith respect to financial compensation (Value) the Service Provider must ensure that each owner is made\naware of the extent of land required, and the consequences that the project may have on matters such as the\naccess, boreholes, irrigation system, parking, frontage of buildings and the like, all in accordance with the\nrelevant sections in the LAC Manual. The document that the land owner is required to sign must be carefully\ncompleted in order to minimise any additional issues that may be raised when the PSP engages with the land\nowner to negotiate the terms of the land acquisition. If the land owner insists on discussing matters relating to\ncompensation, financial loss, legal aspects or any other matter that falls outside of the Service Provider’s area\nof responsibility, the Service Provider must timeously request the Employer for the assistance of the PSP.\nC3-54\n\nWhere it is necessary to relocate families/individuals situated on land under the control of a Tribal (Traditional)\nAuthority, the Service Provider must engage with the Employer and the PSP as soon as it is possible in the\nDesign Phase in order to initiate land acquisition in good time. The Service Provider must as soon as possible\nprovide details of notional sites, quitrents and all other details required in the form of Property Reports. The\nPSP will engage further with the affected families and the Tribal (Traditional) Authority’s leadership in order to\nconclude a relocation agreement with each family affected. This is most often a lengthy process and\nconsequently poses a material risk to the Employer’s programme for the Construction Phase of the project.\nThe relocation agreement negotiated by the PSP will inter alia deal with all aspects relating to the relocation\nof the occupants of the land including for example:\n• Alternative land;\n• Acceptance of building plans for the alternative accommodation proposed;\n• Who will construct the alternative accommodation and by when; and\n• All other relevant matters peculiar to each relocation.\nThe Employer shall indicate if the occupants of Tribal land are to be relocated under this project, or by another\ncontractor. The Works Contract must include the construction of the alternative accommodation, deal with the\nprovision of services, the physical relocation of the family affected and the demolition of the structure(s) from\nwhich the occupants are being relocated. The Service Provider is required to supervise such Works Contract\nand attend to all administrative processes associated therewith.\nThe Service Provider must note that the guidelines contained in the LAC Manual, where applicable to the\nspecific project, constitutes essential information concerning the role and responsibilities of the Service\nProvider and must be considered by the Service Provider in the pricing of the work.\nb) SPLUMA Implications and Duties\n(i) Introduction\nThe Spatial Planning and Land Use Management Act, Act 16 of 2013 (“SPLUMA Act”)\ncommenced from 1 July 2015 and constitutes enabling national legislation.\nSection 25 of The South African National Roads Agency Limited and National Roads Act, Act No\n7 of 1998 (the “SANRAL Act”) sets out the powers of the Employer which include:\n• All strategic planning concerning the South African National Road System; and\n• The planning, design, construction, operation, management, control, maintenance and\nrehabilitation of National Roads for South Africa.\nIn terms of the Constitution of the Republic of South Africa, No. 108 of 1996 (the “Constitution”),\nthe following is relevant:\n• National Roads do not fall into either Schedule 4 or 5 of the Constitution and are thus a\nNational competency;\n• Provincial Planning is an exclusive Provincial competency;\n• Municipal Planning is an exclusive Municipal competency;\n• Municipalities may enact SPLUMA Planning By-laws (the “Municipal By-laws”) to\nadminister Municipal Planning; and\n• Municipal By-Laws are binding on ALL, including Organs of State unless a specific\nMunicipal By-law provides for and expressly stated exemption.\nWith respect to Municipal Planning the following is relevant:\n• “Planning, in the context of Municipal affairs, is a term which has assumed a particular, well\nestablished meaning which includes the zoning of land and the establishment of townships.\nIn that context, the term is commonly used to define the control and regulation of the use\nof land.” (le Sueur judgement) and;\n• “It is, however, true that the functional areas allocated to the various spheres of government\nare not contained in hermetically sealed compartments. But notwithstanding, they remain\ndistinct from one another. This is the position even in respect of functional areas that share\nthe same wording like roads, planning, sport and others. The distinctiveness lies in the level\nin which a particular power is exercised.” (Gauteng Development Tribunal judgement)\n(ii) Impact of SPLUMA on the Employer’s Planning Authority\nC3-55\n\n“Land Development” is defined in the SPLUMA Act as the erection of buildings or structures on\nland or the change of land use. Accordingly National Road Projects are included in this definition\nand are subject to the SPLUMA Act.\nSection 2 of the SPLUMA Act inter alia provides that except as provided for in this Act, no legi-\nslation not repealed by this Act may prescribe an alternative or parallel system on Spatial\nPlanning.\nSection 33 of the SPLUMA Act provides inter-alia that except as provided in this Act, all\ndevelopment applications must be submitted to the Municipality as the authority of first instance;\nand Section 53 of the SPLUMA Act provides inter-alia that the registration of a property resulting\nfrom a Land Development Application may not be performed unless the Municipality certifies that\nall requirements and conditions for approval have been complied with, known as “Municipal\nConsent”.\n(iii) The need for and consideration of an Agreement in terms of SPLUMA\nSection 30(3): Whilst the relevant Municipality may regard an Authorisation in terms of any other\nlegislation that meets all the requirements set out in the SPLUMA Act or Provincial legislation as\nan authorisation in terms of SPLUMA, one may argue that the Employer’s declaration in terms of\nSection 40 of the SANRAL Act was an approval in terms of SPLUMA. This argument may be\npossible in the context of existing National Roads but in regard to new roads including the\nupgrading of roads, the widening of roads etc. this is not possible.\nSection 29: A municipality must in terms of this Section consult any Organ of State responsible\nof administering legislation relating to any aspect of an activity that also requires approval in terms\nof the SPLUMA Act in order to co-ordinate activities and give effect to the respective requirements\nof such legislation and to avoid duplication – Section 29(1).\nA municipality, in giving effect to Chapter 3 of the Constitution, may, after consultation with the\nOrgan of the State contemplated in Section 29(1), enter into a written agreement with that Organ\nof State to avoid duplication in the submission of information or the carrying out of a process\nrelating to any aspect of an activity that also requires authorisation under the SPLUMA Act –\nSection 29(2); and\nAfter a Municipality has concluded an agreement contemplated in Section 29(2), the relevant\nMunicipal Planning Tribunal (“MPT”) may take account of any process authorised under the\nlegislation covered by that agreement as adequate for meeting the requirements of the SPLUMA\nAct – Section 29(3).\nThe Employer therefore intends to conclude a Section 29 Agreement in terms of the\nSPLUMA Act with the Municipalities for its National Road Projects.\nAccordingly, a draft Proforma Agreement is provided as an Appendix in Part C4.\n(iv) The Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement\nThe Employer has and continues to engage various state entities including the Department of\nRural Development and Land Reform (“DRDLR”), the Department of Co-operative Governance\nand Traditional Affairs (“CoGTA”), South African Local Government Association (“SALGA”) and\nothers to have the draft SPLUMA Agreement adopted as part of the DRDLR’s initiative to create\nNational Norms and Standards and Guidelines with respect to the development by the State (in\nall its forms) of Public Service Infrastructure and in particular Public Service Infrastructure that is\nin the national interest such as National Roads;\nAs to the SPLUMA Agreement the following is emphasized:\n• A preamble sets out the context for the agreement;\n• The Agreement deals primarily with the planning impact that the National Road Project (the\n“Project”) will have on the surrounding planning to the proposed the Project;\n• The statement(s) contained in the SPLUMA Agreement and which is of material importance\nis: where the Municipality expressly confirms that it is satisfied, in as far as such confir-\nmation is required in terms of the SPLUMA Act, Provincial Legislation or the Municipal By-\nLaw, that the Employer’s planning and designs for the Project in so far as it relates to\naccess and/or acquisitions of severed land and as depicted on the Road Development Plan\nC3-56\n\nand Access Plan are adequate and further confirms that no further or additional process(es)\nrelating to the Project are required. Such confirmation also extends to land use aspects as\ncontemplated in terms of the SPLUMA Act and the Municipal By-Laws;\nFurther the Municipality confirms that it is satisfied that the information provided by the\nEmployer in so far as it relates to access and/or acquisitions of severed land and recorded\nin the SPLUMA Agreement, is/are sufficient for a land development application contem-\nplated in terms of the SPLUMA Act and the Municipal By-Laws;\n• The Agreement then further provides inter alia for the incorporation of National Roads into\nthe Municipality Spatial Development Framework and the amendment of the Municipality’s\nLand Use Management Scheme;\nFurther the agreement expressly provides that the public participation process undertaken\nby the Employer in terms of National Environmental Management Act, Act No. 107 of 1998\n(“NEMA”), is sufficient and satisfactory to satisfy the provisions of the SPLUMA Act and\nMunicipal By-Laws ;\n• Records the Employer standing as an Applicant in terms of Section 45(d) of the SPLUMA\nAct;\n• Deals specifically with a dispute mechanism; and\n• Provides for key annexures namely:\n- A Corridor Plan; a Road Development Plan and Access Plan.\n(v) Service Provider’s obligations in regard to the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement\n• The Service Provider, as an Agent of the Employer will be required to facilitate the con-\nclusion of the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement between the Employer and the relevant\nMunicipality(s);\n• The Service Provider will require to prepare the annexures to the Section 29 SPLUMA\nAgreement and in this regard proforma examples are attached as an Appendix in Part C4;\n• The Service Provider will be required to interact with the Employer’s Property Service\nProvider (“PSP”) with respect to certain key plan(s) relevant to the National Road section\nbeing the subject of the Project;\n• The above process will entail engagements with all relevant authorities stakeholder(s);\n• The Service Provider will not be required to make a formal SPLUMA/Municipal By-Law\nApplication after the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement has been concluded; and\n• The Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement must be concluded prior to the Project being\ntendered for construction.\n(vi) Process after the conclusion of the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement\nWhilst PSP will undertake and submit the formal SPLUMA and/or Municipal By-Law application\nto give effect to the tasks as outlined further below, the Service Provider may be required to\nprovide certain Project related information and plans in support thereof;\n• The PSP will make the requisite application in terms of the SPLUMA Act and the Municipal\nBy-Law using the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement as the basis for such application;\n• The PSP will notify the Minister of DRDLR in terms of Section 52 of the SPLUMA Act; and\n• Once the MPT has made its decision it may be necessary for the Employer to deal with\nobjections and/or lodge an appeal against the decision of the MPT. In this regard the\nService Provider may be required to provide certain relevant Project information and/or\nevidence in support thereof. The PSP will act for the Employer in such event.\n(vii) One or more Section 29 SPLUMA Agreements\nPending the complexities encountered in the design of the Project(s), it may be necessary to\nconclude more than one Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement.\n(viii) Design amendments\nIt is to be noted that any amendments to the final design of the Road Project will necessitate that\nan addendum to the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement be concluded. The Service Provider will be\nrequired to facilitate the conclusion of such addendum(s).\n(ix) Transfer of access roads and declaration through Towns Agreements\nThe Service Provider is to note that the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement is to be compiled and\nconcluded in conjunction with the above Agreements.\nC3-57\n\n(x) Municipal By-Law: Exemption\nCertain Municipalities have and may still introduce an exemption in their Municipal By-Laws for\n“Public Service Infrastructure” of which National Roads form a part thereof.\nIn such instances the nature and context of the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement may be\nsubstantially reduced and where the Section 29 SPLUMA Agreement would simply act as the\nRoad Project Agreement with the Municipality in order to formalize how the impacts the Project\non the surrounding areas have been accommodated in the final design.\nThe Service Provider must accordingly check the Municipality’s By-Laws in order to establish this\nfact.\nC3.4.11 Road Safety Audit\nThe Service Provider shall in terms of the South African Road Safety Audit Manual (Second Edition, May\n2012), hereafter referred to as the SARSAM:\na) Develop a Scope of Works for the procurement of the services of a Road Safety Audit Team for Stages\n2 and 3 of the Project.The stages and numbering refer to the definitions in SARSAM. Such Scope of\nWorks shall include but are not limited to:\n• Conducting a Project Information Review;\n• Conducting a Site Inspection/s;\n• Producing a Road Safety Audit Report\n• Issuing the Road Safety Audit Report and discussing the initial findings with Employer and Service\nProvider;\n• Initiating and Conducting a Completion Meeting;\nas prescribed in the SARSAM.\nb) Recommend with motivation to the Employer the composition of the Road Safety Audit Team depending\non the size and complexity of the project, as prescribed in the SARSAM. Generally, the Road Safety\nAudit Team will comprise an Audit Team Leader and at least two (2) Audit Team members. The Audit\nTeam Leader is the lead auditor that is responsible for compiling the road safety audit report and\nrepresenting the audit team in liaising with the Employer and the Service Provider. The Audit Team\nMember/s is the second auditor that assists in and contributes to the road safety audit. The Audit Team\nLeader and Member/s must be independent from the Service Provider and must not be under the employ\nof the Service Provider.\nThe Service Provider must ensure that the minimum requirements of the Audit Team Leader and Members\ncomply with the Employers latest Road Safety Audit Policy. A copy of the Employer’s Road Safety Audit Policy\nis included in Appendix I.\nOnce the Road Safety Audit Team is appointed, the Service Provider shall:\n▪ Compile and Issue a Road Safety Audit Brief to the Road Safety Audit Team;\n▪ Initiate and conduct a Commencement Meeting with the Road Safety Audit Team;\n▪ Attend a Completion Meeting if requested by the Employer;\n▪ Review Audit Findings and Recommendations;\n▪ Compile and issue a Response Report including Risk Assessment of Safety Concerns;\nas prescribed in the SARSAM\nThe Service Provider shall implement Design Changes as identified by the Road Safety Audit Team and as\nagreed to with Employer, as specified in the Response Report.\nThe Service Provider should provide written feedback on lessons learned and knowledge gained, which shall\ntake the form of a short report.\nWith the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS to identify potential hazardous locations. This will require assessment data to be uploaded into the\nSARDS portal by Service Provider as per the defined standard format or SARDS interface and then analysed\nusing SARDS.\nC3-58\n\nC3.4.12 Project Cost and Feasibility\nAt the reporting stage of each design development phase a cost estimate of the works, including contract price\nadjustment (CPA) shall be determined. A separate cost estimate shall be compiled for each design option. On\ncompletion of the detailed design stage this estimate shall consist of a priced schedule of quantities. This\nestimate shall include a contractor’s probable preliminary and general costs as well as a provision for contract\nprice adjustment. No allowance is to be made for a contingent sum. However, the inclusion of provisional sums\nwithin the schedule of quantities is acceptable.\nIn addition to a cost estimate of each design being developed a feasibility analysis shall also be conducted as\nspecified.\nC3.4.13 Project Programme and Constructability\nAt completion of the detailed design phase the Service Provider shall provide a comprehensive logic pro-\ngramme of construction activities regardless of the complexity of the project. The programme shall also be\nused to verify a realistic construction period.\nThe programme shall include the following:\n• A work breakdown structure that identifies all major activities.\n• Scheduled duration for each activity.\n• Linkages between activities that clearly identify sequence, floats and critical path.\n• Simultaneous activities shall be identified as well as priority activities.\n• Time for each activity.\n• A risk assessment schedule with mitigating plans of issues that could prevent the due completion date\nbeing met.\nShould the Employer require an electronic version of the programme for review purposes, the Service Provider\nshall supply the programme in a format compatible with the Employer’s software.\nThroughout the development of the project design the Service Provider shall consider the constructability of\nthe design which shall also be reflected in the programme. Typical aspects and/or constraints that should be\nconsidered (but not limited to) are traffic volumes, time of day/year, work space, weather, safety, environmental\nissues and land acquisition issues.\nC3.4.14 Detailed Design Report\nA detailed design report shall be submitted for discussion at the progress meeting. This report shall, as a\nminimum, cover all the duties as specified under Detail Design including:\n• Deviations from standards, e.g. geometric, drainage, road classification, structures, etc.\n• Geometric and capacity improvements\n• Existing versus proposed X-section\n• X-section development strategy – 20 years\n• Intersection/interchange improvements\n• Outcome of interaction with stakeholders\n• Relocation of services\n• Existing pavement condition and accepted pavement design\n• Materials utilisation\n• Geotechnical improvements\n• Road furniture improvements\n• Additional land requirements\n• Drainage improvements\n• New structures and any capacity improvements to existing structures (which includes bridges, major\nculverts, lesser culverts, walls, gantries, light masts and buildings)\n• Comprehensive report on Land Acquisition (where the report shall inter alia report on the risks asso-\nciated with gaining timeous access to the land required)\nC3.4.15 Phase/Stage Discontinuity\nWhenever discontinuity occurs between various design phases within the design development stage, the\nService Provider shall review the design of the Service Provider who undertook the previous phase design.\nThe Service Provider shall also take account of any other information and data acquired from its own approved\nC3-59\n\ninvestigations. Should the Service Provider be required to re-design certain portions of the previous phase,\nthe Service Provider shall take full liability for that portion.\nWhenever discontinuity between design development stage and/or tender documentation stage and/or\ncontract administration and monitoring of the works stage occurs:\n• The Service Provider shall review the design of the Service Provider who undertook the design\ndevelopment (the designer) taking account of any other information and data acquired from its own\napproved investigations. Should the Service Provider be required to re-design certain portions of the\noriginal design the Service Provider shall take full liability for that portion.\n• The designer will be afforded the opportunity to review the final tender documentation compiled by the\nService Provider. This is done in recognition of the designer’s ongoing liability for the design and\nproviding the protection to review and comment on the original design and specifications still being\nappropriate for the prevailing circumstances and/or conditions. In addition, the designer will be provided\nthe opportunity to inspect the construction works periodically to ensure his/her professional obligations\nare not being compromised. Any visit shall be in the company of the Service Provider appointed for\nconstruction monitoring. The frequency of any such inspections shall be appropriate to the scope and\nscale of the Works.\nC3.4.16 Measurement and Payment\nItem Unit\n3.4.1 Concept design\n(a) Road works:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(b) Structures (bridges and other major structures):\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(c) Electrical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(d) Buildings:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(e) Mechanical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(f) Electronic:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(g) Other:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n* Note to tenderer: For tender purposes the rate for fee adjustment\nshall be zero. During the contract the applicable rate will be\ncalculated i.t.o. fee adjustment specification.\nC3-60\n\nItem Unit\n3.4.2 Preliminary design\n(a) Road works:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(b) Structures (bridges and other major structures):\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(c) Electrical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(d) Buildings:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(e) Mechanical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(f) Electronic:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(g) Other:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated cost of the works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n* Note to tenderer: For tender purposes the rate for fee adjustment\nshall be zero. The remaining 10% of the fee is claimable upon delivery\nof the approved respective final report.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.4.4 Where circumstances justify need to pay for the accommodation of\nstudents, this shall be discussed with the Employers project manager.\nThe provisional sum item shall be paid in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nPro-rata payments shall be made for partial months for training provided based on a 23 (twenty-three) working\nday month.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-items 3.4.9(b)(i) and (b)(ii) in accordance with Clause\nC2.1.6.\nC3-64\n\nItem Unit\n3.4.10 Phase/Stage Discontinuity\n(a) Review of previous phase/stage lump sum (LS)\n(b) Review of tender documentation by original designer prime cost (PC)\n(c) Visit to construction site by original designer prime cost (PC)\n(d) Handling cost i.r.o. sub-items 3.4.10(b) and 3.4.10(c) percentage (%)\nThe unit of measurement for payment sub-item 3.4.10(a) shall be the lump sum.\nThe sum tendered shall be full compensation for the time and cost to review the respective documents and for\nthe Design Check and Design Review as specified in C3.4.15, including all travel and subsistence costs, the\ncosts of all written, telephonic and electronic communications and all other incidentals necessary to read,\nunderstand, create and express opinions and decisions from them, regardless of their quantity or complexity.\nIt shall also include the submission of a report addressing the full review and a report addressing the Design\nCheck and Design Review as specified in C3.4.15.\nThe prime cost sum allowed under sub-item 3.4.10(b) is to cover full compensation for the time and cost to\nreview the respective documents including all travel and subsistence costs, the costs of all written, telephonic\nand electronic communications and all other incidentals necessary to read, understand, create and express\nopinions and decisions from them, regardless of their quantity or complexity. It shall also include the\nsubmission of a report addressing the full review.\nThe prime cost sum allowed under sub-item 3.4.10(c) is to cover all costs by the original designer to visit the\nsite.\nThe Prime Cost shall be paid in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nThe rate tendered (%) for handling cost under item 3.4.10(d) is for the mark-up on the disbursement costs\nincurred by the Service Provider.\nThe rates tendered under this payment item shall also include for all costs associated with compliance with\nthe Construction Regulations of the Occupational Health and Safety Act (No. 85 of 1993) as relevant for the\ndesign of the Works during the construction phase as well as the designer’s obligations with respect to the\nrelevant professional engineers’ legislation.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-item 3.4.10(a) in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.4.11 Field work for Identification of Material Sources by\nService Provider\n(a) Personnel cost provisional sum (PS)\n(b) Disbursements prime cost (PC)\n(c) Handling cost i.r.o. sub-item 3.4.11(b) percentage (%)\nThe provisional sum allowed under sub-item 3.4.11(a) is to cover all field work undertaken by the Service\nProvider related to the identification of material sources as specified in Clause C3.4.3. is for the mark-up on the disbursement costs\nincurred by the Service Provider.\nItem Unit\nC3-65\n\n3.4.12 Investigation and sampling for roadbed, borrow\npits, quarries, structure founding materials Small bags number\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.12(a) shall be the lump sum.\nThe sum tendered shall cover all costs associated in establishing an appropriately sized team on site to
