include full compensation for obtaining quotations from local plant hire\ncompanies in accordance with the Employer’s standard requirements, for handling fees and profits of the\nService Provider.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.12(d) shall be the number of test pits excavated by hand labour\n(excluding the sampling and profiling/photography) and backfilled at locations identified by the relevant Design\nSpecialist.\nThe rate tendered shall include for all costs including travel, labour, accommodation, subsistence, plant,\nmaterials, etc. associated with the excavation and safeguarding of test pits and subsequent backfilling thereof\nas specified.\nThe unit of measurement under pay items 3.4.12(e) shall be the number of test pits profiled/photographed by\nthe relevant Design Specialist and sampled (either dug by mechanical means or hand labour) irrespective of\nthe number of horizons encountered.\nThe rate tendered shall include all costs including travel, labour, accommodation, subsistence, plant, materials,\nsample bags, etc. associated with the profiling and sampling of test pits.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.12(f) shall be the number of DCP tests at locations identified by\nthe relevant Design Specialist.\nThe rate tendered shall include for drilling through any “refusal” layers, completing the test and submission of\nthe DCP analysis to the relevant Design Specialist.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.12(g) shall be the number of large bags and/or small bags\ntransported.\nThe rate tendered shall include all costs to transport the bags to the laboratory selected to carry out the testing,\nirrespective of distance.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to all sub-items excluding sub-items 3.3.12(b) and (c) in\naccordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nC3-66\n\nItem Unit\n3.4.13 Laboratory Testing and Reporting\n(a) Standard tests as listed in the Pricing Schedule number\n(b) Laboratory testing where no schedule is prime cost (PC)\navailable, or specialised testing\n(c) Handling costs i.r.o. pay item 3.4.13(b) percentage (%)\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.13(a) shall be the number of tests prescribed by the relevant\nDesign Specialist and carried out by the laboratory.\nThe rates tendered shall cover all costs associated in completing the individual tests and shall include for all\ncalculations and reporting. Any tests proven to be erroneous shall not be paid for.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.4.13(b) shall be a Prime Cost Sum. The Service Provider shall\nidentify the type and estimated quantity of tests required and shall compile quotation document in accordance\nwith the Employer’s standard requirements/policy for the purpose of procuring the relevant service.\nThe Prime Cost item shall be paid in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nThe percentage tendered under pay item 3.4.13(c) is a percentage of the amount actually spent under payment\nitem 3.4.13(b) and shall include full compensation for all costs of the Service Provider as specified.\nWhere the prior identification of materials testing requirements is not possible, a Provisional sum has been\nincluded in the Pricing Schedule for the procurement of such services. Where testing is carried out under a\nProvisional Sum the percentage handling fee tendered in the Pricing Schedule shall include for all the Service\nProvider’s costs associated with:\n- compiling a schedule of all envisaged work\n- production of a document for procurement purposes (including printing etc)\n- calling for quotations/tenders\n- evaluation of quotations/tenders received and recommendations to the Employer.\n- handling fees\n- profit\nProcurement of such services shall be in accordance with Clause C3.1.18.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-item 3.4.13(a) in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.4.14 Protection, removal, re-alignment, relocation and\nreplacement of services prior to Construction Phase\n(a) Services prime cost (PC)\n(b) Handling costs i.r.o. pay item 3.4.14(a)\npercentage (%)\nThe prime cost item shall be paid in accordance with the provisions of Clause C2.1.8. The prime cost sum is\nto cover full compensation for the protection, removal, re-alignment, relocation and replacement of services\nusing specialised equipment in urban areas. If the work is to be performed by the service owner, the amount\ninvoiced by the service owner shall be paid and a Works Authorisation submitted.\nThe tendered percentage shall include full compensation for the handling cost in respect of sub-item 3.4.14(a).\nItem Unit\n3.4.15 Employer’s Gateway Review Process\n(a) Regional Gateway Review lump sum (LS)\n(b) National Gateway Review lump sum (LS)\nThe unit of measurement for sub-items 3.4.15(a) and (b) shall be the lump sum.\nC3-67\n\nThe lump sum tendered shall include full compensation for time related costs, all disbursements, materials,\nprinting and all other costs associated with presenting the project concept or preliminary alternatives\n(whichever is applicable) to the Employer’s Gateway Review team, before commencing with Detail Design.\nC3-68\n\nC3.5 TENDER DOCUMENTATION\nC3.5.1 Scope\nThis section covers the requirements and process for the compilation of tender documentation and invitation\nto tender for the Works Contract.\nC3.5.2 Tender Documentation\nThe following documents shall form the Tender Documents for the Works Contract:\ni) VOLUME 1: The FIDIC Conditions of Contract for Construction, 1999 issued by the International\nFederation of Consulting Engineers (to be purchased separately by tenderers).\nii) VOLUME 2: The Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities\n(Draft Standard October 2020 edition) prepared by the Committee of Transport Officials, (COTO), as\namended.\niii) VOLUME 3: The Project Document, which includes Conditions of Tender, Particular Conditions of Con-\ntract, Scope of Works and Forms, all in accordance with the Employer’s standard proforma document.\niv) VOLUMES 4 and 5: Books of Construction Drawings for tender purposes (if required).\nv) VOLUME 6: Materials Investigation and Utilisation Information (if required by the Employer to be bound\nseparately)\nvi) VOLUME 7: Environmental Management Programme (if required by the Employer to be bound\nseparately).\nC3.5.3 Compilation of Final Tender Document\nThe Service Provider shall adhere to the following process:\ni) Compile and submit all relevant draft tender documentation for the purpose of discussion with the\nEmployer.\nii) Subsequent to discussion of the draft documentation, the Service Provider shall:\n• compile final documents incorporating all the amendments arising therefrom the discussion;\n• compile and write the requisite number of complete sets of document on CD’s/DVD’s with the\nstandard cover detail imprinted on the CD/DVD as well as the CD/DVD case\n• prepare the requisite number of paper copies of the complete set of the tender document;\n• deliver the CD’s/DVD’s and paper copies to the Employer’s regional office before the tender\ndocument availability date specified in the Tender Notice and Invitation to Tender.\nIn the event where draft documentation is of sub-standard/poor quality, the above-mentioned process will be\nrepeated. In such circumstances the additional time spent by the Employer shall be paid for by the Service\nProvider as specified under Additional Duties.\nC3.5.4 Tender Advertisement\nThe Service Provider shall prepare the tender advertisement and submit to the Employer who will place the\ntender advertisement with the relevant organisations.\nC3.5.5 Measurement and Payment\nItem Unit\n3.5.1 Tender documentation\n(a) Preparation of tender documentation lump sum (LS)\n(b) Disbursements lump sum (LS)\n(c) Tender Documents in electronic format number\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.5.1(a) shall be the lump sum.\nThe lump sum shall include full compensation for time related costs associated with the compilation of the draft\ntender documents, discussion/meetings with the Employer and subsequent finalisation of the tender\ndocumentation.\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.5.1(b) shall be the lump sum.\nC3-69\n\nThe lump sum tendered shall include full compensation for all disbursements, materials, printing and all other\ncosts associated with compilation of the draft tender documents, discussion/meetings with the Employer and\nsubsequent finalisation of the tender documentation.\nThe unit of measurement under pay item 3.5.1(c) shall be the number.\nThe rate tendered shall include for all costs associated with the compilation of each full set of final tender\ndocuments and shall include for all expenditure on labour and materials, communication, postage, packaging\nand submitting in electronic format incurred by the Service Provider.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nC3-70\n\nC3.6 CLARIFICATION MEETING, TENDER PERIOD AND TENDER EVALUATION\nC3.6.1 Scope\nThis section covers the requirements and process for the Contractors' clarification meeting, tender period and\nevaluation of tenders received for the Works Contract.\nC3.6.2 Clarification Meeting\ni) The Service Provider shall prepare a tender presentation of the scope of the works for a clarification\nmeeting and submit to the Employer’s Procurement office for uploading onto the Employer’s website,\nfor prospective tenderers for the Works Contract in accordance with the Employer’s standard\nrequirements, which shall, inter alia, include the following: Presenting an agenda for the meeting.\nii) Introduction of the team, including the Employer’s Project Manager and Procurement Officer, the Service\nProvider’s personnel, other Stakeholders, if any (for example, Provisional Government Officials,\nmembers of the PLC and the PLO).\niii) Describing the Conditions of Tender and Tender Data.\niv) Describing the Conditions of Contract and Contract Data.\nv) Describing the Works with as much detail as is required for specific items or operations.\nvi) Describing important aspects of the project, e.g. limits of the Contract, location of borrow pits/quarries,\nbridge sites, display of core samples, etc to provide a holistic overview.\nvii) Announce any amendments made to the tender documents.\nviii) Providing an Employer’s email address for submission of any clarification questions.\nix) The estimate for the cost of the Works shall not be divulged.\nC3.6.3 Tender Period\nThe Service Provider shall in all cases only communicate, through the Employer, with all tenderers during the\ntender period in accordance with Conditions of Tender for the works contract. The actions below need to be\nfollowed:\n• Compiling for distributing by the Employer minutes of the clarification briefing as an addendum at least\n10 (ten) working days before the date and time of tender closure.\n• Attending to any technical queries or points of clarification requested by tenderers by providing questions\nand answers to the Employer for distribution as part of any addenda.\n• Compilation for distribution, by the Employer and confirmation of receipt by all tenderers of any addenda\nto the Tender Document.\n• Informing and obtaining approval from the Employer of any unresolved enquiries, potential alternative\nproposals of which notice has been received from tenderers and changes to date for submission of\ntenders.\nC3.6.4 Tender Opening and Tender Evaluation\nThe Service Provider shall be invited to the opening of tenders for the Works Contract that shall take place via\nlive streaming. The Employer will conduct the compliance of all tender responses and identify all responsive\ntenders. The Employer will issue copies of the responsive tenders electronically to the Service Provide,\nincluding the Compliance evaluation report.\nThe Service Provider shall evaluate all responsive tenders received from the Employer and compile a\nconsolidated Tender Evaluation Report for submission to the Employer. During tender evaluation, the Service\nProvider shall:\n- Conduct the technical and financial analysis and risk assessment of postulated tender as well as\nalternative tenders.\n-\n- Advise tenderers, in terms of Conditions of Tender through the Employer of any arithmetical, or other\ncorrections made to errors in the extension of rates and/or totals in their tenders and the effect of such\ncorrections and receive written acceptance of such corrections.\n- Identify imbalanced rates and request acceptable explanations and/or adjustments thereof in terms of\nConditions of Tender through the Employer, which shall be taken into account in the tender evaluation\nprocess.\n- Make available to the Employer (SANRAL Head Office – W Lategan email: lategan@sanral.co.za ) 1\n(one) electronic copy of the Pricing Schedule of the lowest 2 (two) responsive tenders.\nC3-71\n\nThe Service Provider shall, during the tender evaluation, liaise closely with the Employer with respect to any\npossible disqualification of tenders or issues of a substantive nature identified prior to submission of the Tender\nEvaluation Report.\nThe Tender Evaluation Report shall conform to the requirement of the Employer’s proforma document with\nrespect to content and format. One (1) electronic copy of the Tender Evaluation report shall be submitted to\nthe Employer on or before the date specified in Clause C3.1.9. It is important to note that the evaluation of\ntenders and the compilation of a tender Evaluation Report will be on a similar SANRAL project from\nanother Service Provider, in the case where the Service Provider compiled the tender documents for\nthe Works Contract.\nC3.6.5 Alternative Tenders\nThe Employer promotes the submission of appropriate/innovative alternative for the Works Contract. The\nService Provider shall liaise closely with the Employer as to the acceptability/or otherwise of the principles of\nany alternative tenders proposed by a tenderer during the tender period.\nThe Service Provider shall present the analysis of alternative tenders along with recommendations in the\nTender Evaluation Report. Assessment and evaluation of alternative tenders pertaining to, for example,\nalternative seal or asphalt types, changes to contract period or such like shall not be subject to additional\npayment.\nIn cases where an alternative tender requires detailed analysis, e.g. design checks, the Service Provider shall\nsubmit a motivation, together with a cost estimate, to the Employer for approval prior to commencing with any\nadditional detailed analysis. Such a cost estimate shall be based on rates tendered in accordance with Section\nC3.8.\nC3.6.6 Contract Documents for the Works\nThe Service Provider shall, within 14 (fourteen) days of the date of the Letter of Acceptance, prepare and\ncourier 2 (two) sets of contract documents for signature. These documents shall be prepared in accordance\nwith the requirements listed in the Employer’s proforma document. One (1) electronic copy in pdf format of a\nfully signed (by all parties) contract shall also be submitted. The number of additional sets of contract docu-\nments for use by the Engineer and his site staff shall be at the discretion of the Service Provider.\nThe following shall be bound at the back of Volume 3, or bound together as a separate volume together with\na table of contents:\n(i) all addenda issued, together with proof of receipt, completed and signed by the successful tenderer;\n(ii) all letters, statements and documents submitted by the successful tenderer with his tender;\n(iii) all correspondence between the Service Provider and the successful tenderer prior to tender\nacceptance;\n(iv) the Employer’s Letter of Acceptance of tender; and\n(v) the contractor’s Letter of Acceptance, including any conditions.\nThe contract documents shall comprise all the documents mentioned above, together with Volumes 1 and 2\nand the performance guarantee, which shall remain separate documents.\nThe Engineer shall assess the performance guarantee provided by the Contractor for conformance with the\nproforma working in the tender documentation and if satisfied, shall thereafter submit it to the Employer for\nsafe keeping.\n3.6.7 Measurement and Payment\nItem Unit\n3.6.1 Clarification meeting and tender period\n(a) Service Provider’s cost lump sum (LS)\n(b) Provision for venue and bus prime cost sum (PC)\n(c) Handling costs i.r.o. pay item 3.6.1(b) percentage (%)\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.6.1(a) shall be the lump sum.\nC3-72\n\nThe sum tendered shall include for all costs (excluding those for which a Prime Cost Sum has been provided)\nincurred by the Service Provider with respect to the duties for the clarification meeting and tender period as\nspecified.\nThe unit of measurement for sub-item 3.6.1(c) shall be the percentage.\nThe tendered percentage shall include full compensation for the handling costs in respect of sub-item 3.6.1(b).\nThe Prime Cost Sum items shall be paid for in accordance with Clause C2.1.8.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable to sub-item 3.6.1(a) in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.6.2 Tender evaluation\n(a) Tender evaluation and report lump sum (LS)\n(b) Disbursements lump sum (LS)\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.6.2(a) shall be the lump sum.\nThe lump sum shall include full compensation for time related costs associated with the evaluation of the tender\nand compilation of tender report. It is important to note that the evaluation of tenders and the compilation\nof a tender Evaluation Report will be on a similar SANRAL project from another Service Provider, in\nthe case where the Service Provider compiled the tender documents for the Works Contract.\nThe unit of measurement for pay item 3.6.2(b) shall be the lump sum.\nThe lump sum tendered shall include full compensation for time related cost to familiarise the Service Provider\nwith the specification and estimated quantities for a similar project from another Service Provider, all\ndisbursements, materials, printing and all other costs associated with the evaluation of the tender and\ncompilation of tender report and the submission to SANRAL Head Office (W Lategan e-mail:\nlategan@sanral.co.za) 1 (one) electronic copy of the Pricing Schedule of the lowest 2 (two) responsive tenders.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nItem Unit\n3.6.3 Preparation of Works contract documents lump sum (LS)\nThe lump sum tendered under this pay item shall be for the preparation of 2 (two) Contract Documents for\nsignature by the Employer and the appointed Contractor for the Works in accordance with Clause C3.6.6 and\n4 (four) additional sets for use by the Employer (1), Contractor (1) and Engineer (2) and provide an electronic\ncopy of the signed Contract Documents on a CD. The sum tendered shall include for all expenditure on labour,\nmaterials, communication, postage and packing incurred by the Service Provider in the production and delivery\nof the documents. Payment of the lump sum will be made on receipt of 2 (two) copies for signature and 4 (four)\nadditional of the Contract Documents and an electronic copy of the signed Contract Documents on a CD, as\nspecified, by the Employer. The cost of producing any additional copies for use by the Service Provider shall\nbe deemed to be included in the lump sum tendered for this item.\nContract price adjustment shall be applicable in accordance with Clause C2.1.6.\nC3-73\n\nC3.7 ADMINISTRATION AND MONITORING OF THE WORKS CONTRACT\nC3.7.1 Scope\nThis section covers the duties and obligations of the Service Provider in the provision of the Engineer and site\nsupervisory staff for the administration, construction monitoring and measurement of the Works carried out by\nthe Contractor appointed by the Employer during the original Works Contract duration, extended duration and\nincluding prolonged site stoppages not under the control of the Employer (i.e. Business Rescue or Community\nunrest).\nC3.7.2 Administration and Monitoring\na) Fulfilling the functions of the Contract Engineer (CE)\nThree (3) months prior to the commencement of the Works Contract, the Service Provider shall submit for\nconsideration and approval to the Employer a detailed proposal in the Employer’s prescribed format which\nshall include a CV of appropriate experience and qualifications for the proposed Contract Engineer. In addition,\nthe Service Provider shall also complete Returnable Schedule Forms B1 and B2 for the proposed Contract\nEngineer. Amongst other criteria to be used, shall be the individual threshold of 85% and the limitation to\nparticipate in a maximum number of 6 (six) conventional contracts in active construction phase with the\nEmployer which will be completed after the Taking-Over Certificate is issued to the Contractor.\nThe approved Engineer for the project shall be authorized by the Service Provider to carry out the work\nintended by the specifications and the duties required by the FIDIC general conditions of contract.\nTowards the latter part of the Administration and Monitoring phase, the Assistant Contract Engineer (ACE) (if\nany), shall be afforded the opportunity to act as Contract Engineer (CE) in order to gain experience in this\nposition, to assist in qualifying in this position in future contracts.\nThe duties of the Engineer shall be in accordance with the Employer’s standard requirements and shall, inter\nalia, include:\ni) Conduct meetings with affected communities and relevant forums, if necessary, to establish communi-\ncation channels and to determine issues impacting on the construction phase.\nii) Appointment of suitable, able and competent site staff, together with the administration of such staff\n(including those of any independent Service Provider/s as approved).\niii) Arranging and attending monthly technical and site meetings and keeping minutes of such meetings.\niv) Making arrangements on behalf of the Employer for the provision and reproduction of such drawings\nand documents as may be required by the contractor and site staff for the execution of the works.\nv) Fulfilling all functions and obligations stated or implied for the Engineer, and delegated to the Engineer’s\nRepresentative, in the Employer’s Particular Conditions of Contract, Standard Specifications or Scope\nof Works or any other document applicable to the control and administration of a works contract.\nvi) Issuing instructions to the Contractor, co-coordinating and generally inspecting the execution of the\nWorks for compliance with the contract at such intervals as appropriate for the purpose of the proper\ninspection of the works, directing site staff and delegating the detailed and day-to-day supervision/in-\nspection of the works and site administration.\nvii) Advising the Employer regarding the inspection and testing of such materials and plant as are usually\ninspected and tested and arranging for such inspection and testing to be carried out in accordance with\ncurrent appropriate codes, manuals and guidelines.\nviii) Arranging for the carrying out of performance or acceptance tests and surveys as required by the\nEmployer. With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard\nworkflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of acceptance testing required for each\nlot. The data will need to be uploaded into the SARDS portal by Service Provider as per the SARDS\ninterface and then analysed using SARDS.\nix) Ensure regular inspections by the design specialists for all work on the project.\nx) Approve all materials and ensure compliance of materials mix designs to the specifications. With the\nrelease of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS for approval of materials.\nxi) Actively manage, report and assist the Contractor on training, development and empowerment pro-\ngrammes committed to by the Contractor in relation to his tendered Contract Participation Goals.\nReporting and signing off on monthly basis the mentorship programme for Targeted Enterprises\nprovided by the Contractor and ensuring that all needs of the Targeted Enterprises are recorded monthly\nand dealt with as the need arises. The monthly report shall be agreed with the Targeted Enterprises\nwhich shall also be signed by each Targeted Enterprise undergoing mentorship. Monitor and report\nC3-74\n\nmonthly on training, empowerment, capacity building, development of Targeted Enterprises, labour and\nstaff returns, and any such aspects on the Employer’s ITIS Project Information Module. Separate\nallowance for payment for this duty has been made in the Pricing Schedule.\nxii) Monitor and report on conformance to all relevant Occupational Health and Safety legislation including\nregular internal audits to be conducted by the Service Provider’s nominated specialist, arrangement for\nvisits by the designer at identified critical phases of construction and recording/reporting of Section 24\nincidents.\nxiii) Monitor and report on conformance to all relevant Environmental legislation and requirements.\nxiv) Checking and controlling of quantities measured and agreed by the Contractor and the site supervisory\nstaff and settling any disputes that may arise in this regard. With the release of the SARDS, the Service\nProvider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS for controlling of quantities\nmeasured.\nxv) Issuing certificates electronically to the Employer for payment to the Contractor in accordance with the\nconditions of contract.\nxvi) Review and analyse claims and/or disputes or differences that may arise between the Employer and\nContractor. Present to the Employer the Engineer’s ruling on a claim and/or disputes. All duties with\nregard to claims and/or disputes will be paid for under Additional Duties.\nxvii) Issuing works authorisations, as agreed with and approved by the Employer.\nxviii) Provide the Employer with progress, monthly materials and laboratory and other reports on all aspects\nof material importance regarding the Works.\nxix) Identification of risks to the Employer under the Works Contract, as well as communicating mitigations\nmeasures to the Employer.\nxx) Perform bridge and/or culvert inspections on all major structures receiving upgrades or repairs under\nthe project before the Taking-Over Certificate. Any defects for which the contractor is responsible, shall\nform part of the contractor’s snag list for correction within the 28 (twenty-eight) day period allowed. The\nupdated inventory information and structures principle inspection data of these structures must be\ncaptured on the Employer’s ITIS system before the issue of the Taking-over Certificate of the works\nusing the ITIS desktop capturing system. Confirmation that all the data has been correctly captured in\nthe Employer’s ITIS system shall be confirmed in the Service Provider’s Construction Report. Failure to\nupdate the data in ITIS will result in penalties as specified in the Contract Data.\nxxi) Provide the Employer within 3 months of issuing the Taking-over Certificate of the works with such draft\nconstruction reports, materials as-built records, as-built plans in PDF format and all other documents\nnormally associated with contract administration. The final reports/records shall be provided within 1\nmonth of the issue of the Performance Certificate for the Works contract.\nxxii) Certain construction monitoring duties may be delegated to the Engineer’s representative (RE).\nxxiii) Receive the contractor’s proposed programme, analyse it, comment on it and when found acceptable\nwrite to the contractor stating that the programme is acceptable.\nxxiv) Conducting interim monthly performance evaluations on the Contractor and a final written and signed\nreport to be submitted to the CIDB as gazetted.\nxxv) Training of Assistant Contract Engineer (if applicable).\nxxvi) Declare structures (bridges, culverts, roads, sections of roads, etc.) safe for use in writing before it is\nopened for public use.\nxxvii) Capturing the tendered rates or balanced rates of the successful contractor and also the tendered rates\nor balanced rates of the contractor scoring the second highest points to the COTO rates database in\nITIS.\nb) Head office administration\nThe Service Provider shall supply sufficient head office administrative support to the site personnel to ensure\nefficient and timeous administration of the Works Contract.\nThe Service Provider shall furthermore be responsible for the safe keeping of all original Roadworks and\nStructural Drawings as well as all other documentation related to the different phases of the project, for a\nperiod of at least 5 (five) years after the defects notification period. No additional payment will be made for this.\nc) Occupational Health and Safety obligations\nThe Service Provider shall execute the duties of the Employer, as his appointed agent, as contemplated in the\nConstruction Regulations (2014) to the Occupational Health and Safety Act (Act No. 85 of 1993).\nThe Service Provider shall arrange, formally and in writing, for the Works Contractor to provide documentary\nevidence of compliance with all the requirements of the said Act. The Service Provider shall also carry out\nregular site audits to ensure compliance thereto. Should the Service Provider not have “in-house” capacity to\nundertake such duties, an external recognized specialist shall be appointed. This individual needs to be\nC3-75\n\nregistered with the SACPCMP as a Professional Construction Health and Safety Agent or a\nConstruction Health and Safety Manager. Provision for payment for all OH&S obligations during the\nconstruction phase has been included in the Pricing Schedule.\nd) Monthly Technical and Site Meetings\nThe appointed Engineer for the project shall visit the site at least 2 (two) times per month (excluding the\ncontractor’s annual shut-down period in December and January) on separate occasions. One visit shall be\nscheduled for the Technical Meeting with a thorough site inspection with the Contractor and the Engineer’s\nrepresentative. A second visit shall be scheduled for the monthly Site Meeting with a thorough site inspection\nwith the Contractor and Employer’s representative.\ne) Compilation of monthly materials and laboratory report\nThe appointed engineer shall be responsible for the compilation of monthly materials and laboratory reports.\nThese reports shall be in accordance with the Employer’s guideline documents. The site laboratory Service\nProvider must supply the necessary information to be included in the report, but it will be the sole responsibility\nof the engineer to ensure that the information provided is true and correct. These reports shall be submitted to\nthe Employer’s relevant Project Manager on a monthly basis, for the duration of the works contract. The reports\nshall be submitted in hard copy format. The reports shall reach the Employer by no later than the 7th of the\nmonth. No additional payment will be made for this.\nf) Site visits by design specialist(s)\nThe appointed design specialist(s) for the project shall visit the site to comply with the Construction Regulations\nof the Occupational Health and Safety Act (No 85 of 1993) as relevant for the design of the Works during the\nconstruction phase as well as the designer’s obligations with respect to the relevant professional engineer’s\nlegislation. Furthermore, the design specialist(s) shall as a minimum attend the construction of trial section\nincluding inter alia, stabilization trials, base construction trials and final surfacing trials. The design specialist\nfor structures shall visit the site at least once a month whilst construction of the new bridge and the widening\nof the existing is in progress and whilst remedial works to existing structures is being carried out. It is also\nexpected during the initial foundation stage of structures that the founding material (including for piles) for\nmajor culverts and bridges shall be inspected by an experienced geotechnical engineer. This means that for\nall major structures it is expected that the geotechnical engineer will do the initial approvals of foundation\nfounding material on a monthly basis or more often as required.\ng) Assistance at clarification meeting, tender period and tender evaluation of sub-contract packages\nThe Service Provider shall be required to assist the contractor with regards to the procurement of sub-\ncontractors to comply with Part D of the Construction Contract.\nThe specifications for the clarification meeting, tender period and tender evaluation (by an independent person)\nof sub-contract packages shall be similar to those in Clauses C3.6.2, C3.6.3 and C3.6.4, except for the\nEmployer’s procurement department involvement.\nAllowance has been made for the requirements in the Pricing Schedule under Additional Duties.\nh) Fulfilling the duties of the Senior Materials Technician/Materials Technician\nThe duties of the Senior Materials Technician/Materials Technician shall be in accordance with the Employer’s\nstandard requirements and shall, inter alia, include:\ni) Work in a close relationship with the laboratory including having regular discussions with the laboratory\nmanager about the condition, progress and standard of the laboratory;\nii) Conduct regular laboratory inspections and report and draft report;\niii) Act as liaison between the Engineer’s Representative and the laboratory;\niv) Advise the Engineer’s Representative where the laboratory does not comply with the contractual and\ntechnical requirements;\nv) Prepare and sign-off materials mix and seal designs or any other relevant matter for submission to the\nEngineer’s Representative;\nvi) Verify the requests for lot approval submitted by the contractor and ensure the tests requested are in\nline with the lot submitted; With the release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow\nthe standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and locality of acceptance testing\nrequired for each lot.\nvii) Ensure the laboratory is granted sufficient time for sampling with regards to every request received;\nC3-76\n\nviii) Keeps track of submission time for laboratory results; With the release of the SARDS, the Service\nProvider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS to determine the number and\nlocality of acceptance testing required for each lot;\nix) Receive and verify all the test results from the laboratory manager; With the release of the SARDS, the\nService Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS to verify all tests.\nx) Sign-off test reports and make recommendations for approval or rejection; With the release of the\nSARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS to approve\nor reject tests;\nxi) Inspect works daily which shall inter alia include materials sources, layer works, structures and any other\nmaterials related to items on site;\nxii) Do visual inspections on materials in lots submitted for approval;\nxiii) Inspect all trial sections constructed and complete as well as record trial section checklists;\nxiv) Populate progress sheets linking test requests with the position on layer works; With the release of the\nSARDS, Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS to identify test\nlocations.\nxv) Draft the monthly materials and laboratory reports;\nxvi) Keep As-Built data up to date; With the release of the SARDS, Service Provider will be required to follow\nthe standard workflow within SARDS to automatically update as-built data.\nC3.7.3 Establishment of supervisory personnel on site\na) Supervisory team\nThe Service Provider shall be required to provide a team on site to monitor, administer and measure the Works\nin accordance with the requirements of the Works Contract, Employer’s standard requirements and industry\ngood practice.\nProvision has been made in the Pricing Schedule for the envisaged staffing requirement. The establishment\nof such staff, however, shall be subject to the approval of the Employer. For the envisaged scope of the Works,\nthe supervisory team will consist of at least the following:\ni) Resident Engineer\nii) Assistant Resident Engineer/s\niii) Senior Materials Technician\niv) Materials Technician\nv) Trainee Technician\nvi) Surveyor - full time\nvii) Survey assistant/s\nviii) Trainee (student)\nix) Project Liaison Officer\nx) Administrative Assistants (data capture)\nxi) Mentors/Coaches for Targeted Enterprises (sub-contractors)\nxii) Independent Targeted Enterprises monitor\nThe minimum requirements for qualification and experience of the supervisory team are specified in Clause\nC3.1.11.\nProvision has been made in the Pricing Schedule to cover the total annual cost of employment (TACE) of the\nsupervisory staff which shall include the following:\ni) Basic salary.\nii) Other benefits not reflected in the basic salary, which may include:\n• normal annual bonus (maximum of one month’s salary or part thereof if contract is less than 1\n(one) year) but excluding any performance bonuses or merit bonuses;\n• consulting firm’s contribution to medical aid;\n• group life assurance, accident and disability insurance;\n• pension/provident fund contributions by the consulting firm;\n• allowances forming part of the remuneration package which are pensionable (car allowances,\netc.);\n• computer and cell phone allowance if part of package; and\n• other justifiable costs and allowances approved by the Employer.\niii) costs payable due to all applicable statutory requirements such as:\n• Workmen’s compensation fund contributions;\n• Unemployment insurance contributions; and\n• Other applicable statutory levies.\nC3-77\n\nA standardised site staff overhead factor of 1.44 will be applied to the TACE of the supervisory staff (excluding\nthe surveyor and his assistants, trainee (student) and Project Liaison Officer) which is made up as follows:\nDescription Site staff overheads\nSalaries (Technical) TACE 1.00\nSalaries (Non-technical) TACE 0.24\nTelephone and communication Tendered item\nRental of premises, electricity, water Provided by Client\nTransport not recovered from project Pay item\nPaper, stationary, consumables Tendered item\nAudit, bank charges, interest, insurance 0.08\nMarketing 0.02\nOffice equipment Tendered item\nTraining and development 0.02\nProject direct expenses not recoverable 0.08\nHead Office expenses Tendered item\nNet Overhead Factor before profit 1.44\nProvision has also been made in the Pricing Schedule for additional overhead costs related to the employment\nof the relevant personnel, which may include the following:\n• overtime by salaried professional and semi-professional staff (qualified Engineers, Technologists and\nTechnicians) and all such other staff for which overtime is not payable in terms of the Labour Act;\n• ordinary leave and sick leave (one month);\n• administration related to salaries, legislation, etc.; and\n• other overhead expenses and profit.\nReplacement of staff as a result of any extended period of leave or sick leave outside the normal contractor’s\nyear end break shall be to the approval of the Employer.\nThree (3) months prior to the commencement of the works contract the Service Provider shall submit for\nconsideration and approval to the Employer a detailed proposal in the Employer’s prescribed format which\nshall include a CV of appropriate experience and qualifications as well as a cost estimate (including salary\nadjustments/increases) for each required and/or proposed person on the Employer’s E1K and E2K forms. In\naddition, the Service Provider shall also complete Returnable Schedule Forms B1 and B2 for at least the\nResident Engineer (RE), the Senior Materials Technician (SMT) and the Materials Technician (MT) for\nevaluation. Amongst other evaluation criteria to be used, shall be the individual threshold of 85%, for each of\nthe above-mentioned site staff.\nThe annual salaries for those approved staff shall be substantiated by an auditor’s certificate at the start of the\nproject and whenever salary or staff changes occur thereafter. Prior approval is required for any salary\nadjustments/increases which shall be in line with CPI.\nSurvey services may be required on either a full time or part time basis as appropriate for the scope of the\nWorks.\nWhere provision has been made in the Pricing Schedule, the surveyor shall be appointed as a sub-Service\nProvider and procured directly by the Service Provider in terms of Clause C3.1.7. The Service Provider shall\nenter into a sub-service agreement with the surveyor. The terms of reference with regards to the survey service\non site shall be based on the requirement of the Employer as set out in Chapter 10 of Technical Methods for\nHighways TMH11: Standard Survey Methods.\nThe Employer shall be entitled to instruct the Service Provider to remove forthwith from the Works any person\nemployed by the Service Provider on or about the execution of the Works who, in the opinion of the Employer,\nmisconducts himself or is incompetent or negligent in the proper performance of his duties, or whose presence\non site is otherwise considered by the Employer, on reasonable grounds, to be undesirable.\nb) Relocation of site staff\nProvision has been made in the Pricing Schedule for the costs to relocate the supervisory team to site.\nc) Accommodation\nC3-78\n\nAppropriate housing for the supervisory team will be required to be provided by the Service Provider. A\nprovisional sum has been allowed in the Pricing Schedule for this purpose. Accommodation of the site staff\nshall be located as near to the Works as practically possible.\nIn the event that the contract period does not justify full relocation of staff and families, the Employer may\napprove temporary accommodation such as a bed & breakfast establishment in lieu of rented housing. In such\napproved instances, only the basic accommodation costs (excluding meals) will be payable under the Works\nContract. Any allowances for services, etc. shall be approved beforehand by the Employer.\nWhere site staff elect to occupy their own housing and approved by Employer, payment will be made in\naccordance with the table below.\nTable C3.7.3: Monthly accommodation allowance rates (April 2022)\nAREA IN WHICH HOUSING UNIT IS LOCATED\nANNUAL BASIC SALARY\nOTHER METROPOLITAN AREAS\nUp to – R130 000 R 3 628 R 4 180\nR130 000 – R260 000 R 4 597 R 5 494\nR260 000 – R500 000 R 5 635 R 7 061\nR500 000 and over R 6 731 R 7 987\nNOTE:\n• The amounts in this table are fixed for 1 (one) year only (April-March of each year) regardless of the\nStage of the Project. The Employer may publish revised amounts on a yearly basis.\nd) Establishment of site office\nProvision for the erection and maintenance of a site office building and all related services will be made under\nthe Works Contract. The Service Provider shall, however, provide sufficient office equipment to perform all\nrequired duties for the monitoring of the Works Contract. This shall, inter alia, include the following:\n• all cell phones,\n• safety equipment in accordance with the OHS requirements, e.g. safety jackets, rotating amber lights,\nsafety boots, etc.\n• photo copiers, fax machines, modems, personal computers and printers (including all hardware and\nsoftware)\n• consumables and stationery\n• digital camera\nOne (1) landline, including rental, call and data costs for work related office and fax usage shall be provided\nthrough the Works Contract. Should fixed connectivity not be available mobile connectivity with data shall be\nprovided through the Works Contract.\nProvision has been made in the Pricing Schedule for supplying the equipment as well as the monthly\noperational costs thereof.\nIn addition, the Resident Engineer as his/her assistants shall be equipped with mobile phones for the\nEmployer’s ITIS mobile application, with the following minimum specification:\n• Operating system: Android or higher\n• Camera resolution: 5 Meg pixels or greater – 8MP recommended\n• Screen resolution: 480 x 800 pixels or greater\n• GPS facility: Yes, with geo-tagging for images\n• Data connection: 3G or greater\nC3.7.4 Monitoring of the Works Contract\nThe Service Provider shall ensure that all the work required under the Works Contract is carried out in\naccordance with the requirements of the Works Contract and current best practice and shall include effective\nC3-79\n\nfinancial control. Construction monitoring shall be carried out in a pro-active manner and in accordance with\nthe Employer’s standard requirements and current best practice.\nThe Service Provider shall be responsible for the normal duties associated with the management and\nsupervision of a works contract, which duties shall, inter alia, include:\n• Monitoring and reporting of the Contractor’s programme.\n• Implement Engineer’s quality control plan.\n• Monitor Contractor’s quality control plan.\n• Site audits, inspection, quality control testing, approval, rejection of work. With the release of the\nSARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within SARDS with regard\nto quality control.\n• Measurement and certification of completed work inclusive of cash flow forecasts.\n• Management of ITIS software which includes the following modules:\n- contract module: contract management\n- project information module: uploading of data including personnel and employment data\n- structures module: update inventory report of the structures\n• Provide a final cost estimate for the work contract on a monthly basis, including estimated costs of\nclaims.\n• Regular Site Meetings with Contractor and Employer.\n• Monitoring of the third party claims.\n• Monitoring and reporting of Contractor’s CPG commitments through ITIS.\n• Supervision of traffic accommodation arrangements.\n• Statutory control functions, which may include investigations into the legality of services and accesses\nwithout formal wayleave agreements and assist with the application for approval where required.\n• Attend Project Liaison Committee (PLC) and Project Management Team (PMT) and all other meetings\nrelating to the Targeted Enterprises and Targeted Labour.\n• Monitoring and reporting of the project’s EMP and NEMA requirements.\n• Implement the Engineers requirements in terms of compliance with the OH&S Act.\n• Monitor the Contractor’s compliance with the OH&S Act.\n• Compile all reports and as-built data in accordance with the Employer's standard requirements. With\nthe release of the SARDS, the Service Provider will be required to follow the standard workflow within\nSARDS with regard to as-built data capture.\n• Perform a complete bridge and/or culvert inspection according to the Employer’s requirements on all\nstructures that underwent upgrading, rehabilitation or repairs (excluding replacement of bridge joints).\nSuch inspection(s) should be performed by a SANRAL accredited bridge or culvert inspector for bridges\nand major culverts respectively. A fully updated inventory report of the structure(s) must be electronically\ncaptured online, using the prescribed capturing tool that can be downloaded from the Employer’s\nwebsite, on the Employer’s ITIS system. For Capex projects all bridges and major culverts must be\ninspected and approved by the Employer before the issue of Taking-over-Certificate. This includes\nbridges and major culverts even if work was not done on the structures. Those structures that have been\ndemolished shall be marked as demolished. The full list of structures is as listed in the Appendix. Any\nstructures that are not on the list shall also be inspected, including new structures.\nSeparate allowances have been made for the duties during an extended Works Contract duration or during\nprolonged site stoppages (i.e. Business Rescue or Community unrest).\nC3.7.5 Transport for site supervisory staff and additional services\nThe Service Provider shall provide sufficient appropriate vehicles on site to carry out the duties as specified in\nclause C3.7.4 and C3.7.6. Only travel in the execution of these duties, as well as any other travel necessary\nas a result of any additional duties as ordered by the Employer, shall be claimable. Estimated travel costs as\na result of week-end travel by site staff to their place of permanent residence shall be approved beforehand\nby the Employer.\nTravel log sheets for each vehicle utilised shall be certified by the Engineer and included under cover of the\npayment certificates submitted to the Employer. The kilometre rate for all travel in excess of 3000 ……….\nkilometres per vehicle per calendar month shall be reduced and paid for at 60% of the rate.\nThe vehicle type for all supervisory staff (including PLO) shall be limited to a Category A and B with an 1800\nEngine Volume cc, except for the surveyor, who shall be limited to a 2501 Engine Volume cc. In exceptional\ncases the Employer may consider a different category vehicle and/or engine volume.\nFuel rates can be downloaded monthly by following the link:\nC3-80\n\nhttps://www.transport.gov.za/web/department-of-transport/roads.\nC3.7.6 Project Liaison Officer (PLO)\nThe PLO is a person who acts as the liaison officer for the PLC and PMT.\nThe PLO shall facilitate the employment of Targeted Labour and shall coordinate communication between the\nPMT and the PLC to address the day-to-day project, Stakeholder and community matters that impact on the\nparties to the project.\nThe Standard TOR for PLOs requires of the PLO to execute specific duties during each stage of the project,\ni.e. from project initiation to project completion. Some of these duties overlap project stages and hence, a full\ndescription is provided here.\nThe PLO shall execute the following duties:\ni) Except for taking the minutes of PLC meetings, which is a duty of the Engineer or his representative,\nthe PLO shall provide a secretarial function to the PLC which includes, amongst others, the following:\na. Schedule meetings;\nb. Compile meeting agendas;\nc. Compile document packages for meetings;\nd. Distribute minutes of meetings;\ne. Assist PLC to formulate their communication in writing;\nf. Distribute written communication to and from the PMT and the PLC;\ng. Keep records of all the above and any other PLC documentation; and\nh. Provide any other reasonable secretariat function pertaining to the PLC.\nii) Attend all PLC meetings to report on the day-to-day project, Stakeholder and community matters that\nimpact on the parties to the project.\niii) Attend all monthly site meetings to report on the day-to-day project, Stakeholder and community matters\nthat impact on the parties to the project.\niv) Attend any other meetings related to the project and in which any of the project Stakeholders, affected\ncommunities, Local/Targeted Labour and Local/Targeted Enterprises are involved.\nv) Maintain a full-time presence on site to monitor and address the day-to-day project, Stakeholder and\ncommunity matters that impact on the parties to the project.\nvi) Maintain a full-time presence on site to assist the PMT in the day-to-day liaison with project Stakeholders\nand affected communities. Typical information to be disseminated by the PLO includes:\na. Basic scope of works and how it will affect the community;\nb. Project programme and regular progress opportunities;\nc. Anticipated employment and sub-contracting opportunities;\nd. Project programme as it pertains to the employment of Targeted Labour and sub-contracting of\nTargeted Enterprises;\ne. Occupational Health and Safety precautions; and\nf. Any other information relevant to project Stakeholders and the affected communities.\nvii) Be well acquainted with the contractual requirements as it pertains to Targeted Labour employment and\ntraining requirements.\nviii) Assist the PMT and PLC to establish and agree the eligibility and selection criteria to be followed when\nemploying Targeted Labour.\nix) Assist the PMT in its resources and skills audits by providing a coordinating function between the PMT,\nproject Stakeholders and the affected communities.\nx) Ensure that Targeted Labour databases, compiled from the resources and skills audits, are based on\nthe agreed eligibility and selection criteria and that it is updated as and when required.\nxi) Coordinate the selection and employment of Targeted Labour based on the agreed eligibility and\nselection criteria and based on the Contractor’s labour requirements.\nxii) Ensure that each labourer enters into an employment contract which adheres to current and relevant\nlabour legislation.\nxiii) Ensure that each labourer understands the conditions of his/her employment with an emphasis on the\nemployment start date, end date and wages payable.\nxiv) Identify and inform the PMT of any relevant training required by the Targeted Labour.\nxv) Attend all disciplinary proceedings to ensure that hearings are fair and conducted in accordance to the\ncurrent and relevant labour legislation.\nxvi) Be pro-active in identifying PLC, project Stakeholder, affected communities (including Targeted Labour\nand/or Targeted Enterprise sub-contractor), requirements, disputes, unrest, strikes, etc. and bring it to\nthe attention of the PMT.\nxvii) Assist the PMT to liaise with the PLC, project Stakeholders and the affected communities to resolve any\ndisputes, which occurs due to the project.\nC3-81\n\nxviii) Other than the document records to be kept as mentioned in (i) above, keep record of all other\ndocuments and processes pertaining to the employment of Targeted Labour and any other records that\nmay be of relevance to the functions of the PLC.\nxix) Produce and submit a monthly report to the PMT and the PLC-on-PLC meetings, other meetings\nattended by the PLO, Targeted Labour employment, and project Stakeholder, affected community and\nany other project matters that impact on the parties to the project.\nC3-82\n\nC3.7.7 adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(b) Structures (bridges and other major structures):\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(c) Electrical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(d) Buildings:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(e) Mechanical:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(f) Electronic:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee adjustment* lump sum (LS)\n(g) Other:\n(i) Fee (based on Employer’s estimated Cost of the Works) lump sum (LS)\n(ii) Fee
