The monitoring of risk\nassessments shall be formal, and records thereof shall be available for audit purposes.\nc) Review of Risk Assessment\nThe contractor shall review the hazard identification, risk assessments and standard safe working\nprocedures prior to any work activity commencement and at each production planning and progress\nreport meeting as the contract work develops and progresses and each time changes are made to the\nC3-136\n\ndesigns, plans and construction methods and processes. The contractor shall provide the Employer,\nsub-contractors and all other concerned parties with copies of any changes, alterations or amendments\nas contemplated above.\nActivities carried out without conducting a risk assessment or found to be non-compliant with the risk\nassessment, will be stopped until such time a risk assessment is compiled, and work is carried out\naccording to the risk assessment.\nRisk assessments must be fully communicated to all relevant personnel and must be considered when\nestablishing training, awareness and competency requirements.\nd) Baseline Risk Assessment\nSANRAL will prepare a Baseline Risk Assessment from which the Health and Safety Specifications for\nthe project will be prepared. The Baseline Risk Assessment will highlight all work for which the\nContractor must prepare safe work procedures and or work method statements. In this case the Baseline\nRisk Assessment will not identify risks or control measures, this must be identified by Contractor when\npreparing the Issue Based Risk Assessments.\nThe Baseline Risk Assessment for this Project can be found on page 17 Section 19.1 of this document.\ne) Continuous Risk Assessment\nThe Contractor shall continuously assess the risks of the activities that are carried out. Risk assess-\nments must be in writing, site specific and must be reviewed continuously to ensure it is current and it\naddress all the relevant hazards and risks associated with the specific activity at the specific site.\nThe Risk assessment must be discussed with the whole work crew before the activity starts and the\nwork crew must acknowledge in writing having discussed the risk assessment and that they understand\nit. This acknowledgement must be on site and must be available to the client for audit purposes.\n1. LEGAL COMPLIANCE & DOCUMENT CONTROL\nThe contractor is required to implement systems and procedures to ensure legal compliance through:\n- Identification of all relevant HSE legislation, standards and codes applicable to its operations.\n- Have available copies of all relevant HSE legislation, standards and codes for reference purposes.\n- Update legislation, standards and codes with any changes\n- Communicate to all employees any changes that may affect their accountabilities and conformances\n- Incorporate any legal requirements into their HSE management system.\n- Monitor and review their HSE management system for effectiveness.\nThe contractor shall, as a minimum, comply with:\n- The Occupational Health and Safety Act and Regulations (Act 85 of 1993), an up-to-date copy of which\nshall be available on site at all times.\n- The Compensation for Occupational Injuries and Diseases Act (Act 130 of 1993), an up-to-date copy of\nwhich shall be available on site at all times.\n- Where work is being carried out on a “mine”, the contractor shall comply with the Mines Health and\nSafety Act and Regulations (Act 29 of 1960) and any other OH&S requirements that the mine may\nspecify. An up-to-date copy of the Mines Health and Safety Act and Regulations shall be available on\nsite at all times.\nWherever in the Construction Regulations or this specification there is reference to other regulations (e.g.\nConstruction Regulation 24: Electrical Installations and Machinery on Construction Sites) the contractor shall\nbe conversant with and shall comply with these regulations.\n11.1 Legal Appointments\nAll legal appointments of the Contractor regarding the Health and Safety of his employees who are to\nwork on the project are addressed and governed by the OHS Act and applicable Regulations. Legal\nappointments must be in place and must reflect in the project safety file before work commences.\ni. Overall Supervision and Responsibility for OH&S\nC3-137\n\nSANRAL will appoint the Principal Contractor in terms of Construction Regulation 5(1)(k). A\nMandatory agreement as per Section 37.2 of the OHS Act, shall be signed between SANRAL and\nthe Principal Contractor.\nIt is a requirement that the Principal Contractor, when he appoints other contractors in terms of\nConstruction Regulations 7(1)(c), 7(1)(d), 7(1)(f) and 7(3) includes in his agreement with such\nContractors the following:\n• OH&S Act (85 of 1993), Section 37(2) agreement: “Agreement with Mandatory”\n• OH&S Act (85 of 1993), Section 16(2) appointee(s) as detailed in his/her/their respective\nappointment forms. (Where applicable)\nThe signed Mandatory agreements shall be placed in the project file for reference and for audit\ntrail purposes.\nii. Specific Supervision Responsibilities for OH&S\nThe contractor shall appoint designated competent employees and/or other competent persons\nas required by the OHS Act and Regulations, as well as this specification. Appointments shall be\nin writing and the responsibilities clearly stated together with the period for which the appointment\nis made. This information shall be communicated to and agreed with the appointees. Where\napplicable, the training certificate must be attached to the appointment. Notice of appointments\nshall be submitted to the Employer. All changes shall also be communicated to the Employer.\nBelow is a list of possible appointments for the project, which is not an all-inclusive list, but for\nreference purposes only:\nAppointment Legal Reference\nAssistant to CEO OHS Act 16(2)\nHealth and Safety Representative OHS Act 17(1)\nNominated Health and Safety Committee Member OHS Act 19(3)\nContractor (Sub-contractor) CR 7(1)(c)(v)\nConstruction Manager & Alternate Construction Manager CR 8 (1)\nAssistant Construction Manager CR 8(2)\nHealth and Safety Officer CR 8(5)\nConstruction Supervisor CR 8(7)\nAssistant Construction Supervisor CR 8(8)\nRisk Assessor CR 9(1)\nFall Protection Plan Developer CR 10(1)(a)\nStructure Inspector CR 11(2)(a)\nTemporary Works Designer CR 12(1)\nTemporary Works Supervisor CR 12(2)\nExcavation Supervisor CR 13(1)(a)\nDemolition Supervisor CR 14(1)\nCompetent Person in the use of Explosives CR 14(11)\nScaffold Supervisor CR 16(1)\nSuspended Platform Supervisor CR 17(1)\nRope Access Supervisor CR 18(1)(a)\nMaterial Hoist Inspector CR 19(8)(a)\nBulk Mixing Plant Supervisor CR 20(1)\nExplosive actuated fastening device Inspector CR 21(2)(b)\nExplosive actuated fastening device cartridge Controller CR 21(2)(g)(i)\nConstruction Vehicle & Mobile Plant Operator Authorised CR 23(1)(d)(i)\nTemporary Electrical Installation Controller CR 24(c)\nStacking and Storage Supervisor CR 28(a)\nC3-138\n\nAppointment Legal Reference\nFire Equipment Inspector CR 29(h)\nIncident investigator GAR 9(2)\nLifting tackle inspector DMR 18(10)(e)\nLadder inspector GSR 13(a)\nCertified Explosives Manager ER 12(1)\nFirst Aider GSR GSR 3(4)\nHazardous Chemical Substance Supervisor\nIn addition to the above, the Employer requires that a Traffic Safety Officer be appointed.\nIt is a requirement that the contractor shall provide the Employer with an organogram of all sub-\ncontractors that he/she has appointed or intends to appoint and keep this list updated and\nprominently displayed on site.\niii. Designation of OH&S Representatives (Section 17 of the OH&S Act)\nWhere the contractor employs more than 20 persons (including the employees of sub-contractors)\nhe has to appoint 1 (one) OH&S representative for every 50 employees or part thereof. This is a\nminimum (legal) requirement. The contractor may at his own discretion appoint more OH&S repre-\nsentatives according to site specific requirements. General Administrative Regulation 6 requires\nthat the appointment or election of the OH&S representatives be conducted in consultation with\nemployee representatives or employees (Section 17 of the Act and General Administrative Re-\ngulation 6 & 7). OH&S representatives shall be designated in writing and the designation shall\ninclude the area of responsibility of the person and term of the designation. OH&S representatives\nmust be experienced, permanently employed by the contractor or his sub-contractors, trained and\nable to move freely within their designated area of responsibility.\niv) Duties and Functions of the OH&S Representatives (Section 18 of the OH&S Act)\nThe contractor shall ensure that the designated OH&S representatives perform their functions in\nrespect of the workplace or section of the workplace for which they have been appointed. These\nfunctions include conduct continuous monitoring and monthly inspections of their respective areas\nof responsibility, focusing on unsafe acts and unsafe conditions and report thereon to the\ncontractor. OH&S representatives shall participate in accident or incident investigations. OH&S\nrepresentatives shall attend all OH&S committee meetings. The complete list of functions can be\nfound in Section 18 of the OHS Act.\nv) Appointment of OH&S Committee (Sections 19 and 20 of the OH&S Act)\nThe contractor shall establish an OH&S committee, which shall meet at least once a month, where\ntwo or more Health and Safety Representatives have been appointed. OH&S representatives\nmust be appointed as OH&S committee members. The number of management appointed\nmembers may not exceed the number of OH&S representatives on the committee.\n1. OPERATIONAL INTEGRITY\nThe operational integrity of plant, equipment, structures and protective systems must be monitored and\nassured on an ongoing basis throughout the project cycle. Hazards must be identified, assessed and as far as\nreasonably practicable, eliminated or the risks treated to as low as reasonably practicable (ALARP).\n12.1 Plant & Equipment Integrity\n12.1.1 Construction Plant & Equipment\nThe Contractor shall maintain all items of plant and equipment necessary to perform the work in\na safe condition.\nSANRAL reserves the right to inspect items of plant and equipment brought to site and used on\nsite by the contractor. Should it be found that any item is inadequate, faulty, unsafe or in any other\nway unsuitable for the safe and satisfactory execution of the work for which it is intended, the\nC3-139\n\ncontractor will be advised of such observation / inspection, and the contractor shall be required\nto repair, make safe or remove such item from operation and replace it with a safe and adequate\nsubstitute.\nThe contractor shall ensure that all plant, equipment, and power tools that are brought onto and\nused on site are:\n- Appropriate for the type of work to be performed.\n- Placed on a register and inspected by a competent person or the authorized operator\nbefore use, daily or monthly dependent on Legislation and project requirements.\n- Record inspection findings on a register that must be kept on site.\n- The inspection register shall reflect the serial number of the plant, equipment or power tool.\n- Maintained and used in accordance with the manufacturer’s recommendations.\n- Have adequate machine guarding fitted to all exposed rotating or moving parts, as\nreasonably practicable, that have the potential to cause harm.\n- All electrical power supply units are protected with operational earth leakage devices.\n- Any defective, damaged or sub-standard equipment must be marked as unsafe for use and\nremoved from operation as soon as possible.\n12.1.2 Standards and Registers\nAs standard project procedures, the contractor is expected to:\n- Set up an initial set of registers as per the requirements of the OHS Act and Regulations.\n- Complete the registers for each piece of plant, tool and equipment brought on and used on\nsite.\n- Maintain a complete, continuous and comprehensive inspection and service history in\nthese registers or checklists.\n- Ensure daily, weekly, monthly inspections are done and recorded for all plant, tools &\nequipment by a competent person as required by the OHS Act and Regulations.\n- Have the inspection and maintenance records available for audit purposes.\n1. OCCUPATION HEALTH & HYGIENE\n13.1 Medical Fitness for Duty\nAll contractor employees shall undergo medical examinations and be certified fit for duty by an Occu-\npational Health Practitioner before they are allowed to work on site.\nThe medical certificate must be in the form of Annexure 3 of the Construction Regulations and stipulate\nthe possible exposures the employee might be exposed to during the execution of the project.\nIt is recommended and in the best interest of the Contractor to implement pre-employment as well as\nexit medical surveillance, especially with regards to Section 8 of the Noise Induced Hearing Loss\nRegulation.\n13.2 First Aid\nAccording to GSR 3(4), where more than 10 employees are employed at a workplace/worksite, the\nContractor shall ensure that there is at least one trained first aider for every group of 50 employees at\nthe workplace/site. First Aid boxes must be provided where more than 5 employees are employed and\nmust be readily available and accessible for the treatment of injured persons at the workplace.\nTo ensure immediate treatment of an injured person, it is recommended that all work crews have at\nleast one trained first aider, with a fully stocked first aid box, irrespective of the number of people in the\nwork crew. This is especially important when contractors work at great distances from the nearest\nemergency facility or town. These persons shall be appointed in writing as the first aiders with their\ncertificates attached as proof of competency.\nThe minimum contents of the first aid box shall be as per the supplied list in the General Safety\nRegulations.\nAll treatments done must be recorded on a register and kept with the first aid box. A trained and\nappointed first aider must be responsible for the first aid box and its content. Used content must be\nreplenished as soon as possible.\nC3-140\n\nIn order to ensure prompt response at the emergency facility it is recommended that the W.Cl 2 forms\nbe partially completed with the employers’ details.\n13.3 Hygiene Facilities\nThe Contractor shall ensure compliance to Section 30 of the Construction Regulations with regards to\nfacilities on the construction site as well as where accommodation is provided to employees on remote\nsites. The contractor shall ensure that the facilities are kept clean at all times, either through a Service\nProvider or self-employed persons.\n13.4 Health related Epidemics and Pandemics\nThe contractor shall, as far as reasonably practicable describe in his health and safety plan how health\nrelated epidemics and pandemics will be dealt with. The employer is aware that this section in the health\nand safety plan will not speak to specifics, but generic procedures. The Contractor must ensure that the\nrequirements stipulated in the Hazardous Biological Agents (HBA) Regulation are adhered to and in\nparticular the following as described in the mentioned Regulation:\n• Risk Assessment and risk assessment reviews;\n• Prevention measures;\n• Response measures;\n• Employee training / information sharing;\n• Employee health monitoring;\n• Management of infected persons;\n• Isolation rooms;\n• Employee transportation;\n• Employee accommodation;\n• Eating facilities;\n• Meetings / toolbox talks / Daily safety talks;\n• Cleaning of offices / facilities;\n• Duties of person that may be exposed to HBA’s\n• Monitoring exposure at the workplace\n• Medical surveillance of employees\n• Keeping of records\n• Personal Protective Equipment and facilities\n• Maintenance of control measures and facilities\nOnce the nature and scale of the epidemic or pandemic is known, the Contractor must update his health\nand safety plan with the relevant information and send the updated plan to the relevant appointed OHS\nAgent for approval. Once approved, the Contractor must implement the updated health and safety plan\nand maintain the updated plan on site.\n1. WASTE MANAGEMENT\nThe contractor shall comply with all applicable and relevant Waste management legislation, as well as muni-\ncipal bylaws applicable to waste management.\nThe contractor shall remove all waste generated at the construction site on a daily basis or as soon as possible\nafter generation to ensure good housekeeping at all times. The contractor shall have a waste management\nplan which must be implemented on the construction site, and which will have the objective to ensure that\nwaste is managed according to the Waste Management Hierarchy:\n- Reduce what you can. If you cannot reduce then,\n- Re-use what you can. If you cannot re-use then,\n- Recycle what you can. What you cannot recycle,\n- Convert into energy sources. If it cannot be converted to an energy source,\n- Dispose of in a landfill – this is only to be done as a last resort and disposed without endangering human\nhealth and without using processes or methods which could harm the environment.\n1. HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE MANAGEMENT\nThe contractor shall ensure that hazardous substances brought onto site are easily identifiable and stored\naccording to the requirements of the General Safety Regulations, GNR. 1031 of 1986, Section 4.\nC3-141\n\nWhere flammable liquids are being used or stored, this must be done in a manner which would not cause a\nfire or explosion hazard.\nThe contractor shall have Material Safety Data Sheets (MSDS) readily available for flammable, hazardous and\ntoxic chemical substances and materials brought onto site and shall ensure that his employees are trained in\nthese MSDS’s.\nFlammable, hazardous or toxic chemical substances may not be stored in empty food or drink containers.\nEmpty flammable, hazardous and toxic containers must be disposed of in a safe manner, which will prevent\nfurther use of such a container.\nAsbestos\nA survey of the construction site must be done during site establishment, to locate any asbestos. Should\nasbestos be located, the conditions of the Asbestos Regulations, GNR. 155 of 2002 must be followed and\ncomplied with.\n1. CONTRACTORS\n16.1 Consultations, Communications and Liaison\nOH&S liaison between the Employer, the contractor, the subcontractors, the designer and other\nconcerned parties will be through the OH&S committee. In addition to the above, communication may\nbe directly to the Employer or his appointed agent, verbally or in writing, as and when the need arises.\nConsultation with the workforce on OH&S matters will be through their construction managers and\nsupervisors, OH&S representatives and the OH&S committee. The contractor shall be responsible for\nthe dissemination of all relevant OH&S information to the sub-contractors e.g. design changes agreed\nwith the Employer and the designer, instructions by the Employer and/or his/her agent, exchange of\ninformation between sub-contractors, the reporting of hazardous/dangerous conditions/situations, etc.\nThe contractors’ most senior manager on site shall be required to attend all OH&S meetings.\n16.2 Operational Procedures\nEach construction activity shall be assessed by the contractor to identify operational procedures that will\nmitigate against the occurrence of an incident during the execution of each activity. This specification\nrequires the contractor:\n- to be conversant with all relevant Regulations;\n- to comply with their provisions;\n- to include them in his OH&S plan where relevant\n16.3 Checking, Reporting and Corrective Actions\ni. Monthly Audit by Employer (Construction Regulation 5(1)(o)\nThe Employer will conduct monthly health and safety and document verification audits in\ncompliance with Construction Regulation 5(1)(o) in order to ensure that the contractor has\nimplemented and is maintaining the agreed and approved OH&S plan.\nii. Other Audits and Inspections by the Employer\nThe Employer reserves the right to conduct other ad-hoc audits and inspections as deemed\nnecessary. This will include site safety walks.\niii. Contractor’s Audits and Inspections\nThe contractor must conduct his own regular internal audits to verify compliance with his own\nOH&S management system, as well as with this specification. The contractor shall furthermore\nensure that each sub-contractor`s health & safety plan is being implemented by conducting\nperiodic audits at intervals mutually agreed between the contractor and sub-contractors, but at\nleast once per month.\niv. Inspections by OH&S Representatives and other Appointees\nC3-142\n\nOH&S representatives shall conduct weekly inspections of their areas of responsibility and report\nthereon to their foreman or supervisor whilst other appointees shall conduct inspections and\nreport thereon as specified in their appointments e.g. vehicle, plant and machinery drivers,\noperators and users must conduct daily inspections before start-up.\nv. Recording and Review of Inspection Results\nAll the results of the above-mentioned inspections shall be in writing, reviewed at OH&S\ncommittee meetings, endorsed by the chairman of the meeting and placed on the OH&S File.\n16.4 Project Health and Safety Management Plan\nAs per Section 5(1)(l) and Section 7(1)(a) of the Construction Regulations of 2014, the contractor shall\ndevelop, implement and administer a Health and Safety Management Plan. The plan shall be in writing\nand shall be negotiated between the Contractor and SANRAL or designated OHS Agent and must be\napproved by SANRAL or the designated OHS Agent prior to the commencement of work on site. The\nplan shall demonstrate management’s commitment to ensure employee health and safety as their\nprimary objective during the contract. As a suggestion, the following elements may be used to develop\nthe H&S plan:\nIntroduction\nMission\nPurpose & Scope\nHealth, Safety and Environmental Policy\nHealth, Safety and Environmental Goals\nPlan Objectives\nLeadership and Commitment\nValues supporting commitment\nRoles, Responsibilities and Accountability\nHazard and Risk Management Process\nEffective Consultation\nPlanning\nContractor HSE Alignment\nSub-Contractors\nOn-Site\nLearning and Competency\nProject HSE Training and Competency Requirements\nContractor & Sub-Contractor Duties\nMinimum Training Requirements\nMedical and Induction\nEmployee details\nVisitors to site\nInduction\nInvolvement, Communication and Motivation\nSafety Meetings\nHealth & Safety Behaviour\nInformation and Learning\nHazard and Risk Management on site\nHazardous Activities\nHazardous Areas\nHierarchy of Hazard Control\nHazard and Risk Identification\nRisk Analysis and Evaluation\nDocumented safe work procedures for hazardous activities\nHazard and Risk monitoring plan\nHazard and Risk review plan\nOccupational Health and Hygiene\nFitness for Work\nC3-143\n\nHazardous Substances\nAirborne Chemical Substances\nNoise and Vibration\nPersonal Hygiene\nProtection of Outdoor Workers\nOccupational Health Services on Site\nPerformance Tracking and Accountability\nPositive Performance Indicators\nWorkplace Observations and Audits\nReporting\nIncident Management\nEmergency Preparedness and Response\nIncident Management\nInjury Management\nWaste Management\nHazardous Waste\nNon Hazardous Waste - Recyclable\nNon Hazardous Waste – Non recyclable\n16.5 Project Health and Safety File\nThe contractor shall compile a project specific Health and Safety File that consist of all the relevant\nproject specific documentation. The Health and Safety file may consist of multiple files, which when\ncombined should contain all the required documentation.\nIt is recommended that the project specific Health and Safety file contain at least the following:\n- Scope and summary of the project as well as any scope changes.\n- Notification of Construction Work to DoL / Copy of Work Permit\n- Proof of COID registration (Letter of Good Standing)\n- Contractor Health and Safety Policy statement signed by management\n- Mandatory Agreement – OH&S Act 37.2 (Between Employer and Principal Contractor)\n- Signed Client Health and Safety specification\n- Latest copy of the OHS Act end Regulations\n- Company Organogram depicting Health and Safety Responsibilities, including sub-contractors\n- Employee list including copy of IDs and medicals\n- Project specific Health and Safety Management Plan agreed with the Employer – see point 16.4\nabove\n- Relevant OH&S Legal appointments which includes duties and responsibilities as well as\ncompetencies (training certificate)\n- Copies of minutes of meetings - OH&S committee and other relevant OH&S meeting minutes\n- Designs/drawings (Construction Regulation 7(1)(e)\n- Site specific Fall Protection Plan (if applicable)\n- Risk Assessments\n- Contractor Induction material\n- Waste management Plan\n- Emergency preparedness (first aid, firefighting, emergency plan, etc.)\n- Emergency Contact Telephone numbers\n- HIV awareness program\n- List of hazardous chemical substances used on site\n- Material Safety Data Sheets of hazardous chemicals on site\n- List of plant & equipment to be used on site\n- Inspection Checklists/Registers of plant & equipment and emergency equipment\n- List of Sub-contractors including type of work\n- Sub-contractor 37.2 Mandatary Agreements\n- Sub-contractor appointments which shall include the type of work the contractor is appointed for.\n16.6 Contracting Philosophy\nAny site-specific hazards and safety management expectations will be made known to the Contractor\nprior to the work commencing on site. Legal OHS requirements contained in the OHS Act and\nRegulations as well as SANS Codes are the minimum requirements the Contractor must apply during\nC3-144\n\nthis contract with regards to Occupational Health and Safety. The Contractor shall apply, implement and\nenforce the minimum OHS Act & Regulations and SANS Codes requirements.\n16.7 Workers Compensation Registration\nThe Contractor shall ensure that his employees are covered for any occupational injuries and illnesses\nin terms of the Occupational Injuries and Diseases Act 130 of 1993, which cover shall remain in place\nand up to date for the duration of the project.\n16.8 HSE Non-Compliance\nIt is a legal duty of the client according to the Construction Regulation 5(1)(q) that a contractor is stopped\nfrom executing any activity which poses a threat to the health and safety of persons. Depending on the\nseriousness of the non-compliance only the specific activity may be stopped until the non-compliance is\nrectified or the whole operation may be stopped.\nIt is also the duty of every employee to take reasonable care of his own health and safety and of other\npersons who may be affected by his acts as per OHS Act, Section 14(a). Keeping this in mind, it is\nrequired of the contractor to ensure his employees has the right to remove themselves from any unsafe\nsituation or work activity, without any negative consequence to them until such time as the contractor\nhas made the unsafe situation or activity as safe as practicable possible.\n16.9 Indemnity by Contractor\nThe Contractor shall indemnify the Employer against and from all damages, losses and expenses\n(including legal fees and expenses) resulting from:\n(a) the loss of output and delay caused by the slowing down or partial or total stoppage of work\ncaused by:\ni. all or any of the Contractor’s workforce as a result of a dispute between all or any of the\nContractor’s workforce and the Contractor; or\nii. all or any of the Contractor’s suppliers’ difficulty or impossibility to deliver goods or materials\nneeded to perform the Works;\n(b) any unlawful, riotous or disorderly conduct by or amongst the Contractor’s personnel.”\n16.10 Contractor Conduct\nGuidelines to the most important rules that shall be implemented and maintained by the Contractor:\n- Complete compliance to the OH&S Act and Regulations\n- Hazard identification and Risk Assessments for all activities\n- Daily communication of DSTI talk before work commences\n- Safe access and egress to and from work areas.\n- Compulsory use of lifelines, Safety Harnesses and Fall Arrestors (Lanyards to be attached at all\ntimes)\n- Scaffold shall comply with Legal and SANS standards at all times\n- Good housekeeping and stacking practices\n- Safe lifting, rigging and slinging practices\n- Complying to Legal standards for lifting machinery & equipment\n- No lifting in wind conditions exceeding 30km/h (This is a guide and is dependent on risk\nassessments)\n- Securing of tools, equipment and material at heights\n- Wearing of appropriate personal protective equipment as identified in the risk assessment\nPersonal Conduct\nSupervisors in charge are responsible for ensuring that the employees are aware of the hazards / risks\ninvolved in the work they will be doing / are doing and shall ensure the safety rules are obeyed.\nNo person shall act in a manner that endangers or is likely to endanger, the safety of any other person,\nor cause harm to any other person.\nAn employee who observes any dangerous situation, shall as soon as possible inform the person who\nis responsible for that section of the site.\nAny employee who becomes aware of any person disregarding any safety rules, shall remind that\nperson of the rules. If he persists in disregarding the rules, the matter must be reported to his supervisor.\nC3-145\n\nNo person shall damage, alter, remove, render ineffective or interfere with anything that has been\nprovided for the protection of the site, or for the health and safety of persons.\nNo person shall interfere with or use firefighting equipment without authority and training.\nNo person in a state of intoxication or condition that render him incapable of controlling himself shall\nenter or be allowed to enter the site.\nNo alcohol or illegal drugs shall be taken onto the site.\nAll safety and warning signs shall be obeyed.\nAlways be alert of construction vehicles as well as traffic. Never turn your back to oncoming traffic,\nalways have a line of sight.\n16.11 Contractor and Sub-Contractor Management\nThe Principal Contractor shall establish, maintain and ensure that all his contractors establish and\nmaintain HSE standards and systems as necessary and to comply with the Legal requirements as well\nas these HSE specifications.\nThe Contractor shall be solely responsible for carrying out work on the project, having the highest regard\nfor the health and safety of his employees and people in the vicinity of his work area.\n16.12 Public Health and Safety\nThe contractor shall, as far as is reasonably practicable, be responsible for ensuring that non-employees\naffected by the construction work are made aware of the dangers likely to arise from said construction\nwork as well as the precautionary measures to be observed to avoid or minimise those dangers.\nThis includes:\n• Non- employees entering the site for whatever reason\n• The surrounding community\n• Passers-by to the site\n1. DESIGNING FOR HEALTH, SAFETY AND THE ENVIRONMENT\nDesigning for safety is a process aimed at minimizing injury, death, property damage or destruction and harm\nto the environment, by utilizing an approach to identify and eliminate or control hazardous areas. The project\nDesigner and Contractor must implement a process that ensures safety is incorporated in the design process,\nwhich includes temporary works as contemplated in the Construction Regulations, 2014 Section 12.\nThe Contractor must communicate the anticipated risks and hazards resulting from the design to his\nemployees and establish safe work procedures for the temporary works.\n1. INCIDENT MANAGEMENT\nThe Contractor shall ensure that a culture exists within his company that promotes the recognition, response,\nreporting and investigation of incidents, including near misses (near hits). The Contractor must implement a\nprocedure for reporting and investigating accidents, incidents and near misses. The Contractor should have a\nclear objective and target to obtain zero injuries for the duration of the project and such an objective must be\ncommunicated to all employees.\nAppropriate corrective actions must be implemented and the applicable learnings must be shared within the\nContractors business to prevent a recurrence of the incident or to prevent the near miss from becoming an\nincident in future.\n18.1 Incidents and Accidents\nThe contractor and his subcontractors shall coordinate their investigation of all accidents/incidents\nwhere employees and non-employees were injured to the extent that he had to be referred for medical\ntreatment by a doctor, hospital or clinic. The results of the investigation shall be entered into an\naccident/incident register, which must be updated with each accident/indent.\nC3-146\n\nThe Contractor shall notify the relevant SANRAL Project Manager and or SANRAL OHS Specialist of\nany incident/accident within the Contractors or his Sub-Contractors area of responsibility in writing as\nsoon as possible.\nAlthough the accident / incident is reported to the client, the Contractor has a responsibility and is\nrequired by law to report any Section 24 accidents and incidents to the Department of Labour. Any road\ntraffic accident must be reported to the relevant authorities.\nIt is essential that the Contractor demonstrate that corrective and preventative action has been taken to\nprevent a similar incident in future and that it is communicated to all the Contractors affected staff. A\ncopy of the investigation, corrective and preventative action taken as well as the attendance register of\nthe employees who attended the discussion of the incident and the action implemented to prevent a\nsimilar incident, must be forwarded to the SANRAL Project Manager and or the SANRAL OHS\nSpecialist.\nInvestigations must be completed for:\n- Near Miss Incidents (To prevent it from becoming an incident)\n- First Aid case Incidents\n- Medical treatment case Incidents\n- Fatalities\n18.2 Incident Reporting\nThe contractor shall provide the Employer with copies of all statutory reports required in terms of the Act\nwithin 7 days of the incident occurring. In addition, the contractor shall update monthly the Disabling\nInjury Frequency Ratio (DIFR) and display this information on a signboard at the site office.\nThe Contractor is responsible for collecting, recording, calculating and reporting his and his sub-\ncontractors Health & Safety statistics to the SANRAL OHS Specialist.\nThe statistics should contain at least the following for all employees of all contractors working on the\nproject:\n- Total Number of workers\n- Total Number of hours worked (on the SANRAL project)\n- Total Number of Near Miss Incidents\n- Total Number of First Aid case Incidents\n- Total Number of Medical Treatment case Incidents (Excluding Section 24 type incidents)\n- Total Number of Section 24 type Incidents\n- Preventative actions taken on incidents that have occurred\n- Communication to employees and contractors of incidents and preventative actions.\n1. PROJECT SPECIFIC CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS\nSection 1 to 18 of this document is generic and is applicable to all projects carried out on behalf of the South\nAfrican National Roads Agency, SOC Ltd.\nThe following section contains specific requirements for Contract No: SANRAL C.002-56-2024/1 which must\nbe adhered to in addition to minimum legislative requirements.\n19.1 Baseline Risk Assessment\nThe following is a list of risks identified which forms the Baseline Risk Assessment for the project\nprepared by the Client in terms of Construction Regulation 5(1)(a):\nRisks in connection with: Project Manager and/or OHS Agent to update risks to make it project specific\n• Personal health risks in connection with ablution facilities, eating areas, drinking water.\n• Secure/safe storage of materials, plant and equipment\n• Secure/safe storage and use of hazardous and/or flammable materials\n• Maintenance workshop - onsite repairs to construction vehicles, mobile plant & equipment.\n• Possibility of asbestos in existing structures\n• Existing services, e.g. gas, telecommunications, electrical supply and similar\n• Temporary electrical installations\n• Adjacent land uses/surrounding property exposures\nC3-147\n\n• Boundary and access control/public liability exposures (NB: The Employer is also responsible for\nthe OH&S of non-employees affected by his/her work activities)\n• Biological hazards, e.g. bees, snakes, spiders\n• Environmental risks, e.g. lighting, strong winds, heavy rains, dark environments, hot/cold and wet\nenvironments\n• Exposure to a water environment\n• Exposure to noise\n• Exposure to vibration\n• HIV/Aids and other diseases such as silicosis or asbestosis, where applicable\n• Hazardous Biological Agents which could lead to epidemics and pandemics\n• Use of portable electrical equipment including, but not limited to:\n- Angle grinder\n- Electrical drilling machine\n- Circular saw\n- Generator\n• Excavations including, but not limited to:\n- Ground/soil conditions\n- Trenching\n- Shoring\n- Drainage of trenches\n• Welding including, but not limited to:\n- Arc welding\n- Gas welding\n- Flame cutting\n- Use of LP gas torches and appliances\n• Loading and off-loading of trucks, including material deliveries\n• Manual and mechanical handling\n• Lifting and lowering operations\n• Driving and operation of construction vehicles and mobile plant including:\n- Trenching machine\n- Excavator\n- Sit on roller\n- Plate compactor\n- Front end loader\n- Mobile cranes and the ancillary lifting tackle\n- Grader\n- Parking of vehicles and mobile plant\n- Towing of vehicles and mobile plant\n• Layering and bedding\n• Installation of pipes in trenches\n• Pressure testing of pipelines\n• Backfilling of trenches\n• Protection against flooding\n• Gabion work\n• Use of explosives\n• Overhead Electrical Cables\n• Work adjacent or in proximity of railway lines\n• Work adjacent or in proximity of traffic\n• Working in elevated positions\n• Working in confined spaces – tunnelling\n• Formwork and support work (temporary works) including scaffolding\n• Demolition work, where applicable\n• Bulk mixing plant, where applicable\n• Environmental impacts such as pollution of water, air or soil\n19.2 Daily Site Attendance Register\nThe Contractor shall keep a daily site register so as to be able to identify the entire Contractors personnel\non site in case of an emergency or evacuation situation. The attendance register must include\npermanent as well as temporary workers working on the site.\nAll site visitors and any new contractors shall report to security / reception upon arrival at site. The\nContractor will only be granted first time access to work on the site if all required documentation has\nbeen provided and approved.\nC3-148\n\nAll visitors need to sign an attendance register when visiting the site. Visitors include all persons which\nare not permanently working on the site but excludes temporary site workers. Visitors must undergo site\ninduction training before they are allowed on site to make them aware of the site dangers.\n19.3 Emergency Numbers / Emergency Evacuation\nA list with emergency numbers must be readily available to first aiders and supervisors. Emergency\nnumbers must be site specific and must display the nearest emergency facilities.\nThe contractor shall identify and formulate emergency procedures in the event an incident does occur.\nThe emergency procedures thus identified shall also be included in the contractor’s OH&S plan and\ncommunicated as part of induction training. It is the responsibility of the first aid worker, together with\nthe construction supervisor, to make an assessment regarding the severity of injuries and which actions\nare appropriate. For example: transfer to a medical facility by ambulance or helicopter.\nThe Contractor must implement an emergency evacuation procedure on site to ensure that in case of\nan emergency, all staff will leave their place of work when the emergency siren is sound and proceed\nto the demarcated emergency assembly point. The emergency assembly point must display the sign\n“Emergency Assembly Point”.\nAn evacuation route diagram must be displayed and visible at strategic points in buildings and on notice\nboards.\nAll staff working on site must be given awareness training on the emergency evacuation procedure and\nevacuation drills must be exercised to ensure all staff know the correct procedure to follow in case of an\nemergency.\n19.4 Site Security\nCertain areas where work must be carried out, is recognized unsafe areas and certain other areas may\nfrom time to time become unsafe, due to 3rd party actions. The Contractor must as far as reasonably\npossible anticipate unsafe areas and must ensure that his site staff is safe from 3rd party actions, which\ninclude but is not limited to:\n- Unrests,\n- Violent Demonstrations,\n- Theft,\n- Injury from 3rd parties at all times.\nThe Contractor must, when work is to be carried out in the above-mentioned areas, make provision for\nsecurity services to accompany site staff during the execution of their work, as the Contractor is\nresponsible for the Health, Safety and Security of his own staff. The provision for security services must\nform part of the Contractors tender.\n19.5 Personal Protective Equipment\nComply with General Safety Regulations, Section 2\nThe contractor shall identify the hazards in the workplace and deal with them. He must either remove\nthem or, where impracticable, take steps to protect workers and make it possible for them to work safely\nand without risk to health under the hazardous conditions.\nPersonal protective equipment (PPE) should, however, be the last resort and there should always first\nbe an attempt to apply engineering and other solutions to mitigating hazardous situations before the\nissuing of PPE is considered. The hierarchy of hazard elimination must be followed before the option of\npersonal protective equipment is considered. The following hierarchy of controls must be followed:\n- Elimination\n- Passive Controls\no Substitution – Using a cherry picker or man-lift instead of a ladder.\no Engineering Controls – Installing barrier railings; Installing stairs instead of using vertical\nladders.\n- Active Controls\no Administrative policies and procedures\no Personal protective equipment\nC3-149\n\nWhere it is not possible to create an absolutely safe and healthy workplace the contractor shall inform\nemployees regarding this and issue, free of charge, suitable equipment to protect them from any hazards\nbeing present and that allows them to work safely and without risk to health in the hazardous\nenvironment.\nIt is a further requirement that the contractor maintain the said equipment, that he instructs and trains\nthe employees in the use of the equipment and ensures that the prescribed equipment is used by the\nemployee/s.\nEmployees do not have the right to refuse to use/wear the equipment prescribed by the Employer and,\nif it is impossible for an employee to use or wear prescribed protective equipment through health or any\nother reason, the employee cannot be allowed to continue working under the hazardous condition/s for\nwhich the equipment was prescribed but an alternative solution has to be found that may include\nrelocating the employee.\nThe contractor shall include in his OH&S plan the PPE he intends issuing to his employees for use\nduring construction and the sanctions he intends to apply in cases of non-conformance by his\nemployees. Conformance to the wearing of PPE shall be discussed at the weekly inspection meetings.\nThe contractor shall ensure that all his personnel, excluding those who are permanently office bound,\nare equipped with reflective safety jackets and that these are worn at all times when working on site.\nAny person found not wearing a reflective jacket on site must be removed from the site until such time\nas he is in possession of and wearing a reflective jacket. Reflective safety jackets shall be kept in good\ncondition and any jackets that are ineffective must immediately be replaced by the contractor.\n19.6 Site Supervision\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 8\nThe Contractor shall appoint a competent Construction Manager who shall be responsible for the\nconstruction activities and for ensuring occupational health and safety compliance on the construction\nsite.\n19.7 Working in Elevated Positions\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 10\nThe contractor shall ensure that a fall protection plan, developed by a competent person who is\ndesignated as the Fall Protection Plan Developer, is available on site and understood by all employees\nwho will be working in elevated positions.\nAll employees working in elevated positions shall protected themselves from falls by wearing a full body\nharness and the lanyard shall be attached as far as possible above the head of the worker to a life line\nor other approved and tested anchor point.\nIn addition to obvious elevated work activities, work activities which include:\n- Working on the edge of an excavation where there is a risk of falling into the excavation;\n- Work on the edge of a vertical drop where there is a risk of falling;\n- Work on top of tanker trucks and tanks;\nShall be considered work in elevated positions and Section 10 of the Construction Regulations must be\nadhered to at all times. The hierarchy of controls must be implemented when such activities are carried\nout. As a minimum the employee must wear PPE, which shall include a full body harness attached to a\nrestraint.\n19.8 Structures\nComply with Construction Regulations, Section 11.\nThe contractor shall ensure that all practicable measures are taken to prevent the uncontrolled collapse\nof new or existing structures or any part thereof, which may become unstable or is in a temporary state\nof weakness or instability due to the carrying out of construction work. No structure may be loaded in a\nmanner which would render it unsafe.\nWhen a structure is of temporary nature, all conditions as required by the Construction Regulations\nSection 12 - Temporary Works, must also be complied with.\nC3-150\n\n19.9 Excavations\nComply with Construction Regulations, Section 13\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all excavations are carried out under the supervision of a competent\nperson who has been appointed in writing as Excavation Supervisor.\nThe Contractor must evaluate the stability of the ground before excavation work begins as well as during\nexcavation work.\nExcavations must be barricaded to prevent unauthorized access.\nMaterial removed from excavations, as well as heavy machinery and construction vehicles, must not be\ncloser than 1 meter of the edge of the excavation, to prevent additional loads on the excavation edge,\nwhich could cause cave-ins, to prevent construction vehicles from falling into the excavation and to\nprevent the accumulation of carbon monoxide gas inside the excavation.\nPeople working in the excavation must be adequately protected from cave-ins, by means of protection\nsystems such as trench boxed and shielding and must have a safe means of access into the excavation\nand egress from the excavation.\n19.10 Scaffolding\nComply with Construction Regulations, Section 16, General Safety Regulations, Section 6, and SANS\n10085 – The Design, erection, use and inspection of access scaffolding\nThe Contractor shall appoint a competent person in writing as scaffolding Supervisor. Scaffolding\nInspectors and Scaffolding Erectors must be trained and found competent to carry out scaffolding work.\nIt is important to note that only competent scaffold erectors are allowed to build the scaffolding. The\nscaffold inspector is not allowed to build the scaffold with the scaffold erector team.\nScaffolding shall be erected according to SANS 10085 and shall be tagged “safe for use” after inspection\nindicated that the scaffold is safe to use. The inspection of the scaffold shall be in writing and proof\nthereof shall be available for any user of the scaffold as well as for audit purposes.\nScaffold left erected while the Contractor is not in attendance, must be tagged with a “Not Safe for Use”\ntag and all reasonably practicable measures must be taken to prevent unauthorised access to the\nscaffold.\nScaffold must be inspected by the competent scaffold inspector on completion of the scaffold build,\nweekly thereafter or following severe weather conditions.\nHazards such as overhead power lines must be identified before the scaffold is build and must be\nreflected in the risk assessment.\nWhen using mobile scaffold, employees and materials must be removed from scaffold before moving\nthe mobile scaffold. Hazards such as overhead power lines must be identified before moving mobile\nscaffold and must reflect in the risk assessment.\n19.11 Suspended Platforms\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 17, SANS 10295-2 - Suspended access equipment\nPart 2: Temporary suspended platforms (TSPs)\nAll suspended platform work must be carried out under the supervision of a competent appointed\nSuspended Platform Supervisor. Suspended platform erectors, operators and inspectors must be\ncompetent.\nThe contractor must be in possession of a certificate of design for the use of the suspended platform\nsystem.\n19.12 Cranes\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 22, General Machinery Regulation, Section 18.\nC3-151\n\nCrane operators must be competent to carry out their work safely and must be in possession of a valid\nmedical certificate of fitness, issued by an occupational health practitioner.\n19.13 Construction Vehicles & Mobile Plant\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 23, National Road Traffic Act, 1996\nConstruction vehicle operators must have received training to operate the class of construction vehicle\nor mobile plant and must be in possession of an operator’s card as proof of competency. Construction\nvehicle operators must be authorised in writing and have a medical certificate of fitness issued by an\noccupational health practitioner to operate the construction vehicle and/or mobile plant.\n19.14 Electrical Equipment\nComply with Construction Regulations, Section 24\nThe contractor shall take adequate steps to ascertain the presence of and guard against danger to\nworkers from electrical cables or apparatus which is under, over or on the site.\nThe exact location of underground electric power cables must be determined before any excavators are\nused for excavation purposes.\nThe location of overhead electrical cables must be assessed when working with cranes and lifting\nequipment. Injury may be possible from touching the electrical cables with the crane boom, or from\narching when the crane boom comes too close to the electrical cable.\nAll temporary electrical installations must be inspected at least once a week by a competent person and\nthe records of the inspections must be recorded in a register which must be kept on site.\nElectrical machinery and extension cords must be is a serviceable condition and must be inspected on\na daily basis before use on a construction site by the authorised operator and the inspection checklist\nmust be kept on the construction site.\nElectrical Installations\nComply with Electrical Installation Regulations\nAll electrical installations shall be inspected and approved by an accredited electrical inspector and a\nvalid Certificate of Compliance must be issued for the installation.\nAll electrical installations carried out on site (permanent and temporary) must be in accordance and\ncomply with the Electrical Installation Regulations.\nAll power supplies and generating units must be fitted with a functional earth leakage device.\n19.15 Temporary Storage of Flammable Liquids\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 25 and General Safety Regulations, Section 4\nThe Contractor must ensure storage areas of flammable liquids are well ventilated and “No Smoking”\nsigns are placed at the entrances and ventilation ducts of the storage areas. Firefighting equipment must\nbe available in suitable positions around the storage areas.\nThe Contractor must ensure that good housekeeping is practiced in and around the flammable storage\nareas.\n19.16 Water Environments\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 26\nThe Contractor must ensure that a lifejacket forms part of the employees PPE and is worn when the\nemployee is exposed to the risk of drowning, by falling into water.\nC3-152\n\nThe risk assessment must make provision for the rescuing of persons in danger of drowning and for\npreventing employees from falling into the water.\nWhen working over water environments, Section 10 of the Construction Regulations – Fall Protection\nwill also apply.\n19.17 House-keeping\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 27, Environmental Regulations for Workplaces, Section\n6(3)\nThe Contractor shall ensure that suitable and acceptable housekeeping is continuously implemented\nand maintained on the construction site. Off-cuts and waste must be removed by the end of the shift or\nas soon as practicable.\n19.18 Stacking & Storage of Material & Equipment\nComply with Construction Regulations, Section 28 and General Safety Regulations, Section 8\nThe Contractor shall appoint a competent person in writing with the duty of supervising all stacking and\nstorage operations on site.\nStacking shall only take place in areas specifically demarcated for this purpose. Circular items must be\nsecured with wedges or chocks.\nItems removed from a stack shall only take place from the top most layer of the stack.\nStacks shall not obstruct any fire extinguishing equipment, first aid equipment, electrical switchgear (DB\nBoxes) and ventilation or lighting installations.\nUnstable stacks must be broken down immediately.\n19.19 Fire Precautions\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 29.\nThe Contractor must provide his own firefighting equipment that is within the service date and safe for\nuse. Firefighting equipment must be on a register and inspected by a competent person who has been\nappointed in writing.\nSuitable and sufficient fire extinguishing equipment must be placed at strategic locations and a sufficient\nnumber of firefighters must be available, which must be trained in the use of it.\n19.20 Intoxicating Liquor and Drugs\nComply with General Safety Regulations, Section 2A.\nThe site limit for intoxication is set to zero to complement a vision of zero tolerance.\nAny person found to be intoxicated, or consuming intoxicating liquor or illegal drugs, will not be allowed\nonto the premises and/or will be removed from the premises.\nThe Contractor has the right to test any person entering the premises for intoxicating liquor or illegal\ndrugs and may refuse entrance on the basis of the outcome of the test.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that employees taking prescription medicine informs the Contractor of such\nand shall ensure that the side effect of such medicine does not constitute a hazard to the employee\nhimself or people working in close vicinity to the employee.\n19.21 Confined Space Work & Tunnelling\nComply with Construction Regulation, Section 15 and General Safety Regulations, Section 5\nThe Contractor shall ensure that only authorized persons enter confined spaces.\nC3-153\n\nAn entrance log must be kept to ensure people are not left inside the confined space. Adequate air\nmonitoring must be carried out before entering the confined space. When air monitoring indicated the\noxygen to be less than 20% by volume, the confined space must be purged and ventilated to obtain a\nsafe atmosphere or self-contained breathing apparatus must be used.\n19.22 Site Services\nThe Contractor shall provide and maintain on the Site adequate and suitable sanitary services and a\nsupply of potable water for the Contractor’s, the Employer’s and the Engineer’s personnel engaged on\nthe Contract and, if necessary, similar facilities elsewhere for such personnel off the Site\nDrinking Water\nThe Contractor must ensure that an adequate supply of potable drinking water is available for the\nContractor’s, the Employer’s and the Engineer’s personnel engaged on the Contract and, if necessary,\nsimilar facilities elsewhere for such personnel off the Site. Employees working in hot conditions must\nconsume enough water per hour to prevent dehydration.\nWhere water is unsafe for human consumption, it must be so indicated by means of adequate signage.\nAccommodation\nThe Contractor shall comply with the requirements of Construction Regulation 30 with regards to\nemployee’s facilities and accommodation. Reasonable and suitable living accommodation must be\nprovided to employees who are far removed from their homes.\n---\nI ______________________________________________, duly authorised to sign this Health and Safety\nSpecification on behalf of _________________________________________(the Contractor), do hereby\ndeclare that I acknowledge having read and understand this Health and Safety Specifications.\nSigned at_______________________________on this_______day of_____________________ 20_____.\n---\nContractor Representative Name Signature Date\nC3-154\n\nD3: OHS Audit F: Proforma report formats\nThe content and format of the report shall be in accordance with the Employer’s standard requirements and\nshall include, but not necessarily be limited to, the following, as may be relevant:\na) Locality Plan\nb) Executive Summary\nc) Introduction\nTerms of reference\nGeneral description of the Project\nObjectives and strategies\nScope of the investigations\nd) Road Cross-Section\nStandard Cross-Section\n- National Road\n- Provincial Road\n- Municipal Road\nBatter Slopes\ne) Existing pavement\nPavement history\nMaintenance history\nf) Existing pavement evaluation\nVisual condition survey\nInstrument/mechanical surveys\nTest pit information (Refer to SANRAL’s Draft M1 (2004) Manual for Design Specialist’s\n(Pavement and Materials Engineer) professional responsibilities and modus operandi)\nCore profiles (Refer to SANRAL’s Draft M1 (2004) Manual for Design Specialist’s (Pavement\nand Materials Engineer) professional responsibilities and modus operandi)\nDCP analysis\ng) Traffic information\nAvailable data\nPast traffic loading\nFuture traffic growth\nRecommended design loading\nTraffic accommodation considerations\nh) Structural analysis\nAnalysis of deflections\nAssessment of deflection bowl parameters\nOverlay structural analysis\ni) Geometric Design\n- National Road\n- Provincial Road\n- Municipal Road\nj) Structures (refer to specific requirements of Bridge Report in Structures in Code of Procedure)\nk) Hydraulic Capacity of Bridges and Major Culverts (refer to SANRAL’s Drainage Manual 6th Edition)\n(latest version)\nl) Traffic Accommodation\nAll traffic deviations etc to accommodate existing traffic during the construction to be included in\nthis chapter\nC3-157\n\nm) Ancillary works\nAll ancillary works over and above the main focus of the project should be included in this chapter\ne.g. sub-soil drainage, surface drainage, shoulder make up, road signs etc.\nn) Environmental and OHS obligations and considerations\no) Summary of recommendations, including but not restricted to;\nGeometric improvements\nCapacity improvements\nStructural options (Refer to Bridge Report in Structures C.O.P)\nIntersection improvements\nInterchange improvements\nSafety improvements\nStructures\nPavement design options\nAny other alternatives\nCost benefit analysis\nClimatic influences\np) Construction materials (project/commercial and their appropriate application with respect to OH&S\nobligations) (Refer to SANRAL’s Draft M1 (2004) Manual\nAggregate sources\nSand sources\nGravel sources\nFill material sources\nWater sources\nBituminous products\nq) Estimated costs of alternative strategies\nConstruction programme\nr) Annexures\nThe Report shall also contain the following annexures where relevant:\nPavement analysis:\nSummary of condition survey\nPavement evaluation sheets\nRiding quality measurements\nDeflection measurements\nDeflection vs. rut depth analysis\nFWD parameter analysis\nMaterials investigations: (Refer to SANRAL’s Draft M1 (2004) Manual for Design Specialist’s\n(Pavement) professional responsibilities and modus operandi)\nTest pit profiles\nSummary of test results\nAnalysis of cores\nExisting binder property results\nTexture depth and Ball Pen survey\nConstruction materials:\nTest results summary\nMaterials designs\ns) Draft pricing schedule and cost estimate.\nt) Draft specifications differing from the COLTO Standard or Employer’s proforma document.\nC3-158\n\nTable 1: Economic Indicators Summary (Capital Projects e\nd\ni\nv\no\nr\np\nC3-160\n\nAppendix H: Memorandums of Agreement\nH1 : Declaration of National Road through “Towns”\nH2 : Hand-over of Access Roads Agreement between SANRAL\nand relevant Local e\nd\ni\nv\no\nr\np\nC3-162\n\nAppendix J: 2nd Tier Procurement Procedure\nProcurement of Sub-services\nSecond (2nd) tier procurement include the procurement of any work where either the work is not scheduled or\npriced, or where the process of procurement of the sub-Service Provider is by means of a prescribed 2nd tier\nproforma document provided by the Employer. It includes the procurement of work where rates have been\nomitted of where allowance for work is made under provisional sum or prime cost sum.\nThe following procurement methods is to be followed:\na) Where work is not scheduled but existing rates for similar work exist in the contract and the work can\ntherefore be executed by the Service Provider or his Targeted Enterprise at existing rates.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is to be quantified and scheduled utilising\nexisting rates and approved through the Works Authorisation process.\nb) Where work is scheduled and the payment calculation is based on a formula specified in the contract\ndocument, or where the payment rate is pre-determined or fixed by the Employer.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is to be quantified and approved through the\nWorks Authorisation process.\nc) Where the supplier/sub-Service Provider is not selected by the Service Provider and actual cost is\nreimbursable and no procurement process is possible.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is invoiced by supplier/sub-Service Provider on\ncompletion and approved through the Works Authorisation process at the end of the contract.\nd) Where there are omitted items as part of the existing scheduled scope of work and no existing rates for\nsimilar work exist in the contract.\nA proposal for a new rate shall be submitted by the Service Provider and evaluated by the Employer, by\ncomparing with either adjusted relevant rates in the contract, or by comparing with similar rates on\nsimilar contract, or by comparing 3 (three) informal quotes to substantiate the rate. The new agreed rate\nis approved through the Works Authorisation process.\ne) Where work is scheduled under a provisional sum or prime cost sum and the Employer prescribes a 2nd\ntier proforma either by quotation process or open tender process.\nThe work is to be procured by means of the 2nd tier proforma and approved through the Works\nAuthorisation process.\nf) Where work is scheduled under a provisional sum or prime cost sum and there is no approved proforma\navailable from the Employer and the estimated cost of the work is equal or less than R1 million.\nA minimum of 3 (three) quotations shall be obtained from sub-Service Providers and approved through\nthe Works Authorisation process.\nThe following is the minimum requirements for this process:\n• Quotation to include form of quotation, CSD registration, tax compliance certificate/PIN number\nand a B-BBEE certificate/Sworn Affidavit.\n• Quotations to be evaluated on price and preference.\ng) Where work is scheduled under a provisional sum or prime cost sum and there is no approved proforma\navailable from the Employer and the estimated cost of the work is more than R1 million.\nThe work is to be procured through an open tender and approved through the Works Authorisation\nprocess. The following is the minimum requirements for this process:\n• Tenders to close at the office of the Employer.\n• Tender documents to include Form of Offer, CSD registration, tax compliance certificate/PIN\nnumber, a B-BBEE certificate/Sworn Affidavit, SBD1, SBD4, SBD6.2 and Form A2.2.\n• Tenders to be evaluated on price and preference.\nC3-163\n\nh) The following list of items is exempted from the provisions of paragraphs (f) and (g) and an indication of\nthe method or minimum requirements are given under “Procurement Requirements”.\nProcurement\nItem no Description REGISTRATION\nTo register a new ITIS user, go to the ITIS website https://itis.nra.co.za\ni. Click on Register and accept the Term and Conditions and click on Next\nii. Select Public User from the dropdown on the registration category screen. Click on Next\nFigure 1: Registration Category\niii. Complete the User details form:\nFigure 2: User Details\nC3-169\n\n• E-Mail Address - This email address will be used as your user name to access this website and\nthe ITIS software. Each user must have their own email address and users will not be allowed\nto share an email address\n• Password - This password will be required when trying to access the website and the ITIS\nsoftware. The password must contain at least 1 UPPERCASE letter, 1 lowercase letter, 1\nspecial character and 1 number\n• Security Question - Select a security question from the dropdown. This question will be used\nfor confirmation when resetting your password\n• Security Answer - Enter the answer to the question selected above\n• Name - Your name\n• Surname - Your surname\n• Telephone Number - Your telephone number at the office\n• Mobile Number - Your mobile number\n• Fax Number - Your Fax number\niv. Click on Next\nv. Enter the characters as seen on the Captcha Image and click on Register\nvi. Clicking on Register will send a verification email to the email address you specified. Open the email\nand click on the email verification link.\nvii. After a successful email verification, you should be able to Login\nFigure 3: Verification Email\nC3-170\n\nAppendix L: Gateway Review Template\nC3-171\n\nAppendix M: SARDS Manual\ne\nl\ni\nf\no\nt\nr\ne\nf\ne d\nR e\nd\ni\nv\no\nr\np\nC3-172\n\nAppendix N: Structures Tracking Spreadsheet\ne\nl\ni\nf\no\nt\nr\ne\nf\ne d\nR e\nd\ni\nv\no\nr\np\nC3-173\n\nPART C5: ANNEXURES\nC3-174\n
